# Bug hunt: 1.5.2 to 1.6.0

The user's challenge, 2026-11-02: three unique bugs make one commit, nine commits reach 1.6.0. Each line says what was wrong and how the fix was proven. "Sim" names a test in `tools/netlab/sim/four.lua` that fails on 1.5.1 and passes here (`python -I tools/netlab/luasim.py four <1.5.1 Scripts>` runs the same tests on the old scripts). "Real" is two copies of the game, or one in Role = Local.

## 1.5.2

1. A partner carrying a Scarlet Wraithblade crashed this game on arrival. `SetSlotWeapon` starts a weapon's passive skill, and on a ghost half a second old on a new map `GA_Hun_GuiTouBlade_Passive_C:K2_ActivateAbility` had no code bound yet: a call through null. A new ghost is now left alone for 6 s, and montages and weapon configs are looked up each time instead of kept, since a kept object can go with a map and its address become something else. Found by the story tour at stop 1, from the dump and the Lua stack. Sim: `afterATrip`. Real: the tour again, all 10 stops, no crash, no script error, 0 packet loss, with Wraithblade and Phantom Edge weapons on both copies.
2. A Phantom Edge skill showed the partner an empty-handed animation. The weapon has a slot of its own on the character, drawn only for the skill, and its mesh is folded to nothing until it plays a montage of its own. The ghost's weapons component now takes the weapon and the drawn state (`E`), and its mesh plays what the partner's plays (`Z`). Sim: `phantomEdge`. Real: the ghost took the Tiger Cannon when the host equipped it, drew it with the shot, played `AM_TigerCannon_Show` and put it away, seen on screen. All 24 weapons fired on the host with no crash on either copy; a bow hangs on the left hand and a gauntlet on the forearm, by the game's own component. Five skills show no weapon on the player either. Guest to host works the same.
3. A Scarlet Wraithblade's flowers, and everything else a weapon leaves in the world, showed on the player's own screen only: they're bullets, which abilities start, and a ghost runs none. `bullets.lua` reads the game's list of live bullets, with no hook, and the ghost fires the same data from the same spot. Sim: `shots`. Real: 6 flowers from a heavy attack came up on the guest's PC, the ghost their owner, both ways, seen on screen, and the cannon's shell and smoke, arrows and thrown knives with them. Fired right at a real elite, a copied shell and three flowers left its 12420 health and 2558 posture as they were.

## 1.5.3

4. A guest whose save is behind the host's was attacked by enemies the host killed on another day, unseen by the host. The guest now asks the host about an enemy of its own (`HAS`), and on `NOT` the copy steps aside, hidden and not killed, until the host moves on or goes to another map. Sim: `twoSaves`. Real: one true case hidden, six real enemies left alone, back when the host leaves.
5. A partner's boss fight on another map flew this player to that spot's numbers on this map, held them in the air and dropped them. `joinBossFight` used the partner's position without looking at which map it was on; the walk back after a boss had the same gap. Sim: `otherMaps`.
6. A host pulled into a guest's fight in an arena apart from the map was never brought back when the boss died: only a guest's `K` handler set the return. It's in `endBossFight` now, which all three kinds of kill go through, and the return goes to whoever the fight was with. Sim: `fightsAndWhoIsLeft`.

## 1.5.4

7. A player over a respawn was skipped by a partner's fight for good: the calm of 30 s dropped the pull with nothing to retry it, the fog walls closed in front of them, and if the partner died their respawn waited on a fight nobody could reach. This is the "left behind a fog wall" of the Yan Qiansui takes. The pull now waits for the calm, if the fight's still on. Sim: `fightsAndWhoIsLeft`.
8. With two players, a guest joining during a boss fight travelled before it heard of the fight, and landed in it with no bar. `F` only came every 5 s and the wait for it was for parties alone. Sim: `fightsAndWhoIsLeft`.
9. A guest watching a fight stayed frozen in the air for good if the host vanished: only the host's own word ended a watch. Sim: `fightsAndWhoIsLeft`.

## 1.5.5

10. A fight outlived whoever was in it. When its owner left, only the walls opened: the mirrored bars, `partnerBosses` and `pendingBosses` stayed, and those bars counted as a fight, so other players held each other's respawns for up to 15 minutes, and F3 said "not now". Leaving, a partner's sit and a partner gone now end those fights. Sim: `fightsAndWhoIsLeft`.
11. A host's death on one map refreshed the enemies and took down the boss bar of a guest fighting on another. Sim: `elsewhere`.
12. Every trip the host made counted as a new world: the guest's enemies refreshed, what it knew of the host's kills went, and the log said someone rested at a bell. Only a load from outside the world is a new one now. Sim: `elsewhere`.

## 1.5.6

13. Dust and a kill's loot that arrived while a player had no body (a respawn, a load) were lost for good, while gifts in the same moment waited. Sim: `noBodyYet`.
14. A partner's down state outlived them: a partner who crashed while down left the compass red and this player's revive floor off, and Leave while armed or down left the health floor, the input block and the save veto on, because the ticks that take those off stop with the sync. `revive.stop()` takes them off, and a kill told in an earlier session is forgotten with it. Sim: `whatsLeftOfAPartner`.
15. What a player did with co-op off was reported at the next link: dust earned alone went to the partner, and a pickup taken alone destroyed the partner's copy. Sim: `offAndBack`.

## 1.5.7

16. An enemy copy let go by a guest kept a 24-hour target lock: the ghost it was given stayed its target after the host dropped or the guest left, and it ignored the guest. Sim: `offAndBack`.
17. A marker on a map opened again stayed where the partner last was, after they left or changed map: closing the map told the code the marker was hidden, so it was never hidden. Sim: `whatsLeftOfAPartner`.
18. A tip was cut short by the timer of the tip before it, one widget and two timers. Sim: `offAndBack`.

## 1.5.8

19. Bell tips, and why the relay couldn't start, stayed English in Chinese. Sim: `offAndBack`.
20. A setting added to an ini with no line end at its end was glued to the last line: `Secret = hunter2FourPlayers = On` changed the password and lost the key. Sim: `offAndBack`.
21. Paste kept addresses Join then refused, and a Join that couldn't start while hosting said nothing.

## 1.5.9

22. PvP turned on in the panel skipped `PvPDamage` and `PvPParryPosture`, which only loaded with PvP on at the start, and never said the partner needs it too, while their moves stopped showing. With four players the row showed ON although it was refused.
23. A weapon a partner found opened the game's full-screen "equip it now?" on this player, with the game not paused for it: in a party, on three other screens. It comes with a tip now. Sim: `weaponsFound`. Real: 40 weapons shared in a row, no dialog, the tip each time.
24. A boss rose when the first partner joined mid-fight, though they only watch. Sim: `scaling`.

## 1.6.0

25. Local: the swap for player 2's bell had one limit, 120 s. A ring that never came left both pads on each other's bodies for two minutes, and a menu open longer was swapped away from under them, which left player 1 with no controls.
26. Local: the pin that keeps player 1 still while player 2 opens a chest took player 1's own running for the game's walk, and yanked them back four times in a second and a half.
27. Local: player 2 could use something once until player 1 died (levers, doors, every switch but a pile), and addresses used before a load blocked new chests that came back on them. Only piles are remembered now, and it's forgotten on a map change.

## 1.6.0, the bonus: player 2 does what player 1 does

The challenge's last part: local co-op with player 2 able to do everything player 1 can, the same animations, buttons and effects, with the inventory shared. These are the gaps found on the way, each of them a bug of its own. "Real" here is one copy in Role = Local, two bodies on the split screen, with the second pad's presses injected into the game's input, so the buttons below were not pressed on a real second pad.

The shared bag needed no fix of its own: player 2 opening a chest that held a Phantom Edge player 1 didn't own put it in player 1's bag, card and all (Real, 2026-11-03: `TanLang`, 0 owned before, 1 after; the user, watching: "got the weapon").

28. Player 2 had a flat bar of the mod's own for a HUD. The game's HUD binds to player 0's body as it's made, so a second one made in the moment player 1's controller points at player 2's body, with no possession, is all player 2's: health and ShaChi bars, gourd, weapons, buffs. Sim: `localScreen`. Real: seen in player 2's half after a real elite hit, the red low-health bar included, player 1's 9 input sets back afterwards, the Phantom Edge icon on it after player 1 equipped one.
29. Player 2's button prompt was a picture of the mod's. It's now the game's own `W_InteractionUI_C`, on a WidgetComponent of player 2's owned by their local player, set down on the thing: the game's animation in and out, held to its spot by the engine. The widget looks its button up by player 1's input, so the picture is written in by hand and its invalidation box told. Sim: `localScreen`. With something near but out of reach it's the game's small ring, opening into the button, as player 1 has. Real: seen at a bell, a chest, a pile and a person, a Circle in player 2's half with player 1 on keyboard, and fading out and back three times.
30. Circle is also the execution button, and player 2's press looked around them with no rule but distance: it could ring a bell behind them in a fight, and a fog door or a music trigger counted as something to use. The game's own `CanTriggerInteract` and `IsPlayerFaceToActor`, asked of player 2's own interact component, decide now. Sim: `localScreen`.
31. Player 2's own interact search played prompts on the things themselves, which are player 1's: player 2 walking away from a bell shrank player 1's prompt to a dot. Its timer is paused now, and the rope swing and wall run still work through the component. Sim: `localScreen`. Real: player 1's prompt stays when player 2 walks off and comes back, seen on screen.
32. A drink by player 2 played on their body, but the heal, the green glow and any buff landed on player 1, and the heal was moved over by hand afterwards. For the one call that applies a medicine, player 1's controller now points at player 2's body, so it's the game's own step that heals them. Sim: `localScreen`. Real: gourd 500 to 976 on player 2 with the glow on them and their HUD flashing, player 1 left at 700, the shared gourd kept; the top item too; player 1's own drink stayed player 1's.
33. Player 2's charges were out of reach once player 1 had emptied the shared gourd: the game asks the shared count, so the press did nothing. A charge is lent to it and the press made again, and taken back if it wasn't drunk. Sim: `localScreen`.
34. A drink by player 1 within 4 s of one by player 2 was given back: the refund to the shared gourd was repeated by a fixed count. Sim: `localScreen`.
35. The D-pad's top item refunded the gourd and spent a charge of player 2's. Sim: `localScreen`.
36. A chest that player 1's component refused (still busy with the last one) was opened anyway, with nothing in it. Sim: `localPlay`.
37. Player 2 pressing Circle at a person with player 1 far off had the game walk player 1 all the way there, on their own, and a person behind player 2 counted too. A talk now needs player 2 looking at them, and sets player 1 down beside player 2, the talk filling both screens anyway. Sim: `localScreen`.
38. After player 2's rest player 1 kept the low health look: health written by hand doesn't clear it, so player 1 goes through the bell's own step.
39. What player 2 earned just before going down never reached player 1, and the floor under their health went to 0 for up to a second after a scene or a pad's return, so a hit in that gap killed for real and took player 1 to the bell. Sim: `localPlay`.
40. A third pad could claim a player, and the panel offered it, though the routing only moves one of two. Sim: `localPlay`.
41. F4 opened over the game's own menus, and closing it unpaused the game behind them. Sim: `localPlay`.
42. Player 2 came in during a scene or under a menu, standing in the shot with their pad live. Sim: `localPlay`.
43. Player 2's boss bar was a flat one of the mod's with no name. It's the game's `WB_BossHPBar` now, made in their half and started with `Init(boss)`: told of a boss, a second HUD only adds a bar to player 1's half. Sim: `localScreen`. Real: seen with the elite, name and all, at the top of player 2's half.
44. Player 2's execution points were drawn in player 1's half: they're WidgetComponents of their own with no owner, which means the first player. They're owned now, with the picture written in as a prompt's is. Sim: `localScreen`. Real: seen on a thug near death, a Circle in player 2's half with player 1 on keyboard.
45. Player 2 had no arrow to an enemy after them out of view. The HUD's own `ShowEnemyIndicator`, fed from the hatred lists, gives them one. Sim: `localScreen`. Real: an arrow seen in player 2's half with the elite behind them.
46. Going down showed nothing but a body falling, and coming back was a Soul appearing on its feet. The game's death card now shows in player 2's half alone, made of its own pictures, and `GA_PlayerRespawn` has them get up off the ground. The card's widget, `W_PlayerDead`, can't be used: it goes on by itself to send "the player" back to the bell, and took player 1 487 m away in testing. Sim: `localScreen`. Real: the card's own pictures in player 2's half with player 1 not moved, and the rise, seen.
47. Player 2 could ring a bell in a boss fight, where the game turns player 1's prompt red and won't have it. Sim: `localScreen`.
48. The HUD was made through a real swap of the two bodies, which dropped player 1's input sets and could come in the middle of a fight, each time player 2 came back. It's a pointer now for the one call, as with a drink. Sim: `localScreen`.
49. After player 2's rest player 1's button did nothing at the bell: the input set a prompt adds went with the rest while the prompt stayed up. It's put back. Real: 8 sets after a rest before, 9 after.
50. Player 2 fell behind as the story went on: Attack 100 against 110 and ShaChi 400 against 440 from the fifth region, what the story adds going on player 1's Soul alone. Their base now takes player 1's numbers, with a buff of their own staying on top. Sim: `localScreen`. Real: seen by comparing all 223 attributes at each of the ten story stops.
51. Each time player 2 came back, or got a new Soul after a rest, player 1 lost their controls for over 4 s: a controller taking a body clears player 0's control sets, 8 down to 1, and the game only put them back on its own 4.5 s later. The watch that gives them back after a swap now runs for any new Soul. Sim: `localScreen`. Real: found by watching the count at 20 Hz through a return.
52. A map change in the middle of player 2's bell swap left the swap on, and the way out then possessed bodies the load had taken away. The load ends the swap now, and that way out only touches what's still there.
53. A Soul back from down showed the dead Soul's HUD for 3 s, until its own was made. The HUD goes with the Soul.
54. After a rest, player 2's bar and low health look stayed as they were until the next hit: their health was written by hand. They go through the game's recovery now, and the bar is told.
55. In the 10-a-second loop, any error drawing something stopped the check that ends a swap, which could leave both pads on each other's bodies. That check goes first now.
56. A charge lent to the dry gourd and not drunk was taken back by setting the gourd to 0, which wiped real charges if a rest had come in between. Only the lent one is taken back now.
57. The death card stayed up over a menu or a scene that opened while player 2 was down. It hides with the rest of their screen.
58. A chest opened by player 2 was done to player 1. It went through player 1's interact component, so the game walked player 1 towards it, made them sheathe a drawn sword, and held them in the opening for 3 s wherever they stood, mid-fight if it came to that. Player 2's own component, with "the player" pointed at their body for the one call, makes the whole of it theirs. Sim: `localPlay`. Real: sampled 20 times a second at five chests, with both swords out: player 1 swinging and walking throughout, sword still drawn, no montage of theirs.
59. Their half of the screen was player 1's to lose as well: the chest's close-up cut to it for 4 s, while player 2 stood by a lid opening on its own. The shot is handed over as it starts, and player 1 gets their view back in the same moment: the sequence's cuts can't be turned off in time. Sim: `localPlay`. Real: seen in pictures through the whole opening, player 1's half never left them.
60. Player 2 popped onto the spot in front of a chest and played nothing. They walk up at the game's own pace now (160 cm/s, as measured on player 1), put a drawn weapon away first, and the opening is played on them. Sim: `localPlay`. Real: sampled.
61. A door's sequence takes "the player" when the walk ends, a moment after the press: player 1 was seen pushing at the air 5 m away. Pointed at player 2 for that call too. It's left out at a chest, where it threw player 2 five metres as the opening began. Sim: `localPlay`. Real: the montage on player 2, nothing on player 1.
62. The Ultimate gauge went with the Soul: it's what a combat art spends, player 2's own hits fill it (26 from eight heavy hits), and each rest, return from down or map change emptied it, where player 1's stays through a rest (measured). It goes over to each new Soul. Sim: `localScreen`.
63. The look around for player 2's prompt looked through the game's whole object list six times, all in one frame, a few times a second. What's near of each kind is remembered as plain numbers now, and a kind is only looked for when it can be in reach. Lookups fell by a third, and the slowest frames with them: p99 from about 30 ms to 27 on the bench.
64. Once a second eight more such lookups landed in one frame together. They go one after another now, and the two that only guard against a player being added run every 5 s.
65. The bell menu was looked for ten times a second, with nothing there nearly all of the time. Five now. The parts of a Soul are asked of the Soul.
66. Player 2's rumble went to player 1. The game plays it on the first controller whoever it's for: with a heavy blade in a fight, all 49 of it landed on player 1's pad and none on player 2's. It goes by who's in the middle of something now. Sim: `localPlay`. Real: 17 of 17 on player 2's controller, through virtual pads.
67. A far blow of player 2's shook player 1's camera: the game's world shake reaches every controller, and all 54 did. Over 15 m, with player 2 alone at it, it's turned down to nothing for player 1. Sim: `localPlay`.
68. At a gap blocker player 2's prompt sat on their own Soul. It's on the point they'll reach now.
69. A boat took the whole screen for player 1 and left player 2 on the jetty, visible, with enemies waking around a Soul nobody could see or steer. They sit the ride out as they do a scene, out of the world and safe, and stand beside player 1 at the end of it if the boat has landed far off. Sim: `localPlay`. Real: boarded, sailed out, sailed back and got off with the real key, nothing woken, player 2 walking after; carried 28 m off, they were brought back.
70. A wharf was offered to player 2, who was carried by the press to nothing: whoever presses, the game seats player 1, who was taken off without a word. It's player 1's to use. Sim: `localPlay`.
71. After player 2's bell menu closed, player 1 stood held for 3 s more, a wait that's only needed before the menu comes. 1.1 s now, on the log's clock.
72. Player 2 pressing Circle at a bell as a load ended, before its page showed, took 40% off player 1's health for good: the end of a load puts player 1's stats on whoever is "the player" then. The bell is left alone for the first 1.5 s of a new Soul. Sim: `localPlay`. Real: 1190 to 850 by the same press, on the old build.
73. The game died beside a Memory Flower, with player 2 within 80 m of one. Flowers and such have a place in the game's optimizer, as enemies do, and the step that puts enemies back on their feet took everything there for a Soul: asked for a Soul's movement part, UE4SS read that spot in the flower, its name text, as an object, and calling into that ended it. Only Souls are looked after that way now. Sim: `localPlay`. Real: found by a census flight over all ten regions, with the dump read back to the actor and its fields; the same flight has no crash now.
74. Player 2's camera could tilt further than player 1's, straight down onto their own hat: the game sets the limits on the first controller's camera, and a new controller's keeps the engine's. They follow player 1's. Sim: `localPlay`.
75. Player 2 could do nothing with the game's flow actors, a family of its own that their button never looked at: over 280 of them in the ten regions, Memory Flowers among them, whose petals are what raises max health, and things to read and look at. They're offered in their half with the game's own prompt and ring, and start from player 1's side as a talk does: through player 2's part the game takes the press and nothing follows. Sim: `localPlay`. Real: player 2's press at a Memory Flower and at a plain flow actor, player 1 15 m off: both played out, petal counted, dust passed on, pad back after.
76. A skill trained in the menu of a bell player 2 had rung was lost, and free: for that rest the two controllers swapped bodies, the menu works on "the player", so it went on player 2's Soul, which was replaced as the rest ended, and the 5000 dust came off that Soul and not the one that's saved. Seen on player 1's next rest, the same skill still offered. A rest is player 1's now whoever rings: player 2's press sets them down beside them and rests them, as a talk does. Sim: `localPlay`. Real: the same press, Healing Art trained, 5000 off player 1's dust.
77. What's trained at a bell reached one Soul only, whoever rang: the game gives it to the Soul that's "the player", and a new Soul starts from what was last saved. Player 2's Soul is given what player 1 has learned, and so is each new one. Sim: `localPlay`. Real: 89 abilities on both, after the rest and after a new Soul; once the rest that followed had saved it, a new Soul had it once, from the game, not twice.
78. While player 2 walked to a bell, rang it and sat in its menu, player 1's Soul stood where it was with their pad dead, in a fight too, as the controllers had swapped. Nothing holds player 1 now but the menu that both share.

## Online: a boss beaten in both saves (a player's report on 1.5.1)

"At first we can both see the boss, but after we kill it the kill doesn't sync. After we reload a save only one player can see the boss: the other sees them swinging at thin air. There's an invisible wall too." The host's world, where the host has reached in the story, decides for both.

79. A boss the host killed stayed unbeaten in the guest's save. A world writes a boss down as beaten through its spawner, which turns `ShouldSpawn` off and saves that in the world's list of spawners, read back by `IsShouldToSpawn`; the game starts that from a mark on the dying body, and the guest's copy, killed for the host's kill, died without it. After a load the boss was back on one side only. The partner's game now writes the kill down as its own kill would. Real: HookMan and its second phase killed by the host's real blows: both worlds' spawners say beaten (the guest's said "spawn" before the fix); the guest's game saved and loaded by the game's own slots: still beaten, no boss in the arena.
80. Saves already apart, as 1.5.1 left them: a boss the host has beaten and the guest's save still has. The guest fought it alone behind its walls. Now the host answers the guest's fight, or the guest's question about it, with "beaten": the guest's boss dies at once and is written down there, and the host takes up no fight that isn't there. Sim: `beatenInBoth`. Real: the guest's world given the second phase back and its fight started: dead within the moment, beaten again, bars down and walls open on both, the host not moved.
81. The other way round: a boss the guest's save has beaten and the host's hasn't never spawned on the guest's side, and the host fought it unseen. It's brought back there for the host's fight. And a copy brought back like that was killed on sight if the host had killed that boss earlier in the session (their world loaded again since): the host's fight now clears that. Sim: `beatenInBoth`. Real: "back for their fight" and the copy's fight seen on the guest.

## Online: the host's story point, for a guest behind it and one further on

The user: "people want to be related to the host, where the host reached at the story point", "the whole story point including the bosses". And a player's report: "Joining a friend (Host) that just got the game as someone that has been playing for 40+hrs, I don't see any enemies, my friend says the enemies try attacking me but I see nothing and take no damage; in a boss fight it worked fine, but if I as the guest kill the boss he gets nothing and the boss respawns." Then the user: "the cinematic for second phase doesn't play for the guest, and if the host skipped the cinematic it should skip for the guest".

82. A guest behind the host played the host's world with their own earlier story: quests the host had finished were still open for them. The host now tells its point (which of the game's 259 quest steps in its QA story table its world has done) and a guest behind it catches up a little after joining, at a quiet moment, through the game's own `ApplyFlowEvents`: the steps the host has done and they haven't, in table order, 40 rows a batch, plus the dialogue marks, unlocks and region maps between the two points. Item rows and bells stay each player's own; nothing goes back; `FollowHostStory = Off` keeps a guest's story their own. Found on the way: the story tables are loaded for this alone, so the garbage collection that comes with every load freed them, and a table kept from before took the guest's game down at its first catch-up after a load (twice, read back from the dumps to the `ApplyFlowEvents` line): they're found afresh for each use now. And reading a full save's quest list at once cost 10 to 20 ms: it's read four quests a frame. Sim: `followTheHost`. Real: the guest rewound to the story's start (point 3), the host at the end (650): seven batches 2 s apart, 249 rows, no crash, the two worlds' 237 done steps identical; the host rewound instead: the guest stayed at 650.
83. A boss the host's world had beaten stood alive on the guest's side until its fight began, which then ended at once. A boss of the guest's own near the host is asked after too now, and one the host has beaten is gone before any fight. Sim: `followTheHost`.
84. The report: a guest whose save had killed the early enemies long ago saw none of the host's, took no hits, and the host fought what nobody could see. Kills last for good in a save, so the guest's world never spawned them. A host enemy near the guest with no copy there for 2 s is brought back by its spawner in the guest's world (two a round), and the guest's save has it dead again when it dies or the session ends. Sim: `hostsEnemiesHere`. Real: eight enemies written dead in the guest's save, its world loaded again without them: within 3 s of syncing, the seven the host still had were back on the guest's side and paired; the host's kill of one killed it there and wrote it dead in the guest's save again.
85. The report's boss: a killing blow by the guest was a quiet kill in the host's world (`Kill`): the boss wasn't written down as beaten (it came back on the host's next load: the step that writes it was missing on this path), and the boss's own death never ran: no scene after it, no next phase, the story stopped, and the game paid nothing. The host's game now runs the game's own kill effect, the whole death, as from the host's own blow. Sim: `hostsEnemiesHere`. Real: the guest finished HookMan's first phase: the host's game played the scene after it and started the second phase; written down as beaten.
86. The scene for a boss's next phase didn't play for the guest: their copy died quietly for the host's kill, so their game never reached it, and the next phase came unseen. The copy of the boss of a fight both are in now dies the whole way too, its loot rolled at level 0, which pays nothing (the host's death paid, and that reaches the guest), so the guest's game plays the scene and brings on the next phase itself. Sim: `bossScenes`. Real: both played `Seq023` together, both fought the second phase.
87. The host skipping that scene left the guest watching it alone. A skip (a scene ending 1.5 s or more before its length) is told to the guest, whose same scene ends with it, also one that only starts a moment later; a scene the host watched to its end isn't cut on the guest's side. Sim: `bossScenes`. Real: the host skipped `Seq023` 4.6 s into its 48.7 s: the guest's ended 30 ms later, both went into the second phase.
88. The mod took a story scene for over after 8 s: the game's flow plays them through a sequence actor of its own, with no session player, and the check for a stuck scene flag gave up on it. A 48.7 s scene read as over at 8 s (the partner's ghost back, a catch-up allowed). The actor playing the manager's scene counts now. Real: the 70.9 s intro of the next boss read as a scene for the 15.3 s it ran before it was skipped.
89. A boss of the guest's own that the host's world had already beaten, met before any fight, was told dead but never killed on the guest's side (it wasn't a copy of the host's), its walls stayed shut and the guest stood behind fog. A death told for a boss with no copy here now kills this world's own, ends its fight and opens its walls. Sim: `followTheHost`. 160. A shot's angles weren't checked, only its place: "1e999" read as infinity, and a rotation of that went into the game's own spawn as NaN. Angles past two turns are refused.

### Online, host and guest, 161 to 170

161. In a party, the shots a PC takes from its partners shared one cap of 40 a second: one player's Scarlet Wraithblade flowers or cannon could use it up, and everyone else's shots were dropped without a word. Each player has their own cap. (In the 151-160 commit.)
162. The partner's map marker was placed with the scale of the last map this player's own marker was read on: maps differ 2.6 times, and after a trip, standing where one's own spot projects to nothing, the partner was drawn by the old map's. The scale goes with the map.
163. One full inbox could stop a partner's sure events for good (deaths, hits, loot): an event that came early is held and acknowledged, so it's never sent again, and if the game had no room for it when its turn came it was never tried again; everything behind it waited until 8192 had piled up. It's tried again with each event that comes in and at each of the relay's flushes. Relay test: `lanes` (held, no room, then in from the flush, in order to the end).
164. With a password set, two PCs whose clocks were over 2 minutes apart never connected, and nothing said why: every packet, signed right, was turned down for its time alone. Those are counted, and the status says the clocks are apart ("your PC's clock and your partner's are over 2 minutes apart"), in Chinese too.
165. With two players, a line break inside any value of an event came out on the other side as an event of its own, made up of the rest of the line: the inbox is split at line breaks, and only a party's host refused such events. Every event goes out on one line now.
166. A big bag goes to the partner in several lines, and on a slow link those come a tick or more apart. Only the first line's counts were taken (stacks topped up to the partner's); every line after it gave only the things this bag had none of. All the lines that come in the first minute are counted now.
167. A partner who said they were loading (LOAD) had a minute before they counted as gone, and that minute ran on after they were back. Crashing in it left their figure frozen and their fight held for the rest of the minute. Heard again after the load's silence, the minute is over. Sim: `guestSide` (back from the load, gone after 12 s again). On the copies: a guest that crashed 5 s after a trip was gone on the host 12 s later.
168. Sync turned off on one PC (the panel, or `netoff`) told the other nothing: for 12 s it went on showing that player's figure frozen where they stood, enemies held for them and their fight's walls shut. Turning off says so now (BYE), and they're gone at once; sync on again brings them back. Sim: `guestSide`. On the copies: gone 0.1 s after `netoff` both ways, back on `neton`. Their relay stays linked, so the tip says it too ("your partner turned co-op sync off.", in Chinese too).
169. In a party, one guest resting at a bell ended every boss fight on the other PCs, not only that guest's: a second guest's copy of the host's fight lost its bar and walls, E at the wall stopped working, and the host's B is told once, so it never came back. A guest's rest ends that guest's fights alone now. Sim: `bells and deaths` (fails without the fix).
170. With two players too: the partner resting at a bell while this player was in a boss fight of their own took the bar down and brought every monster back in the middle of that fight. The partner resting isn't in it: a fight of one's own goes on, and the enemies here come back at the next rest. (The host dying still ends it: that's everyone's.) Sim: `bells and deaths`, two players (fails without the fix). On the copies: a boss bar up on the host, the guest's R bell came, and the bar stayed with "A boss fight of your own is on".

### Online, host and guest, 171 to 180

171. A load the partner told of (LOAD), or their sync turned off (BYE), outlived a sync turned off and on again here: in the new session a crash took the minute to notice. Both go with the rest of what was heard.
172. Two numbers from the wire had no bounds: a kicked stone's resting place ("1e30" is a number, and the boulder was put there), and a gift's kind, any length. Positions past 100 km and kinds over 16 characters are dropped, as everywhere else.
173. A guest's copies of the host's enemies are held at 1 health so only the host says when one dies, but "held already" went by the copy's address. A copy gone (unloaded, a rest) and a new one put at its address by the game was taken for held and never got the floor: it died here on a hit, the host's alive. The floor is set at every turn (on a puppet the host holds for a guest too).
174. A pairing remembered by address (an enemy of the host's, missing from a snapshot for a moment) was taken again with no look at what's there now: another kind of enemy at a gone one's address was steered as the host's. It has to be the same kind now, as a new pairing does.
175. An enemy stepped aside here (not in the host's world) was remembered by address too: a new one at that address took the old one's record, stood with its AI stopped and was never woken. The record goes by the enemy's name as well.
(173-175 come from a review of the enemy rounds; the address reuse can't be staged in the sim, so they stand on the reading.)
176. A partner's sit at a bell rested a player alive in a boss fight of their own: full health and gourd mid-fight, with nothing else changed, and the partner could sit down for it on purpose. The user's rule now: the boss is rested too, back to full health, and the tip says so. The dead and the spectating still come to the bell the sitter rang, with nothing lost. Sim: `bells and deaths`. On the copies: boss 1313 to 4376 of 4376, host 170 to 850 of 850, the fight going on.
177. (A feature, the user's: "once you reach it you can travel to it for free".) A trip from the map to a bell takes a Traversal Talisman (`Item_Traversing`) and can't be made without one. `freetravel.lua` keeps one in the bag at all times and gives back what a trip took, the player's own talismans included, so every bell already reached is a free trip; nothing new is unlocked. Quiet (no card), not sent to a partner as found; the talisman can't be sold (`bCanSell` False, no use of its own), so a free one is worth nothing else. On the copies: none left, one back within a second; one taken during a trip and one taken 2 s before a trip, both given back (4 stayed 4). Not seen: the game's own charge through the map's screen, which is scripted where the bench can't press it. (Then the user made two map trips on the host copy: one taken at each, one given back, 5 talismans before and after.)
178. E at a black wall with no floor found just past it (a drop, stairs, a ledge behind the doorway) left the player outside with "try another part of it", and a boss whose walls all looked like that could never be joined (the user's worry: "are you sure all accessible to all bosses?"). The pass now looks beside the one inside too, a few metres off them, on the floor of the fight, and walks the player across to there. Online and split screen both (one `game.passFog`). Sim: `guestSide` (a drop just past the wall, the fight's floor further in: across to beside the other player). On the copies: `bench/bosswalls.ps1`, every arena of the nine story maps from the outside: 92 arenas, 80 in at the first try, 5 more on a second (the tester had stood on a ledge or too soon after landing), one not a boss's (a helper's spawner outside its own wall).
179. Found by that run: E only looked for a wall whose line was within 6 m above or below the player, and some walls' lines lie well under the floor, the wall rising from them. Lei Dang's (phase 2, Ximeng) is 8 m under it on both sides, so at that wall E did nothing at all, and Chang Hong's, Gu Wanyue's, the Lion Dance's and an arena on DengDingShanDao looked the same. A wall's line may now be up to 15 m under the player (6 m over, as before). Sim: `guestSide` (a wall 8 m under the floor). On the copies: all five got in with it: Chang Hong, Gu Wanyue (2 of 3 walls; the third's line is 36 m above where the tester stood), the Lion Dance (by 178's fallback, 45 m in), DengDingShanDao's MBoss arena, and Lei Dang. So every arena of the run is reachable with E, the helper's aside.

### 1.6.1: the game update of 2026-11-04

180. Steam's update of 2026-11-04 (build 26367565) stopped UE4SS before any mod loaded: "AOB scans could not be completed ... GNatives ... GUObjectArray". The signature files of the UE4SS package the guide names were pinned to build 26266538 (its timestamp, image size and addresses) and refused the new one, though the code they look for had only moved. Phantom Pact now ships its own two signature files (`mod/ue4ss/UE4SS_Signatures`, into `platform\steam\ue4ss\UE4SS_Signatures`), written for it: not pinned to a build, each match has to agree with itself (GUObjectArray: the lea and both reads of index +4 name the same array; GNatives: 255 handlers, all in the game's own code). Each pattern matches once in both builds and gives the same addresses as before (checked on both EXEs on disk). The old package's own files may not be changed or passed on, so they aren't. The build check knows 26367565. On the copies, build 26367565: both found, 1.6.1 loaded ("tested"), linked at 1 ms, a boss fight matched on the guest and its death ran there too.

### 1.6.2: joining with a finished save

181. A player's report: joining a newcomer's game with a 100% NG+ save filled the newcomer's bag with the finished save's weapons, items and gear, and spoiled their game. On linking each player sent their whole bag and the other took what they lacked, both ways; and a quest the host had under way that the guest's save had finished was finished for the host too. The user's rule: "the host is the one that gives the things so if a guest joined a host he will catch up to him but if the guest who has 100% joined the host nothing will happen". Now only the host's bag is sent on linking (a guest still gets the host's boss weapons the story needs), a host takes no bag, and a guest takes none from another guest. A guest's quest steps count for the host only when finished while linked: the first report after linking is the guest's baseline, and it starts again at each link. Money was already only what's gained while linked, no lump sum at the link. Things picked up while playing together are still shared both ways, as before. Sim: `the host's story point` (a guest ahead at the link changes nothing; a step the guest finishes while linked does reach the host; the bags one way).

### 1.6.3: player reports

182. Fierce Axe (the Dragon Pillar camp): the swordsmen fighting beside the players ("NpcHelper", also spelt NPChelper and NPCHelper) were synced as the host's enemies, so on the guest they were held and couldn't be talked to, and the guest's copies of the mobs died by a quiet kill no quest hears of, saved dead so a reload didn't help. Allies are left to each world now, and a copy dies by the game's own death at level 0, which pays nothing (checked on the copies: no dust, no items, no drop). Sim: the allies aren't shared; a copy's death is the game's own.
183. The Bomber that opens the way to the Red House (BP_Monster_BombManRespawn_C) respawns again and again for the puzzle: killed for good on the guest, the way stayed shut. Enemies the game respawns like that are each world's own now.
184. Quest items (the tower key of Deep in the Valley, Yuer's Flesh Flower, every "NarrativeItem" in the game's item table, 85 of them) are never shared: a pickup took the other player's copy away and put the item quietly in their bag, so their quest never moved (or they handed in what they never found). Each player picks up their own, card and quest step included; not in the join-time bag either. Sim: a quest item one player takes stays in the others' worlds.
185. Both players handing in to Yuer at once froze one of them in the dialogue: a story step applied under an open dialogue finished what the talk waited for. Story steps and a partner's scene skip wait while a dialogue is open (and 5 s after).
186. The Order's hunter came with both phases at once, two bars up: a phase of a boss is never spawned while another phase of it is alive. Phases are one boss by name (HookManP1/P2, but also ChangHong, MuXiaoKui and LeiDang, whose first phase has no P1 in its name).
187. The gold-masked boss's posture bar stayed empty on the guest after a break: the guest's copy now follows the host's posture back up, as health does.
188. Madman's Broken Sword: its drink (right button + F) puts a wine jar in the left hand, and the partner's ghost drank from nothing. The jar now shows on the ghost (a plain mesh from an allow-list, never the game's own jar class, which is a bullet). Checked on the copies with a screenshot. A sweep of 13 weapons and about 230 abilities found no other prop.
189. Chains broken with a Phantom Edge out of a fight stayed whole in the partner's world: blockers that break near a player now break in every world on that map. Levers and gates that one player opens open in the others' worlds too (one way), as chests and kick-stones already did.
190. A killed enemy's health bar was left floating over the spot: it goes with the kill now (checked on the copies).
191. With story sync off (FollowHostStory = Off) a guest still got the host's things on joining: the join-time bag no longer fills either.

### 1.6.5: player reports on 1.6.3

192. "Why can't I ring the bell in my friend's game?" A guest whose save is behind the host's has the host's long-dead enemies alive in their world; near the host the mod hides them, AI stopped, not killed, so the guest's save keeps them. But the game refuses a bell with a live enemy within some 10-30 m, hidden or not, collision or not (tried on a real bell: refused with a held enemy 8 m off, hidden too; rung with it killed, or 30 m off, or 500 m up). Such an enemy now waits 500 m up and comes back to the same spot when the host moves on. Proven on a real bell. Sim: `two saves` (it waits high up; back where it stood).
193. The forest's last boss (YuCong, before Lu Family Village) died on the host with no scene after it, its body left, and the trip on never opened, in the host's save for good. A guest's killing blow forced the boss dead when the game wouldn't let it die then (between phases, its health locked for the scene to come): the kill effect refused, the mod fell back to a quiet kill outside the boss's script. Now a boss the game won't let die is left at 1 for the game, a floor the game set (Health_Min) is never crossed by a partner's blow, a guest's kill of a boss far from the host runs the game's whole death (it was the quiet kill), and the host never spawns a phase of a boss of phases because a guest's fight asked (only its own fight script does, after its scene). Phases are grouped by name with "_P<n>..." and "_Seqs..." too (YuCong_P1, YuCong_Seqs028, YuCong_P3_1/2/3, YuCong_P3_Yuu). Not proven on YuCong itself: the bench save is past it. A save already stuck needs the fight again or an older save.
194. After the host's fight with a boss the guest had beaten in their own save (brought back for it), the guest's copy could stay standing with its own AI: the guest was "in combat" at every bell near it (the bell at Mirrorwood Depths) and shut out of the next boss's arena. When the host's fight ends, a copy of that boss still standing is put down, by its name or another phase of it, if the host killed it or the guest's save had it beaten. Sim: `player reports on 1.6.3`.
195. "He teleported into the arena, but I couldn't enter": F3 now takes a guest into the partner's boss fight from anywhere on the map, not only from over 50 m away; E at the black wall still works.
196. A guest who joined during the host's boss fight is held watching it until it ends; a fight with no end (a boss left standing with its bar up, a long scene, the host's state gone quiet) held them for good, keys and pad dead: "he was stuck standing in place". Now they're let go after 3 minutes, free to move, with F3 to go in. Sim: a fight that never ends.
197. The gold-masked man of the first map: "one of the players can't move or attack, and the boss becomes unbeatable". Two causes found that fit. (1) The guest's copy followed the host's posture and health back up after 4 and 20 of the enemy updates; at 30 a second (1.6.4) that was 0.13 s and 0.67 s, shorter than a round trip, so the guest's own hits on the posture bar were put back and a break never came on the guest's side. Now 1 s, by the clock. (2) The gold-masked man isn't a boss to the game's codex, and with the players apart, enemies in his arena could be handed to the guest's game mid-fight, the host's copy held at 1 health. Nothing is handed over while either player's boss bar is up. Sim: in a boss fight nothing is handed to the guest's game. Not reproduced on the boss itself.
198. Story catch-up did nothing since the game's 11-04 update: the two story tables it read (FlowEventTable, FlowQuestBranchStateDataTable) are gone from the game. The rows now ship with the mod (storydata.lua, 659 rows, 57 quests) and are applied with the game's own calls: quest steps, flags, flow marks, bells, UI. Proven on the copies: a guest caught up 57 rows.
199. The chains the host broke before the golden-hammer boss, and the bell after its scene, never came to the guest (reports 7 and 8): they're the level's own flow, not story rows. The host now sends what its flow adds while linked (flags held for two looks, flow steps, woken bells), told again to anyone who links later; a world loading in is not sent. Sim: `player reports on 1.6.3`.
200. A guest's killing blow lost or refused left the enemy at 1 health on the guest, hit after hit, for good (report 1). It's sent again every 0.8 s up to 4 times, and the host answers one it didn't take with what the enemy has left (LR, to that guest alone). A copy hit onto its floor by any amount counts. Sim.
201. Small enemies went invisible after a teleport or a bell sit (report 3): the host said "not here" while its own world was still loading them in. Nothing is said while the host's world settles, and a "not here" goes stale after 25 s. Sim.
202. Talent-tree slots locked after a player left (report 5): the leaving player's clone ghost is put away (hidden, frozen far up) instead of destroyed mid-world, as its end of life unbinds the skill systems it shares with the player. Unproven cause.
203. A boss up again on the host after a fall (Xiaoxue) stayed dead for the guest (report 9): it comes back on the guest, tried again until it's up. A guest's own earlier hit kept the dead body paired and stopped this (found by the fuzzer, seed 6). Sim.
204. The forest's last boss stood twice for the host (one spawned for the guest's fight, one from the host's own scene): the spawned one goes when the world's own appears; a ghost twin (5-6 players) isn't taken for that. A boss the game won't let die keeps its level, floor and bar.
205. New game: both players came out of the prologue without the healing gourd. Co-op keeps its hands off the prologue: no ghost, no partner trips, no story flow, no partner's boss, F3 refused. A `gourd` dev command gives one to a save already missing it.
206. A guest crashed in the frame it made the host's ghost and hid it at once after a trip: the ghost's weapons are hidden a moment later.
207. The guest started a boss fight first, and the host's own fight with that boss was taken for a mirror of the guest's, ended with it: walls open, bar gone, the boss alive, its kill the host's alone (report 9). The game's own bar on a boss shown for a guest makes it the host's own fight. Sim.
208. A boss that fell in the host's fight and stood up again under the bar the game kept up (Xiaoxue) came back on the guest with no fight, and its death there was a quiet one: no scene, no reward. The host takes it as its fight again and says so. Sim.
209. The chains the host broke just before its boss fight waited on the guest for the whole fight, a fight shown there taken for the guest's own (report 7). Sim.
210. A copy written to exactly its floor took the guest's blows with no drop, none ever sent (report 1): it's held half a point above. A killing blow with no answer at all lifts the copy so the next one is told again. A line from the host stops hiding an enemy at once (report 3), a host's same-map bell trip lets its world settle before saying "not here", and a line from before a K no longer brings the dead enemy back with another standing in for it. Sim.
211. A downed player watching a partner whose body was put away (a party shrinking) watched the sky till respawn. Put-away bodies are taken back for the next ghost instead of a new one each time. Twin classes not found once are tried again in 30 s. Enemies no longer turn to a partner on another map in the 3 s before the ghost hides. Sim.
212. Issue #15: a story item one player picked up (the Snake Tongue, a token half, in the snake houses) vanished from the other player's world with nothing given, their quest stuck until they swapped host and guest. Quest items are never shared (each player picks up their own), but a pickup whose item couldn't be read, or was read before the item table was, went out as loot. Only what's known to be loot goes now, and a known quest item is never taken from the other world. Sim. Not checked against the reporter's log.
213. Issue #15 as it really was (from the reporter's log): the story items of the snake houses lie in searchable piles. A pile one player opened was taken from the other's world, the quest item inside with it, never shared. A pile or chest whose content is a quest item now stays for the other player to open. Sim.
214. The game crashed inside UE4SS within a second of a map's load, three times on the bench (each copy in turn), just after a trip. A trace of every step named the chains-and-levers job, whose world search still hands back the old map's actors being torn down then. The slower jobs wait 5 s after each load. Not yet proven: the bench sweep runs again.
215. A report on 1.6.9: "after the boss's guard is broken, it keeps getting hit stunned ... it can't fight back until we kill it". 1.6.9 never had 1.7.0's limit on a partner's flinches (72ee710 is on 1.7.0 only): every guest hit that made the guest's copy flinch was forced on the host's boss. Two things were left in 1.7.0. The host forced every hit on an empty bar, read after the partner's own write. And on the guest's screen the copy flinched at the guest's every swing and dropped the host's move till the next one. A break now plays once: the hit that empties the bar, or one that broke the copy (the game holds a broken bar a little above 0, the elite swordsman's at 4), and not again within 6 s. Nothing is forced over a reaction the game set itself. A boss copy's own flinch in the middle of the host's move gives way: the move again, its ability and its animation where the host's is (`puppets.resumeMove`). Sim: `bossReactions` (a break, once, the copy's flinch giving way). Real (`stunbench2.ps1`): Xigu's elite swordsman, its bar put at 150, light attacks every 0.45 s for 20 s. The host alone: about 8 s of flinches while its broken bar refilled (the game's own, solo too), then it fought (11 moves of its own). The guest alone, before: the copy reacted 16 s of 20 and skipped the host's attacks. Now it plays the host's taunts, flinches and attacks as the host's boss does (10.1 s reacting against its 7.9 s).
216. A report on 1.6.9: joining a boss fight a step late, "the boss health bar sometimes duplicates (shows two bars), and the boss's health bar remains full and doesn't decrease" (on the partner's screen it went down). Not two bars on one boss: on the bench the mod's mirror bar and then the game's own on the same actor left one bar, the HUD keeping one an actor. Two bosses, each with its bar. The one spawned for the partner's fight (a late joiner's, or one for a fight that began out of reach) was to give way to the same boss from this world's own fight scene. It never did. `dropExtraBoss` read the cached enemy list, made before that spawn, so its first look found nothing and let the watch go. A spawn not in yet as it was asked for made no watch at all. Now the look is fresh (once a second, while a watch lasts), a watch outlives a miss in its first 3 s, and one not in yet is kept by its spawner and found there later. Another boss of the same id counts as the scene's too, of another class or not. A late joiner's stand-in is let go when the fight ends, as a joiner's is. The mirror moves with the pairing: when the host's id is paired with another copy, the bar on the one before comes down. Sim: `four.lua` (a stand-in found by its spawner gives way to the scene's boss; it failed on the code before), and the review's two-bar scenario (A, C, D, E, F; one variant left, a scene boss of another class and another id).
217. A report on 1.6.9: "On the guest's side, some items may be obtained twice when picked up. Such as Killing Aura Amber." Since 1.6.7 no pickup was told (`I`) to the partner in a session where nothing had read the game's item table yet. A pickup is told only once that table is read (issue #15), and with quest items shared, `keptApart` no longer read it. The partner's copy stayed, so the second to pick it up had it twice, once from the share and once their own. It shows on the guest: the host usually leads, and the host's table was read only by a share of something it already had. `keptApart` reads the table again, so every pickup is told and the partner's copy goes, as in 1.6.6 (quest items too, shared since 1.6.7). And a shared item is given a second time (GiveItem) only when the bag's count didn't move at all. A fourth Sha-Chi Amber shard became an amber as it came, the count fell, and a second shard was added; local co-op's player 2 the same. Sim: `pickupsFresh` (it failed on the code before); the review's scenarios A, A2, A' and E. Not done, rarer: two players at one pickup within a second, a copy loaded after the `I` came, and a joining guest's copy of a chest the host opened alone (the review's patch, `rep3_fix.patch`, has them).
218. 217's report again, its bigger cause (found by the review's skeptics): a kill's reward given in both worlds. The host's game gives it at the death and shares it (`T`) that frame. The guest's copy dies at the host's K by the game's own death, and its game gives the same thing a moment after. `receiveGift` drops a share only when this world gave first, so on the guest, the share first, it kept both: the guest's side, as reported (8 or 9 of the 37 amber fragments come from kills). A partner's share now waits 3 s before it's given, up to 12 s while a scene plays (a boss's death), never past the 15 s the check goes by. Given here meanwhile, it's dropped: one each. Shares from a partner come 3 s later for it. Sim: a kill's reward, the guest's own a second after the share, one each (it failed on the code before), and the review's P1 at 0 and 1 s. Left: the same thing from both games 17 s apart (a scene item on the bench, 11-03), and a chest the host opened paid again to a joining guest whose copy came back shut.
219. A report on 1.6.9: "all items will only show as picked up after opening the chest", "sometimes the client picks up an item, but the host doesn't actually get it. If you teleport a long distance, then fast travel back, you'll find the item glow on the ground is still there and can be picked up". The user: "people want to be notified what their friends picked up on the right same as when you pick items the small notification that appears". Four causes, on the bench 11-09. (1) A share given with the backpack's notice flag only waits in its queue (`PushGetPropInfos`) until the game's own pickup flow shows it, at this player's next pickup or chest; a share of something they had came with the flag off, nothing at all. `TryToPushPickupUI` shows it at once, the game's own line on the right ("Forged Metal + 1"), when the game can (`CanPushPickupUI`, no card up). A thing new to the player would get the full-screen card in a game that isn't paused for it: held a moment first, quietly (added and taken back), the game counts it seen and shows the line. Notes, keys and quest items keep their card, at the game's own moment, the lines queued behind one with it. (2) The partner's copy of a ground pickup, destroyed at their word (`I`), was made again when its area streamed back in, and one loaded after the word was never taken: the names are kept per map for the session, and such a copy goes as it loads. (3) A ground pickup's share was dropped as "the game gave it here too" when this world had found the same thing within 15 s, two players finding the same herb in a moment. What our copy held is waited for (`game.awaitShare`), and its share is given whatever was given here lately. (4) A pickup whose share never came (taken in without the flag) left this player nothing, their copy gone: after 16 s it's given from what the copy held, a thing of the item table only, and a share after that is dropped. Sim: four, "a partner's finds on the right" (11 checks, 7 fail on the code before); `two` differs only by (4): its guest's pickup gives no share. Bench, two copies: a material the guest's game was given showed on the host's screen 3 s later as "Flawless Twinned Metal + 1", no card, though it was new to the host. Not done: across a reload, a partner-taken pickup is in the host's save again (the game's own save of it, `PBZActorSaveComponent`, not written). Two adversarial reviews of this (11-09/10) found more, fixed: (5) a copy not loaded here when the word came had its share dropped if this world had found the same thing lately, and went as it loaded: nothing at all. The word now says what the pickup held and how many (`I <name> ? - <item> <n>`; an older one's three fields read as before), waited for the same. (6) A share matched by its item alone: a kill's reward of 2 took a pickup of 1's wait and the pickup's share came on top (5 where 3). A share takes the wait of its own count first, and one of what this world was just given, not the pickup's count, is that event's. (7) A party: a second guest's word for one gone here is noted, its share the same thing, and one guest's word again for a pickup back in their world isn't taken for a second's. A guest whose copy was found taken at the first's word still tells its own (it didn't: the host got two). (8) One's own take told by the partner's word before this world's look, its copy destroyed, was taken for a copy not loaded, and the share came as a second: a copy at the last look, gone since, its thing just given here, is this player's. Sim: four, "pickups in a race" (6 checks; 4 fail on the code before, 2 guard the new paths), and the reviews' 17 scenarios, all passing but one case where a guest takes a copy the word already removed (the game's walk-up can't). A third review (11-10, 12 agents, every finding run in the sim and put to a skeptic: 9 of 9 confirmed) found these, fixed: (9) the word's count is the taker's game's last give of the thing, and a kill's reward between their take and their look made it the reward's: with a find of this world's own of the same thing a while before, the pickup's share was dropped as that find's and the reward's given in its place. A share is another event's only when this world's own give came no more than 3 s before it arrived. (10) The note for a second guest's word went by item alone, and their own find of the same thing just before took it. It goes by count too. (11) A word before this world's first look, as it loads in, was filed under no save (the look reads the save's name), and the copy that came back later was there to take again. The word reads it. (12) Old shares were taken off under the one found, so its place pointed past the list: the word's handling stopped with a script error, the copy still there (older than 1.7.0). Old ones go first, and the copy goes before anything else. (13) A player who ran to a pickup from out of the look's 5 m and took it as the other did was taken for a copy not loaded. One within 15 m at the last look counts, and that take is told the others, as the look would. The list of shares given grew until a partner's ground pickup cleared it (older than 1.7.0): it's cleared as each comes. Known, not changed: a word over 16 s after its share (the link down between the take and the look) gives it a second time, as 1.6.9 did for a loaded copy. Sim: four, "pickups in a race" (11 checks now; the 5 new ones fail on the code the review saw).
220. A report on 1.6.9: "for some bosses, after the host enters, the guest can't deal damage; when the guest becomes the host, still can't" (the 11-06 report on 1.6.6 again; no boss named, no log). Not reproduced. Bench 11-09, two copies, both saves past the four arena bosses: YuCong's third phase brought back the mod's way (`game.spawnBoss`) took the host's hits, then a guest's who came late (4 sent, 4 applied, on both); ChiLian (alive by YuCong's arena) took nobody's before its fight woke (hidden, an idle montage: the game's own), and once awake the host's and a late guest's both (9 sent, 9 applied). A lead that fits both halves: the host's world tells its new flow steps (`FLOW n`), and a guest marks them done without running them (`ExecuteNodeGuid:Add`), a mark that "skips its setting up" (flowsync's own words). The host's own map load on the bench sent 74 of them, under the 200 taken as a load. A boss armed by one would stay unarmed on a guest whose world hadn't run it, and the mark is saved: so too when that player hosts later. Tried and taken back (da821db, reverted 11-10): the steps of the 20 s after the host's own load held back as its state. Two adversarial reviews found it held real ones: a step in the 5 s the mod keeps still after a load was never sent, the end of the window took steps of play for the load's, and a second load inside it cut it short. The safe repair, a fresh look at the end of that quiet, would search the world mid-load (214's crash). The steps of a host's load reach a guest as in 1.6.9. Sim: four, the 1.6.3 reports part (a step just before the host's load, one on arrival, one in a gap of the link with a load in it: each reaches the guest). Still unproven, still open: it needs the boss's name or the guest's UE4SS.log.
221. The 1.6.9 report again, 11-10, after aae17cc: "When playing online with teammates, after the boss's guard is broken, it keeps getting hitstunned. The boss can't recover its aggression or poise, and it can't fight back until we kill it." aae17cc was checked on Xigu's elite swordsman only; on a real boss, the Gold-Masked One (bar 1055, bench 11-10, both players swinging every 0.5 s for 30 s), 1.7.0 as it was still did it: 5 breaks, 9 moves of its own, 21 s of the 30 reacting (1.6.9's rule, every partner flinch forced: 4 breaks, 12 moves, 20 s; the host alone: 1 break, 23 moves, 7 s). Three causes. (1) A partner's blow only comes reported, and the host wrote its posture with none of the game's rules for one: every guest blow took 80, while the host's own swings were mostly deflected or dodged (one in four landed), so the bar was empty again 4 to 5 s after each fill. A boss now takes no posture from a partner's blow in a break of its own and the fill after it (the game's own blows take none then), for 6 s after its bar is back (the game's break leaves it fighting fresh; the same on both PCs), or while it turns blows aside, a deflect, parry or dodge by the move's name or its /Dodge/ or /Deflect/ folder (no game tag says so: the boss's ability system carries none), not the attacks that come out of one. No reaction of theirs plays on it then, nor in the 6 s after a partner's break. Its health takes every blow. The same rules run on the guest's copy, fed by the host's line, so its bar keeps with the host's and doesn't break there alone; a copy that this very blow broke is left broken (its break and the guest's execution play out) and the blow goes to the host, even a parry's with no health in it. A boss's ghost twin (six players) takes partner blows by the same rules: every flinch of theirs was forced on it. (2) A guest's break that the game held a little above empty, the Gold-Masked One's at 1.64, the elite swordsman's at 4: above 1.5 it was taken for a bar the game had filled again, so nothing ever filled it, and every break of the copy's broke the boss again (1.6.8's refill missed it). A partner's break that plays on the host empties its bar there, filled 6 s later as a break's, one already at 0 too; empty is a two-hundredth of the bar, and the guest no longer pulls its copy down to a broken bar so held (it broke a second time there). (3) A partner's blow that emptied the host's bar with no break on the copy used up the break's once, and the copy's own break a moment later was refused: the guest saw a break the host's boss never had. Only a break that plays counts now (its tag, State.Break, not the poise levels Hitreact.Break.LvN), none is forced over a break or fill of the game's own, and a break the copy already had plays though the boss deflects. Two adversarial reviews (11-10: 9 agents on the causes, 9 on the fix) found more in the first version, fixed: the guest refused the very blow that broke its copy, by the copy's own break (a parry's break went nowhere); a blow leaving the bar a little up, not broken, was filled to the top 6 s on (stamp only a bar a blow empties, one already at 0 too); each PC's own CoopBossPoise set the recovery, 3 to 30 s (a fixed 6 s now); attacks named like dodges. Bench after (the mod's code, two copies): both swinging, 2 breaks (one of them the test's own, its bar put at 150), 15 moves, 13 s reacting, the boss countering while it filled; the guest alone, 30 moves in 40 s, 1.4 s reacting, the bars it broke filled again 6 s on. What's left is the game's own: every hit flinches a boss while its broken bar fills, and the partner's figure's real (token) blows from behind it flinch it too. Open: a guest's execution of a boss may not play its victim's half on the host (1.7.0's limit on a partner's flinches since 72ee710; the state's name is unknown, not tried on the bench). Sim: four, "a boss's posture and a partner's blows" (21 checks, 12 fail on the code before, the rest guard the new paths, an older guest's among them), and six players' boss twin (fails on the code before).
222. The same report, its last part, found by the user watching the bench (11-10): "the bosses should not be able to be knocked back ... if they get knocked back or react to hit they can't do anything". After 221 the posture rules were the game's, but every hit still made a boss flinch, and two players hit twice as often: on the Gold-Masked One, both swinging, it spent 51% of the fight reacting (31% with one player), 57% of the damage going in in the refill after each break, where every hit flinched it. Its armour profile can't stop that: the Gold-Masked One's AbstractEndureConfig (AbstractEndure_Minion) gives EndureLevel 0 in plain attacks and none between moves or in the refill, and every level put at 8 changed nothing; ActorState refuses an endure state put on by hand; HitreactComponent:CanBreakEndure goes through the script layer, but answering "no" in a hook only cut flinches between breaks. What works is letting the flinch go at once: CoopBossArmor = On (the ini ships it; left out, it's off, so with the row off nothing changes) makes a boss this PC runs, while someone is with you, end a hit state of the flinch family (under State.Normal but its scripted Perform, Launchingback, PoiseBreak and the poise levels Break.LvN; the 21 the Gold-Masked One has were read from its HitreactStatesConfig, and one not known is logged once) with ChangeHitState(None, nil, true), stop its reaction montage and take up the move the hit cut where it was (puppets.resumeHost, as resumeMove does on a guest's copy: the copy never stopped it), at the start of the scaling tick and in puppets.watchHost before a montage goes out (a flinch let go never reaches a guest as a move); the guard break, an execution, death, Custom, frozen, air and floor states stay the game's; bossMayReact plays no partner flinch on it (their break still does); and a guest's boss copy gives its own reaction to their hit way at once, in the host's move to that move, between moves to nothing (puppets.armourCopy, by the guest's own setting: both should set it the same; on the bench the guest still saw 15% before it, mostly a knockback playing on after its hit state was over). A review of the first version (3 agents) found: it went by the connection, so a partner on another map, down, or watching a fight begun alone gave a boss fought alone its armour (now: a partner's figure here, this player up, not a solo fight); a hit still cut the host's boss's move while the guest's copy played it on, its replayed hits landing on the guest (now taken up again on the host); a new extra tick reordered the others (folded into the scaling tick: the two sim's old-against-new compare is the same with it off). The numbers, the user's calls after the bench: CoopBossPosture = 200 (the guard bar 200/300/400%: two players broke it 3.49 times a minute on the enemies' 130%, one player 2.65) and no CoopBossHealth (bosses on the enemies' 150/200/250% again; the 200 of d19a7cf was the same day's). Ideas tried on the bench and dropped: a 200% bar with a refill twice as fast (the game held the fill back to 1.4 times; 44% reacting), a bigger bar alone (estimated 42%). Bench with the final code (two copies, the shipped ini, both swinging every 0.5 s for 120 s): the host's boss reacting 4% of the fight and the guest's copy 5%, nearly all of it the guard break's own stagger; its guard breaking 2.67 times a minute (one player: 2.65). Not proven: a player's perfect deflect with super armour on (a script spamming GA_Hun_Tang_HoldDeflect_C for two minutes only got player 1 hit and guard-broken, no deflect landed); none of the boss's 21 hit states is a deflect recoil, and the State.Normal.Counter seen came with knockback montages from plain hits. Sim: four's "boss super armour" (18 of its checks fail on the code before; the rest guard the new paths: not with you, a fight begun alone, the row off), the shipped ini's numbers, bossReactions and bossPosture on the game's own flinch path; two, old scripts against new: the same with the row off.
223. A player's report on 1.6.9 (11-09): "When playing online with friends, there is no sound effect when teammates hit monsters, it feels like they are hitting air, which is very awkward"; and on 1.7.0 (11-10) "Can we see sound sync in a few days". 1.6.5's token Attack (d1ac7d0: the figure's swings to land as the game's own hits, with sound and spark) was never measured; its only bench check had failed. In co-op the figure only plays montages, never abilities, and its blade lands nothing: on the bench (11-10, two copies, each copy's sound metered on its own through Windows' own audio API, no download) the guest's 12 hits played nothing on the host (fold z 2.6), and with a hook on the game's hit-effect chooser the host's 3 hits asked it nothing on the guest. Now (hitfx.lua, PartnerHitSounds = On, the ini ships it; left out, nothing changes): each PC hooks its chooser, BP_HitEffectPlayer_Chooser:GetEffectData (a Blueprint function, so the script layer sees it, with no interface parameter), and keeps what its own hits on non-player bodies asked for (attack type, strength, body material: the hero's blade on flesh, a light hit, is SFX_Combat_Hit_SlashTangBlade_Meat_Light and three blood systems from DT_HitSound_TangMeat and DT_HitParticles_SlashTangMeat) for 0.4 s. When the mod counts that hit (a guest's report, or a drop on an enemy this game runs with one of its moves just before) the newest goes as a quick HX <enemy> <type> <strength> <material>, and every other PC asks its own chooser the same, posts the sound on its copy of that enemy and spawns the blood on its body's bone, 25 cm toward the hitter's figure. No wire change for older versions (they drop an event they don't know); no slow-down, camera shake or rumble (the hitter's own); capped at 24 a second and 50 ms per enemy, within 60 m; off in PvP. Bench after (two copies): every HX sent was played (17/17 and 10/10 for the guest's hits, 5/5 for the host's), the averaged sound rising +0.023 about 0.3 s after each play (0 of 500 random moments as high on the host; 12 of 500 on the guest, 5 hits), about 80 ms after the hitter's own sound on loopback; blood seen by the enemy on the host. Bench lessons: a hook on HitreactComponent's IsAdditive, OnStateEnd and ChangeHitState crashed the host at the enemy's first deflect (GetEffectData and CanBreakEndure alone ran clean); the chooser asked for a deflect gives nothing (the game plays deflects through another effect system), so an enemy's deflect of a partner's swing is still silent. A sim pass (27 checks) and a review (3 reviewers, each serious finding checked by a skeptic) found, fixed: the hook fires on the mod's own chooser asks too, so a receiver that had just moved filed the partner's hit as its own and sent it back (a flag while it asks); a partner's blow written by handlers.D, or a guest-run enemy written down to the guest's lines, read as the host's own drop (D is wrapped, its blow taken off the baseline; guest-run ones only noted); a killing blow told again (0, 0, L) took a fresh record meant for another hit; a guest's hits on an enemy its own game runs far from the host sent nothing (handover.share now counts them); an effect player of a class without GetEffectData was asked anyway (IsA HitreactEffectPlayer_PBZBase now). Not covered: deflects.
224. The longest tips ran off both sides of the screen in one line, in English too (the game's tip text never wraps): "no friend hosting found. You must be Steam friends, ..." (249 letters) showed only its middle, and the F5 panel's longest lines ran past its box (the user, 11-10: "Fix it"). lang.fit breaks a line longer than a tip holds (150 letters; the panels' box 110) into the fewest lines, as even as they go, at its spaces or between Chinese characters, never before a closing mark; a line break already in it stays. Arabic is wrapped by its own text block (WrapTextAt 1700 for a tip, 1100 in a panel). Sim: fitting() and the tips and panels in a session (four.lua). Bench (11-10): the no-friend tip in two lines in English and in Arabic, its Chinese on one line that fits.
225. A player's report on 1.7.1, Apprentice Healer: the guest found two of Yuer's three herbs and the host the third; back at the camp the host couldn't hand them in, and after joining the friend's world, the herbs in the bag and the map saying they were in, Yuer still wouldn't take them. Yuer never reads the bag: her hand-in shows once the world has xg.营地玉儿.收集药材可提交, which her own flow raises once 药材1, 2 and 3 are all up, and each of those is raised only by searching that herb's pile (BP_Rubbish_C, its flow preset's AddTagWhenSuccess) in that world. The mod sent the herb (T) and took the partner's copy of the pile away (O), never the flag; and a step it finished for a player (a catch-up, a guest's step on the host: FinishQuestBranch) never raised the flags the step's own tasks wait for, so the map said ready and Yuer offered nothing (bench, 11-11: three real searches, yes; herbs shared, given quietly or with the step forced, no; the flags added by hand, yes). Many quests count found things the same way: by the flags of the piles they lie in, or by a thing's own flag while it's held (the item table's AddTagWhenHas, on about 100 things: the Bagua boy's relics, the snake parts, the masks, keys, Xiao Zhang's treasures, the Four Axes readings, Mo Jizi's letters, memory petals), which the game raises only as the thing's pickup card closes: a shared card waits for the game's own moment, often long after, and the bag on joining has none. Now: a pile or chest searched sends its own flags after its word (OF, both ways; an older version drops it), raised on the others' side when it's calm, their copy loaded or not, and a chest the host opened before is paid with its flags too; a step the mod finishes raises its branch's own tag tasks' flags (BPT_HasTag, not the ones waiting for a flag to be gone); once a load, while linked, a quest still under way gets the missing flags of its finished table steps back (the report's own save); and a thing the mod gives raises its own flag, quiet or not.
