Bench (two copies, 11-11, from a save at the quest's start, the release settings): the guest searched herbs 1 and 2 (the host's piles not even loaded) and the host herb 3: both worlds had all three flags and Yuer's own, the game finished the step itself, and the host handed in and finished the quest; a step forced the old way on the guest had its three flags back 5 s later, the host its own with the guest's step, and Yuer offered the hand-in on both (the guest's went through); a relic given quietly and a treasure with its card raised their flags at once; on these saves nothing else was put back. Bench lesson: indexing an empty game list from a probe (list[1]) froze the host; count it first. Sim: four's "quest flags" (23 checks); two, old against new: the same. Not covered: a pile an older partner searches (no OF; its step's flags still come with the story); things used, not found (the pinwheel stays each player's own, as before).
226. Issue #16 (Swor-dart, read on 1.6.9, with a player's report on 1.6.8: "boss posture not visibly changing on partner hits although HP changes"): a partner's blows moved a boss's health bar but not the gold posture strip under it, on both PCs. The mod writes a partner's blow into a boss's posture by hand (`game.setPosture`: on the host, the guest's reported blow and the refill 6 s after a partner's break; on a guest, its copy pulled down or up to the host's line, and a blow its copy takes back), and a value written so reaches no listener: the strip follows the game's own changes only. And a guest's bar for the host's fight (`showBossBar`) was never started with its boss: the game starts a fight's own bar with `Init(boss)`, which sets its `Character` and has it follow the boss's posture by itself; registered by hand, it had no `Character` (address 0 on the bench) and its strip never moved, not even at the guest's own blows (its health the HUD follows by itself). Bench (two copies, 11-11, the Gold-Masked One by bell NLES_002, his escorts killed, his brain stopped, health 60000, the host's bar started as a fight's own; one player swinging): before, ten guest blows took his posture from 1.000 to 0.507 and the strip stood at 1.000 on both PCs (56 and 58 of 61 samples off); three host blows that landed took it to 0.852, the host's own strip with them, the guest's at 1.000. A posture written by hand to half moved the strip only with the bar's own `ForSeq_UpdateShachi` (0.500 a tenth of a second on). Now `setPosture` tells the boss's bar with `ForSeq_UpdateShachi`, the call a PvP foe's strip has used since 1.7.0 (`setFoeShachi`): the bar started with that boss, or else, as its health is told (`refreshBar`), one nobody started, never in a match (a foe's has no `Character`); and `showBossBar` starts the bar it puts up with its boss, as local co-op starts player 2's. After: the guest's blows took it from 1.000 to 0.507 and, roles swapped, the host's from 1.000 to 0.532, the strip with the posture on both PCs at every sample (0 off of 61, 60, 61 and 61); a guest's break emptied the host's strip and the refill 6 s on filled it, the guest's own break and fill on its own strip (0 off of 72 each). Sim: four's "a boss's posture bar and a partner's blows" (12 checks, 7 fail on the code before; its fake bars have no `Character` till `Init`, as the game's); two, old against new: the same. Not covered: two bosses whose bars nobody started take each other's posture writes, as their health always has.
227. Issue #16, its second point: a guest took the host's story flow (`flowsync.lua`: flags, flow steps, the bells it wakes) each in a pcall, and then let the whole queue go, taken or failed; a bell whose lighting failed, once the guest came to it, was let go the same. One that failed was lost till the next link (the host tells everything again then). None has been seen failing in play, but nothing tried one again. Now a call that fails waits for the next calm second, 5 tries in all (`TRIES`), and then goes with a log line ("couldn't be taken here", "couldn't be lit here"); the same one twice in the queue (a relink's telling again) is done or tried once; no more than 400 wait at once; and what waits is let go when the guest stops following the host's story or its story table turns out another, as in the prologue and with sync off. Sim: four's "a story step whose call fails" (5 checks, 3 fail on the code before: a flag and a flow step that failed once, one that fails every time tried 5 times and let go, a bell that failed once); two, old against new: the same.
228. Issue #16, its third point: a partner's move (`U`) heard while their figure couldn't play it was dropped: no figure yet (made a moment after a trip or a link), a hidden one (they were down, on another map, in a scene), its animation not up, or the move not to be had yet. A move is told only as it starts, so the figure stood through the rest of it, a getting-up after a revive most often. Now the newest is held 0.75 s (`HOLD_S`), looked at ten times a second, and played as soon as the figure can, as far in as the move is by then; a newer move, its end (`U - 0`), a match, or the 0.75 s let it go (counted as failed if the figure was there). And a partner who starts sync again or joins a party (`S`, `J`) is told the move this player is in, as far in as it is: they had missed its start. Not in a match: a guard's move held there could cut a swing of theirs (`M`). No wire change: an older partner plays a move told again as any other. Sim: four's "a move their figure can't play yet" (8 checks, 5 fail on the code before: a move whose figure's animation wasn't up, how far in it starts, a figure hidden and shown again, a partner who links again told the move and playing it); two, old against new: the same. Not on the bench.
229. A boss bar's name after 226 (asked: does its Init swap a boss's real name for its internal one?). A bar the mod puts up (`showBossBar`: a guest's bar for the host's fight) comes up with no name at all, as in 1.7.1; the game's own fight names its own bar. Init names it as the codex does: the elite swordsman's "Jagged Steel, the Frenzied Brute", now on the guest's bar too, where 1.7.1 showed none. But the Gold-Masked One, who has no codex row (177 rows, none his), it names by his internal name, `BP_Monster_GoldMaskMan_C_...`, and 226 put that on a guest's bar. Now a name Init gives that is the boss's own object name is taken back: the bar keeps the name it had, none. Bench (two copies, 11-11; the elite's fight started by the game as the host walked up): the elite's name on the host's bar and the guest's; the Gold-Masked One's bar, put up on the host, with no name on either PC. Trap: a boss taken off its bar and put back at once gets its old widget back, its name and `Character` with it, which made two readings look as if the HUD named a bar itself. Sim: four's posture-bar part, two checks: a boss the codex lacks keeps no name (fails on 226's code), a codex boss's name taken with Init (fails before 226).
230. A review of 226-229 (four reviewers, a skeptic on each finding): a boss bar the HUD hands back. The HUD keeps its boss bars in a pool (BossPoint, a dynamic entry box): a bar taken down comes back for the next boss registered, its Character and name as they were. 226 started a bar only when it had no Character, so one still on the boss before (his body still in the world, or the copy keepBossBar's pairing swapped out) was left on him: on a guest, the Gold-Masked One's bar after the elite swordsman's said "Jagged Steel, the Frenzied Brute", his health and strip stood still, and keepBossBar put him up three times more. Now `showBossBar` starts any new bar that isn't this boss's already, and the name Init gives a boss the codex lacks is taken to none, never to what the bar said before (229 put the previous boss's name back on a handed-back bar); local co-op's player 2 bar has the same (`game.noInternalName`; since 1.6.0 it showed BP_Monster_GoldMaskMan_C_... in player 2's half). Bench (two copies, 11-11): the elite's fight started by the game, then the Gold-Masked One's bar: the guest got the elite's own bar widget back, started now with the Gold-Masked One's copy, no name, and its strip and health followed the host's six blows (1.000 to 0.803, every sample). Also asked: does Init start boss music on a PC only showing the other's fight? No: the HUD's RegisterBossHPBar calls the bar's InitBossMusic, once a bar, before Init, on the host's own fight and on a guest's mirror alike (so since mirror bars began), and Init calls none; a guest 35 m off the host's fight sounded no different (its level 0.013 before, during and after, against the host's 0.013 rising to 0.057 in the fight). Sim: four's "a boss bar the HUD hands back" (a pooled HUD: the elite's bar for the Gold-Masked One, body there or gone, and the pairing swap; 5 checks fail on 229's code) and local co-op's player 2 bar name (fails since 1.6.0).
231. The same review: a move held for a figure that couldn't play it (228) was only looked at from an extra tick, and those wait out this PC's quiet after a load or a stall (5 s), longer than a move is held (0.75 s): the figure made after a trip, the case 228 named first, still stood through the move. Held moves are played from the frame tick now, which runs in the quiet as handlers.U does. Sim: four's held-move part, a move held in the quiet (fails on 228's code).
232. The same review, the rest of issue #16's point 2: what waits for the story flow was kept when sync went off (227 let go the bells and the counts, not the queue), so a flag that had failed was taken at the next session's first calm second, maybe in another save; the queue and the owed flags go now. A flag owed for the other player's pile or chest search (OF, flow.raise) whose call failed was let go with the whole list: it's tried again a calm second on, 5 times, as the host's flags are. And a stuck save's mend (225) marked a step mended before raising its flags, so a flag the game wouldn't take was left short for that load: a step one of whose flags failed is mended again at the next read of the quests, 3 times in all. Sim: four's story-step part, three checks (an owed flag failing once, a failed flag at sync off, a mend's flag failing once), all failing on 229's code.
233. A player report on 1.6.9: "When I play multiplayer with my friends, monsters deal very little damage to me. Attacks that used to take half my health now only take a sixth. It feels like I have absurdly high armor" (also as "my character has become extremely tanky"). Not reproduced online: on the bench (11-11) every hit took the game's own number with the mod off, with 1.6.9's release files and with this code, alone, as host and guest, as local players 1 and 2, early (a Xigu thug 60/80/200, the Gold-Masked One 30-200 by move) and late (a Level 10 spearman, the enemy's Attack x a share a move), and after 1.6.9's held respawn. Only Wayfarer takes less, 0.7 of every hit, the game's own: in 1.6.9 each player's own difficulty counted for the hits they took. The one way the mod did it: local co-op's hold for a player whose joystick reads as unplugged sets their health floor to their health, so nothing hurts them (bench: 0 of 28 boss hits), meant for a player who can't play. It caught player 1 playing on all the same: the keyboard and mouse always reach player 1, and a pad the game reads itself (a DualSense) can be missing from pads.txt; alone in Local mode too. A held player 1 seen steering (their movement's acceleration, which comes from input alone) is let go, till that pad is seen plugged in again; one standing still is held as before. Sim: four's padHold part, held, steered, still again, the pad back and gone, alone in Local mode (5 of its checks fail on the code before). Bench: the pad gone, standing still, held at 850 and 0 hits; the W key held a second, "plays on without their controller", floor 0 and all 30 of the boss's hits at the game's numbers; the pad back and gone again, held; alone in Local mode the same.
234. A player report on 1.7.1, three players online: "our boss damage seems to break at random times (sometimes it's right, then swaps over to wayfarer damage)". The game's own side was ruled out on the bench (11-12): a death's respawn puts nothing of the save back (the mode, bag and dust as they were, saves held back), and a boss already standing takes a mode change at once (the Gold-Masked One hit 200/100/60/30 on Hellwalker and exactly 0.7 of each after a switch to Wayfarer). So it was the mod putting a player's own mode back: co-op difficulty (1.7.0) does that 20 s after they're alone, and a partner counted as gone after 12 s with nothing heard. After a wipe everyone respawns at once, and a respawn's load tells nothing (no LOAD): the first one back from a load of 15 s or more had its own load counted as time alone and saw the others, still loading, gone, so its own Wayfarer came back at once, Hellwalker again as the next one came. Now a partner gone quiet without turning sync off counts, for up to 90 s, while the link says someone is there (main.dll's relay keeps a link alive through a load), unless their relay started over since their game last spoke (a crash and a restart: the review of this fix); and time this player isn't in the world to look isn't time alone. Sim: four's party difficulty part: a wipe with loads of 20, 30 and 40 s, the guests' loads of over a minute, a minute's hang (3 fail on the code before), a hang past 90 s (own mode back), the guests' links gone just as the host comes back, a crash and a restart, sync off.
235. The same report: "if host dies during fight, they don't get items and bell (if it spawns afterwards) doesn't show up". On the bench (11-12, Xigu's Gold-Masked One and his two guards, the host's respawn held as the mod holds it), the game runs a boss's death with its player down another way: the boss's dust is paid and kept, its "killed" flag and a step of its own are set, but none of its win's steps run (its reward, Jagged Steel's IronBlade, a step on bell NLES_002's actor, two more), and the respawn never brings them. A guest's world, its copy killed by the host's word, runs the whole win, 10 to 15 s after the death there; the reward reached the host after its respawn as the guest's find, the win's steps never, and the host's dust while down was never shared. Now a guest whose own world ran that death (killForReal) while the host was down tells the host what the death added there over the next 20 s (a scene there not counted, a load ends it; a flag only once it's been there two looks 4 s apart, never a thing's own flag while held; never a bell: the win's bell comes as a step on the bell's own actor, and a bell woken there was the guest's own ring, a crane bell's right after its boss say, and lit in the host's world by word it cost the host its own first ring and that scene: the merge review with the bells work), and the same for an enemy falling within a minute after it and 60 m of where it fell (a guard the win waits for). The host takes what its world lacks once it's up and settled, and only for a boss it owes: one by the mod's word, or one of the fight it went down in, known by name (the game takes the bar down as its player dies, and the gold-masked man is no boss to the codex, so nothing of his was owed). 15 s after the host is up, the boss's dust the game kept goes to the others as at a live kill, the part of it the dust watch held back (down, or in its quiet just after); kept or not goes by the most the wallet rose by since the death in a read, the partners' shares not counted, so spending at the bell isn't a pay lost and a pay that comes late isn't given again; where the game paid nothing, its loot and dust are given, never more than its own. Down again before that's settled: settled then if the pay came, else that down counts as the same one. A boss up again after a fall (Xiaoxue's) is owed nothing. The reviews: the draft told from the K on, for 15 s, a guest's own flags among it, and a flag on for a moment; gave back dust spent after the respawn; paid a fall as a win; owed the gold-masked man nothing; a pay read late or low was given or shared twice; down again before it was settled lost it. Sim: four's "host down when the boss dies" part (16 checks fail on 1.7.2, 14 on the draft). Bench (two copies): the host's world got all of the win's steps from the guest's, the bell actor's among them, and IronBlade after its respawn, and the guest the host's kept dust. Not covered: a quest item from a win (never shared); a step's effect beyond its record may only show at the next load there; what a guest's own play adds within its look (a flag in its first 16 s, a step or bell in 20) still reaches the host; where the game paid nothing, dust the host earns itself in its first 15 s up is taken for the pay, and nothing given.
236. A player report on 1.7.1 (three players online, early game): "unless we're near each other (like within 75m), bells and certain things don't sync". The game loads a level's things only around each player, and two kinds of thing were shared only with a world that had them loaded at the word. A chain one player broke (`BLK`) was broken in another world only if loaded there then, else dropped for good; a lever, gate or door (`SWO`) was kept two minutes, then dropped, and the host told again only what its own world had loaded open, so one a guest opened far off never reached the host or the other guest, and one opened while a guest was on another map never reached them. Now each waits for its load, kept by map (200 at most a map) over trips away and back, done even with nobody else on the map, forgotten with the session and with a world loaded from the title; and the host names every 20 s the chains broken in its world and the switches it is still waiting for, with its open ones as before, for a guest who joined later or was elsewhere at the word. A batch costs a receiver no map search for what it already has, and none at all in the quiet after a load (it waits for the first look after); a 1.7.2 PC passes over a batched `BLK` and takes a single one as before. A review of the first version found the trip, the alone, the session and the quiet cases. Sim: four's "chains and levers another player opened far off" and "chains and levers waited for: kept by map, for the session" (6 of their checks fail on 1.7.2).
237. The same report: bells. A bell the host woke went to the guests (`FLOW b`) and was lit when a guest came within 30 m of it on its map (since 1.6.7, so a guest joining a host further along doesn't get every bell). But the map subsystem says "None" for the map at times (seen on the bench, 11-12), and a bell's map never matched it: then no host bell was lit at all. And a bell a guest woke was told to nobody. Now the same-map test goes by the bell's level (from the game's map table) and takes "None" as not known; a bell is found in the table by its row's name too (most rows aren't named as their BellID); and a guest's bells woken while linked go to the other guests (`FLOW b` from a guest, passed on by the relay; not the bells from before the link, nor a load's whole list). The host's world takes none: it holds the story, and a bell lit there by word, never rung, would never have had its first ring's flags (the camp's crane bell raises Yu'er's arrival). Sim: four's "bells a guest wakes" and the "None" check in "a crane bell's scene" (fail on 1.7.2). Bench (11-12, this code, two copies): the host rang NLES_004, 111 m from the guest at the crane bell (where the guest's map had read "None"); 3 m from it the guest still had it unrung, and back at the crane bell it was lit ("lit here, as you came to it and went on").
238. The same report: "crane bell cutscenes aren't sync'd and only appear to the one who hit the bell first, even if all 3 of us time it correctly". A crane bell (`BP_MelodiousBell_StoneCrane_C`) plays a scene at its first ring (`OnFirstTouchBell`, then `Seqs003-Seqs008-Seq038`, 18 s). On the bench (11-12, 1.7.2, two copies at the crane bell NLES_006, the host ringing first) the mod took it from the guest three ways. 3 s into the host's scene the guest's PC hid the host's figure, its collision off with it; the figure stood in the bell's trigger, and the guest's bell prompt shut at that instant (`InteractionEnd`), back only with the figure: the guest's E did nothing for the whole scene (six such shut-and-back pairs, each in step with the figure). The host's scene plays 18 s of a 213 s sequence, so its end read as skipped and was told ("skipped after 18.3 of 213.2 s: the guest's too"): a guest's same scene a moment behind is cut. And the flags of the host's ring reached the guest four minutes on, and the guest's own first ring of the bell then went straight to its menu, no scene. Now a figure hidden for a scene alone (the partner's or this player's) keeps its collision; a scene that begins within 10 s of this player's own first touch of a bell (`OnFirstTouchBell`, hooked) is that bell's while it plays, its skip neither told nor taken (an older host's skip of it included; a scene after a plain sit, a respawn's rest among them, is the story's as before); a bell another player rang is never lit under a player standing by it (it is once they've been by it and gone on: theirs to ring; what they stood by is forgotten at each load), and while a guest stands by a bell the host rang, not rung in their world, the host's flags and flow steps wait (ten minutes a stand at most; a bell only another guest rang holds nothing back), and for 15 s after this player's own first touch, the bell woken at the touch and its scene coming some 5 s on. Two more reviews found the first version's sit window too wide (a boss's scene by a bell), the stand kept over a load, two lookups a tick, and the gap between the touch and the scene. Also checked there: a partner's rest (the bell's `RestoreHealth` on some loaded bell) wakes no bell. Sim: four's "a bell's first ring is each player's own" and "a crane bell's scene" (fail on 1.7.2). Bench after (11-12, this code, the host ringing first again): the guest's prompt didn't shut as the host's figure was hidden, the guest's ring 12.7 s on gave its own whole scene, and the host kept its scene's end to itself ("its end isn't told as a skip").
239. A player report on 1.7.1 (three players online, Weeping Valley): "we like the hitting noises update as it feels more immersive, but they want air swings (non-hit) audible". A partner's swing that hit nothing made no sound on the other PCs. Cause (bench, 11-12, two copies, the Tang sword): a player's swing sounds through its attack's ability, not its montage. The light attack's montage AM_Hun_Tang_A has no notifies at all; its ability's timeline (LegoAbility: AbilityTimelines, each step an action at a time) has the sounds: the shout SFX_Combat_VO_Hun_ChuZhao_02 at 0.253 s, the whoosh SFX_Combat_Hun_Common_Wave_Light at 0.273 s, cloth at 0.277 and 0.304 s, the blade's retract at 1.537 s (GAA_PlaySound_Opt_C steps; three moves name the weapon's own sound by a tag instead, GAA_PBZWeaponSound_C, through the weapon config's Sounds table). A partner's figure plays only their montages, never their abilities. Before, each copy's sound metered on its own: the host heard nothing at the guest's 12 air swings (28 of 500 random moments as high), the guest heard each of its own (0 of 500). The whoosh alone is quiet: posted 20 times on the host's own player it rose as high as 22 of 500 random moments did, the shout 0 of 500; what's heard of a swing is mostly the shout with it, so playing the weapon's steps alone (the first build: 17 posted for 12 swings) still measured as nothing (152 of 500). Now (hitfx.lua, the PartnerHitSounds row): each PC learns, from the game's data, the moves of the weapons a partner holds (W and E: the configs' Equip and Owning Abilities, each class's timelines or its parent's), each sound step filed under the montage last started before it, two timelines a step on the mod's timer (whole abilities took up to 6.7 ms in one tick; six timelines 2-4 ms). When the figure starts one of those montages (moves.playOn, as heard or held as in issue 16), each step is posted on the figure as the montage gets there, once a play: a re-tell or a part looping back (the sender's own position, now passed through moves.hold and playOn) plays nothing already played. Steps of the character, the body or the weapon, played whole; not loops or stops (StopOnEnd, _Loop, _Stop), menu, music or controller events (a deflect has a QTE menu sound, dodges shake the pad), steps at the player's place or a fixed one, the interact kind, or the surface and battle switch classes. The sword: 72 moves, 72 events. Not after a newer move, the move's end, the figure hidden or made anew; within 60 m, at most 30 a second a partner. No wire change: a partner on 1.7.1 or 1.7.2 is heard too. Not in PvP (the figure plays their abilities there). Bench after: the host heard the guest's 12 air swings (rise 0.010 at +0.49 s, 0 of 500 random moments as high; the guest's own +0.48 s), 77 posted, 0 dropped; both swinging at once, the host's own swing stayed as loud with the partner's sounds on as off (rise 0.019 against 0.017). A fresh agent's two reviews found, fixed: a weapon this PC hadn't loaded held up learning everyone's after it; a step played again when a move was told again or looped; one cap for all partners; a step could sound on a figure made anew; the cost a step; stop, loop and controller events let through; and a new tick of its own reordered the others' game calls (the two sim against 1.7.2: learning now runs on the mod's timer, kicked by W and E). Not covered: two abilities sharing a montage (the first read decides its sounds), a Phantom Edge's config (sim only), the figure's sword trail.
240. A player report on 1.7.1, three players online: "healing flasks randomly used more than 1 use. i.e. 5 uses to 1 use". A partner's sit at a bell rests this player (SIT), and the mod filled the gourd by hand to its base size (MaxTime). But the extra drinks a skill node or a talisman gives (the bag's MedicineExtraUsage) are counted into the gourd's own count: on the bench (11-12) two extra drinks over a gourd of 2 read 4 of 4, and the hand fill took it to 2. So a full gourd lost its extra drinks at every partner's sit, and the next drink seemed to cost several. A real sit fills it with the bag's own RestoreQuickItems (hooked on the bench: 2 to 4, no save written); a partner's sit now fills the gourd to its whole size as the game counts it (GetMedicineTotalMaxUsage, 4 there), never below what it holds. Not RestoreQuickItems itself: it fills the D-pad's top item too, which a partner's sit never did (a review of this fix: no co-op easier than before). Ruled out on the bench: a partner's drink (the host's gourd stayed put while the guest drank), a held drink key (one charge), every drink one charge. Bench on the fix (its first form, RestoreQuickItems): the host at 2 of 4 after two drinks, the guest sat at the bell for real, the host's gourd 4; sat again, still 4. Again on the final code: the host at 2 of 4, the guest's first ring at that bell (a scene, then the game's Bells tip: the rest, and so the sit, comes as the tip is closed), the host's gourd 4. Sim: four's bell sits (the fake's whole size counts the extra drink), 2 checks fail on the code before.
241. The same report: "popups for other players didn't work, only when you grab an item you still get all popups they got". A partner's note or quest item, new to this player, comes with the game's full-screen card. On the bench (11-12, 1.7.2's files) that card sat in the bag's display list (DisplayItemData) waiting for the game's own pickup, which, its animation through, calls TryToPushPickupUI and then ReadyToPlayConfirmUI. Meanwhile the bag said a card was up (IsDisplayPickupUI) with nothing on screen, so the line of the partner's next find waited too, for good: the guest's note and then their metal, nothing on the host; the two calls by hand put the note's card up, Space closed it, and the metal's line showed at once. (1.7.0's flush looked for a card in the line queue, where the real one isn't.) Now a waiting card goes up the game's way at a calm moment: no move of the player's own or health lost for 2 s, the game's own fight count (CharacterState.InCombat) at 0 and, on a guest (whose game counts no fight with the host's copies), no live enemy of the host's within 15 m (both let go after 2 minutes of waiting: the count can stick), no boss fight, scene, talk, game screen or pause; again every 5 s while it waits, 6 times at most, with a line in the log; lines alone go at once as before; not in a PvP match. Bench on the fix: the guest's note and metal, the note's card up on the host by itself within 7 s, Space, the line after it; again on the final code, the card up 2 s after the note's share. Sim: four's partner's-finds part (the fake now holds a new thing's card in the display list), 3 checks fail on the code before (out of a fight, after a scene, a guest beside a host enemy).
242. The same report: "story items still duplicate (twin flesh lilies for example, I have 2)". A story item is one of a kind, but each world has its own copy of its pickup: the taker's share comes by T and their word (I) takes the others' copies away by name. A copy the word doesn't find stays (made in that world under another name, made again in a later session, a pile the O word missed), and its taker then had two, their share giving everyone a second; the bag on joining topped a guest up to the host's count as well. Bench (11-12, 1.7.2's files): the guest took the Twin Flesh Lilies (I_01001), the host's game gave the host its own copy 20 s on: two each. Now a one-of-a-kind story item (the item table's NarrativeItem, but for the one made of several, the dragon token: 84 of 85) is held once: no share, bag catch-up or chest pay of one this player has, never more than one; the game giving one this player has already (seen in the bag hook, which runs before the game adds it) is kept to one and not sent; and the share of one that never came is given from our copy only if the word took it (with none taken, one handed in came back). Bench on the fix: the guest took the Hunyuan stone (I_01002), the host's own copy 20 s on was kept to one, one each. Sim: four's "a story item, once each" (three players, two, the bag on joining), 4 checks fail on the code before; the dragon token still comes twice, and a player without the item gets it from their copy when a partner's second take sent no share.
243. A review of 240-242 (18d340e), its finding, worse than 1.7.2: a player without a story item could have none for the whole session. A partner who has it already takes another copy (theirs made again in a later session, or the host's bag topping them up, which goes host to guest only, so a host can lack what a guest has): their game gives a second, game.storyAgain keeps them at one and sends no share, but their take's word still goes, and our copy goes with it. Loaded at the word, ours was given from once its wait was out; not loaded, the wait was dropped and the copy destroyed as it loaded; a pile's or chest's (Yuer's herbs lie in piles) went with no wait at all. Now a story item is given from our copy when that copy goes and its share never came: a ground copy not loaded at the word is named in its wait, owed when the wait is out (game.copyOwed) and given as it loads and goes (game.copyWent); a pile or chest a partner searched is waited for as a ground pickup is, at their word or as ours loads (game.storyCopyGoes; the O word now says who). Never to one who has had that story item in this save, held or given this game run (game.hadStory; the bag looked at every 5 s, game.watchGear): handed in since, it came back (and the copy loaded at the word gave it back before too). And game.storyAgain only while co-op is on: the bag hook stays for the game's run, and alone the bag is the game's. Sim: four's "a story item, once each": the host's copy loaded 25 s on and in the wait, a pile searched with the host's loaded and not loaded, a handed-in one staying so, one held from the save and handed in staying so, a share to one who has it, co-op off: 8 checks, 7 fail on 18d340e (all but the share to one who has it), 6 on 1.7.2 (whose share gave the host the thing, and the guest two).
244. The same review: with two partner cards waiting, the first put up and still open, both calls went again every few seconds over it, five times, then "still waits after 6 tries". Nothing the calm test reads saw the open card: the loot card is one of the screens that are always on, the game isn't paused with a partner, and reading isn't a move. What a second pair does over an open card isn't known, maybe a card with no button (90, 108). The bag says it: CanPushPickupUI is true while a card waits unseen and false from the first call till the last card waiting is closed, the game putting each next one up by itself after Space (the bench, 11-12). Now the pair goes only while it's true, again only if no card came up. (The player's own pickup, whose end puts the game's own card up, holds one too: moves.lua counts every moment of a montage of their own as a move.) And the calm test's two searches of the game's object list come last, only when all else is calm: a card can wait through a whole fight. Sim: four's partner's finds: two cards, the first open 15 s, nothing over it, the second up after it, then the line; a card waiting while the host is hit, plays a montage of their own or has a game screen up (checks the calm test lacked); a scene longer than the 12 s a share waits out one (in the 5 s scene the share itself waited, and the calm test was never asked), and a card a fight held when a scene began: of these 6, only the two cards fail on 18d340e (4 calls over the open one), all 6 on 1.7.2 (no card went up by itself).
245. The user's rule for the 1.7.1 report's point 2 (11-12), and their reading of it. The rule, over their answers: "Nothing, own sit only: you heal and refill only when you sit yourself, as in the game", "but a sit should make boss health go full i am scared people will abuse it", "NO, you heal back and the boss goes full why not?", then "yes both health and gourd should refill that what sitting always does in any souls game". Their reading of "5 uses to 1 use": the player drank 5 times and 1 use counted ("this could be because his friend sat on the bell where we refilled for everyone"). Both readings are one cause: online, the only thing that wrote a player's gourd was game.restHere on a partner's SIT. A gourd with no extra drinks was refilled at every friend's sit, so it gave more drinks than it holds (three players, bells sat often); one with extra drinks was cut to its base size (240, the bench case). Now, on a partner's sit: in a boss fight (their own, or a partner's they joined or struck: a helper too, a review of 11-12 found them left out, the boss full and nothing else) the fight starts over, the player rested (health, the gourd to its whole size as 240 fixed it, Revive's down count cleared as a sit clears it) and the boss back to full health, told as before ("you're rested, and so is the boss"); any other player who's up gets nothing, and no word; one who's down (Revive) stays down and keeps their down count (their pick, "Stay down"; a partner's sit is "partnersit" to the rest listeners now); the dead and those watching a fight still come to the sitter's bell. The "you're rested too" tips are gone (English, Chinese, Arabic). And any sit puts the bosses of fights back to full health, in the world that runs them (net.sitBossesToFull): a player's own; the ones they started, their bar gone with their death; on the host every partner's fight it runs, the sitter's own included, and one over with its boss alive; the copy of a partner's fight a player joined or struck. Reviews found these in turn: a guest's own fight on a boss the host's world runs (the copy set full alone was taken down by the next line: rested, the boss still at 30%), a party's host down and waiting in its own fight, a guest's copy far from the host, a fight over with the boss alive. Not with PvP on, nor in a match, as before. Local co-op unchanged. Sim: four's "sitting at a bell" and "a sit and the bosses of fights still on": out of a boss fight nobody's rested or told; in one, the boss full in both worlds and its players rested (the gourd 3: 2 and an extra) and told, the guests helping in a fight too (two players, a party, a party's host down); one who's down stays down, in a boss fight too; the dead still come to the bell; a party's host down, the boss far off, a fight over; PvP on unchanged. (two never sits: its bell is a Refresh Monster rest.)
246. A player report on online co-op (1.7.1/1.7.2): "The blue Memory Flower cannot be synced; only the first person to pick it up gets it which is an issue because it is served as a key progression material" ("it is an item on the ground to pick up"). A Memory Flower (BP_FlowInteract_MemoryFlower_C, one of the game's flow actors) plays a memory of about a minute and a half when picked; then, in one frame, the game vanishes it (FlowerDissapear, kept in the flower's own save data: it's destroyed as it loads later) and adds its petal with AddPropsByMap, notice on (the item table's ItemType MemoryFlowerPetal, 128 of them: six of Inanis' make her Memory Flower, "+10% HP"); as the petal's card closes its flow ends, raising the flower's quest flag (FlowerQuestTags) and beginning the flower's quest (FlowerQuest: XianYin_C, "Resonant Strings", on each of Inanis' flowers). The petal went to the others by T as any find does, but the game keeps a petal once a save: once in the bag, even taken back at once, it never takes that petal again, quietly or with its notice, before or after a load, and gives nothing for it (it still puts the petal's card up). grantLoot's way with a thing new to a player (held a moment quietly and taken back, so the game shows a line and not the card, then given, then GiveItem if the count hadn't moved) spent it: "Shared loot: F_02056 x1 (0 -> 0)", and the receiver's own copy then played its memory for nothing. The host's flow step for a pick reached guests by FLOW but hides no copy at a load; a guest's pick never reached the host's quest. Now a petal goes in once, as it comes, with its card (a note's way) or quietly in the bag on joining, never held first, and never to one whose bag has had it (its entry stays, at 0). A pick (the game's FlowerDissapear on this player's flower with a petal added in the same frame, both hooked) is told to everyone with the petal and the flower's flags (MF <flower> <petal> <flags>, a new word: 1.7.2 ignores it); the flags are raised there when it's calm. A partner's copy goes the game's way once that petal is in their bag, now or as it loads in that session (not one they're picking at that moment), unless it's a quest's flower: one naming no petal of its own (a story's, whose quest flow gives the petal; viewing some opens a quest, the story table's "后街-慈母记忆之花") or naming a quest (a collection's: its quest begins only with the flower's own flow, never with the flag, the bench). That copy stays for them to see: their view later runs its flow and begins their quest, and pays nothing twice (the petal's card shows a second time). Either way, host or guest. The vanish's hook goes on again when the flower's function is loaded anew: on the bench it was a new object after a trip to another map and after a reload. A fresh review of the first form (d7493d0) found the quest flowers, a vanish told as a pick with no petal behind it, the copy going before its petal came, and searches with no end: all changed as above. Sim: four's Memory Flowers part, 35 checks (the host's pick, a guest's first, a re-link, a story's flower, a collection's, a vanish with no petal, a guest down at the word, a copy that never loads, one loaded late, a guest mid-pick, three players, a partner's book page and whole flower, the bag on joining), 24 of them failing on 1.7.2's scripts; two against 1.7.2: every call and event the same, the logs differ by the table's one new line. Bench (two copies, 11-12, the Crimson Forest by bell LZSJ_010): on 1.7.2 the host's F_02056, the host's quest flower's F_02011 and the guest's F_02054 each came to the other as 0 -> 0, for good, and a petal held and taken back by hand stayed refused after a load. On the first form the host's pick of F_02056 and the guest's of F_02055 each came to the other 0 -> 1 with the copy gone and the flag up; a re-link gave nothing twice; after a reload both picked flowers were destroyed as they loaded in both worlds. On this code: the host's pick of F_02056, the guest's petal 0 -> 1 and its flag, its copy kept (Resonant Strings' flower), and the guest's own view of that copy afterwards ran its flow and began its quest (XianYin_C, branch 1) with one petal still; the guest's pick of F_02055 first, the same on the host, flag and all; the host's pick of a quest flow's flower, F_02011 0 -> 1 on the guest and its copy kept. Wider, at the user's word ("all the special items, not only the Memory Flowers"): the game keeps four item types once a save, not petals alone: add, take and add again gave 1/0/0 for a book's page named Page_, a whole Memory Flower (FA_), a book (Book_) and a petal, and 1/0/1 for K_, TU_, T_ and R_ items. The share's hold lost a partner's Page_ page, book or whole flower the same way (pages named R_, 192 of 218, never went through it). They go in once the same way now (game.keptOnce, game.giveOnce). A survey of the other special finds on the bench (Xigu and the Crimson Forest): the plain flow actors are things to read or investigate, two of Xigu's give a little dust (shared by C already); the visage tree opens a dream realm (the Human-Faced Banyan bell, a trial, flag rszx.榕树之心.开启任务1) where the Banyan Heart would come; the odd piles BP_Rubbish_A_C and BP_Rubbish_b_C are the pile class's children, so the pile share covers them; the hanging gift is a gameplay blocker; the scarecrows are teleports. A second review (11-12 evening), changed: such a find's card is marked as a note's is (game.tipsWanted), so it goes up when a note's would (with 241, at a calm moment); and a partner's new word is looked for at the next slow tick, not after an older word's 30 s (a look put off by another search in that frame keeps its turn). Not covered: a plain flower's copy (no quest) that never loads in the session of the pick stays (the late load is sim-only, and no plain flower was found on the bench); petals already lost to 1.7.x stay lost in that save, as the game refuses them.
247. Local co-op: player 2's weapon switch set the backpack's weapon in hand (`CurrentWeaponIndex`, `CurrentMainWeaponID`), which the save keeps. So the save, and player 1 after a reload, had whichever weapon player 2 last switched to (the bench, 11-11: player 2's switch to their second sword left the backpack on it while player 1 still held their first; the backpack's own swap and put-back then gave player 1 that pick). Now the backpack's weapon in hand goes back to player 1's a moment after player 2 switches, written as the backpack's own fields, which move nobody's hand (bench: written both ways, neither player's hand moved). Part of player 2's own gear (`loadout.lua`). Bench, on the built code: player 2 switched to their wooden sword, the backpack stayed on player 1's Tang.
248. Local co-op: pad 2's Options opened player 1's menu, not player 2's own (found with two pads on the bench, 11-11, testing 247's feature). The game's menu layer (CommonUI) answers a joystick's Options in the viewport's key call, before the game's input gets it, and as player 1's whichever joystick pressed it: the engine gives every joystick to the same Windows user. So the mod's relay never saw it (neither controller had Options down) and the menu opened shared, as before. main.dll now keeps player 2's Options from the game while the mod asks for it every frame (`pp_pad_grab` in `pad_route.hpp`, the same viewport call that routes player 2's sticks), and counts it; the mod then opens player 2's own menu. The press's release is kept too, a mod that stops asking gets Options back within a second, and with an older main.dll it's as before. Bench (two virtual pads, 11-11): pad 2's Options opened the menu with the PLAYER 2 badge, pad 2 picked a second main weapon there and B closed it, and player 2 held both while player 1 held their one; pad 1's Options opened player 1's own menu, and pad 2's inside it closed it as before.
249. Local co-op, player 2's own menu (247): after player 2 changed their outfit, both halves of the screen showed player 2 (the user's own pads, 11-11). The Appearance page moves player 1's camera to its preview point (`BP_AppearancePreviewPoint_C`) and, leaving, hands it to the body the controller points at, which in player 2's menu is player 2's. As their menu closes, player 1's camera now goes back on player 1's own body when it's on player 2's or on the preview point, then and for 2 s after; any other camera (a scene's) is left alone. Bench (11-11): reproduced (closed, player 1's camera on player 2), then with the fix "Player 1's camera back on them after player 2's menu" and each camera on its own player; player 1's outfit unchanged by player 2's pick.
250. Local co-op: player 2's health bar didn't go down when they were hit (the user's own pads, 11-11; since 1.6.0 gave player 2 the game's own HUD). The HUD's health reading (its `Get Health Percentage`) asks for the game's local character, player 1, whichever HUD asks, so player 2's bar showed player 1's health. Their own health now goes into their bar by hand (`ForSeq_UpdatePlayerHP`, as the mod's drinks already did): at each change of theirs, shown as a real change is, and again at each of player 1's and as it comes back up, the game writing player 1's there. Bench (11-11): player 2's health to 400 and their bar was at 47% at once; the game's write of player 1's full bar imitated and player 1's health changed, and theirs was back at 47%. Sim: four's HUD part, 4 checks (theirs held, a hit of theirs shown, player 1's hit, filled again).
251. Local co-op, player 2's Cross (the user, 11-11: "when pressing X it doesn't play the same way it would for player 1 or it would act delayed or more weird"). Two causes, both seen on the bench (11-12). The game swaps Cross from jump to climb with a button set of its own (`IMC_InteractionSpecialMove`, priority 1000) that its prompt puts on player 1's pad while a climb is offered; player 2 never had it, so their Cross jumped first and the mod's climb only took after the landing: at the same wall run player 1's run began 0.20 s after the press, player 2's after a hop at 0.82 s. And the mod chose climb spots itself, the nearest within 1.5 m of its box: Cross at the top of a waterfall ran player 2 up the wall at its foot, turned round, where player 1 jumps. Player 2's climb spot now passes the game's own rule, `IsValidInteract` (with their body's own block, sprint and air tags, as the game passes player 1's) and `CanTriggerInteract`, which gave both players the same answer at every spot tried (the wall run from below and from the ledge above, a side run, a ladder from in front and behind, a ledge); the prompt offers the nearer of a thing to use and a climb, one at a time as the game does for player 1 (at that wall run, the run over a rubbish pile 3.6 m off); and the game's own set goes on player 2's pad while a climb is offered (`IMC_Interaction` while a thing to use is), off again with the prompt, never copied to player 1. A press looks around afresh (the look-around's two-second memory had a wall run's foot out of reach after the ledge above it), and the retries and the draw-and-sheathe fallback run only while the game still offers the spot. Bench after: Cross on the ledge a plain jump; at the wall run, the run at 0.17 s, on top at 1.75 s (player 1: 0.20 s, 1.73 s). Sim: four's "player 2's Cross and Circle", 13 checks (the rule, the sets, the nearer thing, turned away, a menu, a swing).
252. Local co-op, player 2's Circle and what they may use: the facing half of the game's rule (`IsPlayerFaceToActor`) answers for player 1's screen whichever body it's given (bench, 11-12: player 2 at a rubbish pile, looking at it or away, always "no"; player 1 there, either way, "yes"; at the bell, player 1 facing it got "no" too), so chests, levers, piles, doors and Memory Flowers came and went for player 2 with player 1's view. Faced is player 2's own view now (60 degrees either side), with the game's `IsValidInteract` and `CanTriggerInteract`. While a thing to use is offered, Circle is the game's use button and not its execution, as for player 1 (`IMC_Interaction`). Bench: player 2 opened a pile with player 1 40 m off (dust +220), and rang the bell facing it, whose menu came up full width for both.
253. Local co-op: during a chapter's title (`WB_ChapterIntro_C`) or a tip (`WB_hud_tutorial_C`), pad 2's stick walked player 1 (bench, 11-12: 11 m in a title's 6.5 s, 2 m in a tip's 1.6 s). Any screen counted as a menu, and a menu gives both pads to player 1, but the game doesn't pause for these and plays on under them. A screen whose input is the game's (`InputConfig` Game, 2) and doesn't pause, or the tip (GameAndMenu, 1, no pause), isn't a menu now: the split stays and player 2 plays on with their own pad. A load still holds them both. Sim: the same part, 5 checks.
254. Local co-op, the user (11-11): "player 2 can execute every monster which is not how player 1 plays", then "I just walked near each one and pressed O ... each one was executed directly without me hitting them". Not reproduced (bench, 11-11 and 11-12). The game's execution rules are one set for every body (`PDA_GlobalMechanicConfig_C`): a stealth one needs the target not in combat, not invulnerable, and the executor behind it within 3 m. Walked up by pad along the game's own paths, a spear thug and a lone twin-hoe woman noticed player 2 by themselves within a second (awareness 0 to 100, Search, Combat, turned and fought); unhurt thugs fighting player 2 gave no execution on Circle from in front; the thugs by the farmer carry Stand.Invulnerable (no stealth) until player 1 comes. Since 252, Circle with a thing to use offered no longer executes. Ruled out after (bench, 11-12): changing player 2's look in their own menu (nothing left on either body, and an idle thug noticed player 2 within a second after it), the mod's own wake-ups for a lone player 2 (all 12 enemies it woke had their AI started, bosses too), the co-op health scaling (each enemy keeps its share of health), and the rule itself (a stealth execution is barred only for a dead executor). The one free kill found is a scripted sword thug 90 m from the start bell (spawner `Xigu_Malethug001`, 1 of 210 loaded) set never to start its AI: it stands still, and player 1 can take it from behind as well. The difference found runs the other way: the mod makes an idle enemy notice player 2 within 3 m on any side (since 1.4.2), and the game offers a stealth execution only inside about 3 m (none at 3.8 m), so player 2 can't take an awake enemy unseen from behind, which player 1 can (2.3 m behind an idle thug, unnoticed for 4 s). Changed at the user's word (262); the report itself is still open.
255. Local co-op: player 2 fought with the walking-about camera. The game counts player 1 in a fight (`CharacterState.InCombat`, a plain tag, one for each enemy fighting them; bench, 11-12: two thugs, 2; both given up 7 s after player 1 left, 0) and never player 2, though the same thugs hated both; a thug fighting player 2 alone gave player 1 nothing either. The tag is what the game camera's combat mode needs (`NBCameraComponent.CombatModeTag`), and it's read by the body's fighting stance (`PBZ_ABP_Hun_Base`), health coming back only out of a fight, the quest sense. Side by side in one fight, player 1's camera was `CameraType.Combat` with the game's soft focus on the thug (`WeakFocusAnchorActor`, `GE_WeakFocus_HasTarget`), player 2's `CameraType.ShoulderDefault` with none. Player 2 is now in a fight while a live enemy in combat has them on its hatred list, looked at once a second in noticePlayer2's pass on the enemies found that frame, and out when none has, down or out of play; the tag goes on through the camera's own requirement (a container made by hand with `MakeGameplayTagContainerFromTag` crashed UE4SS on the bench, and a first version reading the one-second enemy cache crashed it once after player 1 jumped 71 m, likely an enemy freed in between). Bench after: in a fight 0.1 s after the enemy came in, the camera `Combat` with the soft focus on it, the same as player 1's beside them; out 0.3 s after it gave up. Sim: four's "player 2 in a fight", 7 checks.
256. Local co-op: an enemy kept on player 2 while they were down, the whole 6 s until their body went (bench, 11-12: a spear thug, three special attacks on a body that can't die), and turned to player 1 only then. Down is out of the fight now, as a partner down online is (`game.ghostInFight`): off every enemy's list and not to be put back while down, and an enemy that was after them turns to player 1. Bench after: the thug was on player 1 1.6 s after player 2 went down, and back on player 2 after they were up. Sim: 5 checks in the same part.
257. Local co-op, player 2 down, the rest of 256: with them off the lists, a thug's own sight put the body lying there back on its list 2 s later and went back to it (bench, 11-12). Down, their body is the scenery's side now (`FactionComponent:SetFaction` to `Faction.Environment`: neutral to the enemies and to player 1, as a partner down online is hidden), and it's off every list again just before it goes, as they come back: a list kept a body gone for good. Bench after: a twin-hoe woman was on player 1 0.8 s after player 2 went down, only player 1 on her list until they were up. Sim: 1 check.
258. Local co-op: player 2 fighting alone put player 1, far off, in the fight too (bench, 11-12: a twin-hoe woman, then a spear thug and its rock thrower, fighting player 2 70-80 m from player 1 at the bell): player 1 on their lists, the game's in-combat tag on player 1 and their camera in its fight mode at the bell, and the twin-hoe woman turning to player 1 for 2 s mid-fight. Two causes. The mod's own wake-up of an area's enemies told every one of their scripts player 1 had come into the area; the awareness script now hears of player 2, who did come, and only the others (the invulnerability before a fight, `AIScript_PBZMisc`) of player 1, unless player 1 is in the area too, when the game's own way stands. And the game itself put "the player" back on the spear thug's list every few seconds; an enemy fighting player 2 with player 1 over 40 m off (beyond its eyes and ears) now has player 1 as neither side, its own override only (`SetFactionRelationOverride`), cleared as player 1 comes near, the fight ends, player 2 goes down or leaves. Bench after: player 2's 18 s fight with the spear thug and its rock thrower, player 1 never on a list, never in a fight. Sim: 4 checks.
259. Local co-op, player 2's own menu (247), the rest of it: pad 2's Select, tapped, opened the game's map as the shared menu, player 1's, and from its tabs (Character, Items) what was put on went on player 1 (bench, 11-12). Pad 2's Select reaches player 2's controller (unlike Options, 248), so the game's sense-only set (`IMC_OnlySense`, priority 100) goes over their others: the hold stays the quest sense, and its action, taking the button, keeps the map's tap (`IA_Map`, the HUD set's) from the game for them; the tap is the mod's, player 2's own menu opened on the map (`loadout.begin` then `IA_Map` on player 1's input), as their Options opens it. Not copied to player 1 (the controls watch). Bench: pad 2's tap, the map with the PLAYER 2 badge and its tip, closed by pad 2's B ("Player 2's menu closed"); pad 2's hold, the sense on both screens and no menu; pad 1's tap, player 1's own map, no badge. Sim: 3 checks.
260. A review of 251-259 (one reviewer, the diff only): no crash or certain bug; fixed what it found likely. An enemy leaving player 1 out of a far fight (258) is known by address and only those are put back (one neither side to player 1 of itself was cleared every pass), player 1 comes off its list at every pass while it fights player 2 (the game putting them back was left on if the first removal failed), and all are put back with player 2 down, out of play or on the boat, where noticePlayer2's pass doesn't run; player 2's in-combat state ends there too. Player 2's prompt sets (251) are looked at on a change and once a second, not every tick, and a set not found is looked for again in 10 s. A Select tap (259) opens no menu if player 2's own can't start (a shared one would put their choices on player 1) or while they're down, and a Select still held as a menu closes isn't a tap. The area wake-up (258) gathers an enemy's scripts before telling them, and falls back to the component's own event if none could be told. Bench after: player 2's far fight with the spear pair, player 1 never on a list; Select's tap, player 2's own map; Cross at the wall run, the run at 0.23 s. Sim: four 1479 ok (2 new).
261. Local co-op: no health bar over an enemy player 2 was fighting (bench, 11-12). An enemy's bar (its `hpbar` WidgetComponent, `WB_EnemyHPBar`) is drawn in its owner's half, and the game makes every one player 1's; and it puts the bar up (hidden-in-game off) only as player 1's blows land: a twin-hoe woman at 1540 of 1700 from player 2's light hits had none, at 1280 from player 1's hers was up. The bar now goes to whoever the enemy is after, player 2's half while it's after player 2, up for them once it's hurt; one we put up goes again when it's after them no more, and every bar is player 1's again with player 2 down, on the boat or out of play. Looked at in noticePlayer2's pass, on enemies found that frame. Bench: the bar over her in player 2's half and none in player 1's; back in player 1's half 14 s after player 2 left. A probe of mine that called into every widget component's widget crashed the copy (UE4SS, 03:46); the mod touches only an enemy's own bar. Sim: 5 checks.
262. Local co-op, the user's answer to 254 (11-12): "it should be able to sneak kill". The mod pointed player 2 out to an idle enemy within 3 m of it on any side (`noticePlayer2`, since 1.4.2, from before the enemies' awareness was told of player 2), and the game's sneak kill, never offered from in front, reaches about 3 m: player 2 could never take an awake enemy unseen, which player 1 can. Now the mod points player 2 out only in an enemy's face (within 3 m at once, within 10 m after 1 s, as before); beside and behind it, the enemy's own senses decide, as they do for player 1 (a Xigu thug's: sight 23 m within 80 degrees of where it looks, hearing 30 m, touch; both players on its awareness list). Bench (11-12): the mod no longer points player 2 out from behind; one spear thug, each player set down 2.6 m behind it in turn, noticed each by itself within 0.4 s; a sword thug noticed player 1 set down 2 m behind it at once, and offered player 1 2 m at its side the sneak kill, then noticed them within 2 s. Not seen on the bench: player 2 walking up to an awake enemy and taking it (a body set down near an enemy is heard landing, a twin-hoe woman turned to player 2 landing 14 m behind her, and pad 2 couldn't walk player 2 on the two spots tried behind a spear thug 80 m from player 1). Sim: four 1485 ok.

### A stable release: the boss that can't hit the guest, and what a guest's death lost (2026-11-15 and 16)

The user's goal for the next release, with the newest Nexus post in it ("my friend is host and i guest, the boss cant hit me") and their own word from play ("many things gets lost after the guest dies"). Looked for on two real copies with a probe that counts a boss's moves against the hits the player takes (each `A` heard and what the replay made of it, each drop of health).

263. A boss the guest fought and the host didn't stood asleep with its bar up (five players' reports since 1.6.6, never reproduced). Beside the host all was well: 37 of 37 of a boss's moves started as real attacks on the guest's copy, 15 hits in 25 s on a guest standing still. But the host's game wakes a boss for its own player alone, as they walk into its arena; from 45 m of them its optimizer takes its mesh's collision and slows it (a tick a second from 60 m, nothing out of the camera's sight); and it puts a camp's enemies away the moment its player leaves the camp's spawn volume, a guest's figure in there or not. A boss was never handed to the guest's game as a thug is, and for a guest's fight the host's game brought its own copy out and ran the fight from it. On the bench: the guest walks up to the elite of Xigu's first arena with the host 45 m behind at the bell, its bar comes up, 0 hits in 30 s; the host 90 m off, 0 in 60 s more, the boss gone from the host's world within 3 s; and with the host's body moved 70 m from its own fight, the guest's copy stood idle beside the guest for 55 s. Now the guest's game runs a boss the guest is fighting and the host isn't (`handover.bossGoes`: the guest's B and no fight of the host's with it), and one in no fight that the guest is clearly nearer, by the thugs' rule; it's the host's game's again once the host is in that fight (walked in by E or F3, the game's own bar on it there, or a blow of the host's from within 30 m: `handover.hostIn`), with the share of health it had left; and a guest's kill of a boss its game ran is taken though the host's copy is held at its floor. Bench after, same steps: the host 45 m behind, the fight begins in the guest's game and it's hit (4 hits in the 30 s it takes to begin, 72 in 50 s once on); the host 90 m off, 22 hits in 60 s; the host walks up and strikes it: "this game's own now", the boss turns on the host (101 hits in 60 s on a host standing still), at 92% in both worlds as before. Sim: a new part: 8 of its checks fail on the old scripts, the rest guard the new paths; three old checks said the old rule and are changed.
264. The host's respawn wasn't held for a guest the boss was going for. A guest is in the host's fight once they've walked in or landed a blow (`net.struck`), and the host down waits only for one who is: on the bench the host died as the boss swung at a guest who hadn't hit it yet, and was back in 2.3 s, the boss at full health in both worlds. A partner's boss going for this player (its `A` names them) has them in its fight now. With a blow of the guest's first, the host waited as it should and the boss fought on: 76 hits on the guest in 100 s. Sim: 2 checks.
265. Enemies that came back after a guest's own death were never given their moves again. A guest's copy has its AI stopped, so the moves the game hands an enemy as its AI goes along are given by the mod as the host names them, and that was done once per address and move (`movesLookedAt`), forgotten only at a partner's rest. The guest's own death refreshes its world: new actors, often where the old ones were, without those moves, for good; they swung and hit no one. Looked for now each time a move won't start: given if the copy hasn't got it (as a figure's moves always were). By reading: on the bench the three thugs watched came back on other addresses that time, all 17 of their moves started. No sim check: the stand-in's enemies refuse every ability.
266. What else a guest's own death kept of the old enemies by their addresses, from a read of every table kept so (a helper's audit, each line read again): who hit which and whom each was steered to (`game.forgetFocus`), which copy went with which of the host's (`net.matchSticky`: a late K could find the wrong one), what's an enemy and what's a boss (30 s of the old answers), which bar is only shown for a partner's fight (`mirroredAt`, cleared by a bar's own end alone: a later boss there, in the guest's own fight, was taken for a copy and no fight was told), three failed tries at a bar, and a boss brought out for a fight. All let go in that branch. And a fight shown there that isn't coming back at once (its player down and waiting) is let go too: kept, its B told again was dropped as shown already, and the fight had a bar there and no walls.
267. Boss super armour stayed off for the rest of a fight once the guest had died in it. Any pass without the partner's figure marks the fight as fought alone (as it should while they're down), and the mark only went with the fight. It goes now when the partner is in the fight again (their F 1: walked in, or a blow landed); one who only watches says none, and a fight begun alone stays as it began. Sim: 1 check.
268. One the guest's game runs was made tougher again on the host every pass. Its copy there takes the guest's most health, which read as a new body; the guard against that looked the copy up by the actor's name in a list kept by enemy ids, and never matched. The host's log said "1 enemy at 150% health" each second (164 times in one bench run) while the bar jumped between the two numbers. Known now by the hold on that copy (`net.heldAlive`). Bench after: twice in 20 minutes.
269. A guest took a new body on an old address for the one it had scaled. Only the host judged by health (a guest's copies take the host's); a guest had no test at all, so after its own respawn the new copies kept the base's posture, and one its game then ran for the host fought at the base's health. A guest goes by the most of its posture now, which nobody else writes. By reading. (On the bench the elite the guest's game ran stood at 12420 and went to 18630 as the host took it over: 12420 is its co-op number, 150% of 8280, and the second was the host scaling a copy that came to its world already the guest's to run, on top of the guest's numbers: 225%. A copy held for the guest's game is kept as scaled at what it has: 276.)
270. A guest's enemies went out of sight when the host stood outside their camp. The host answers "not in my world" (NOT) for a guest's enemy it hasn't got loaded, and the guest hides it; but the host's game has a camp's enemies out only while its own player is in the camp's spawn volume. Nothing is said now for one the host's world has alive in its records and put away (`game.sleepsHere`: its spawner still spawns, has nothing out, and this player isn't in its volume): it's the guest's own till the host is in there too. Not seen on the bench yet (the three by the arena were the guest's by handover each time).
271. Local co-op: player 2 had fewer gourd charges than player 1. Their own charges were as many as the gourd's own size (`MaxTime`), and the game counts a skill node's and a talisman's extra drinks on top (`GetMedicineTotalMaxUsage`), which a rest gives player 1: 2 against 3 on a gourd of 2 with one extra. One count for both now (`game.gourdMost`, which a partner's sit online uses too), and the charge handed back to the shared gourd is never over it. Sim: 1 check.
272. Local co-op was Hellwalker whatever `CoopDifficulty` said, Off too: the setting was read as the online roles link, and never for `Role = Local`. Read there as local co-op starts. Sim: 2 checks. (Taken back the same day at the user's word: 295.)
273. An enemy that changed hands could stand in its fight with its AI stopped. Found checking 263 on the bench: the guest's fight with the elite on, the host walked up, the game began the host's own fight with it ("this game's own now") and the mod handed it back in the same moment; the boss then stood beside the host, in combat by the game's word, its behaviour tree stopped, for as long as it was watched (40 s and more), while one run before it had fought (101 hits). The game runs a fight as one short tree after another, and a tree stopped while the copy followed the other game's can leave that chain waiting: started by hand (`RestartLogic`) it made one move and stopped again; the game's own `StopAI` and `StartAI` put it back to idle with its fight over, so not that. One that changed hands is looked at from then on, on both PCs, once a second: in a fight, its tree stopped, in no move (the game stops a tree itself for those) and in no scene, at two looks running, it's started again (`handover.stayAwake`). Bench after: the host joins, and in the 90 s after the first minute the boss made 37 moves, 112 hits on the guest it was after. Sim: 1 check.
274. A boss's items reached a guest twice (the sim, noted by the release session; "doubled" items in the Nexus posts on 1.6.x). The host's game pays the host for a boss's death and shares what it gave (`T`), as for any kill since 1.6.7; a boss's items were also told to the guest by name (`I loot:`), from the days before shares, on the host's own kill and on a guest's killing blow: one from each. Told by name now only when owed (the host down, its pay kept till it's up). Sim: 2 checks (guest 2 before, on either blow).
275. A boss's dust came twice to both players. The guest's copy of a boss dies the game's own death at the host's K, for its scene and its story, with its level put to 0 so that it pays nothing; on the bench (11-15) the copy paid the guest its dust all the same, on top of the share of the host's (`C`), and the guest's game then shared that rise back to the host as earnings. A copy's own pay is taken back now: a rise in the wallet within 5 s of the copy's death, up to what that copy rolls at its own level, once, and not shared on (`game.copyPay`). (Within 8 s of the last such death and up to what those copies roll all told, since 308.) Sim: 1 check, with the stand-in paying at level 0 as the game did (1400 each before). Bench after (11-16): the host kills the elite of Xigu, its roll 6600: the guest's copy paid the guest again 1.3 s after the death, taken back at once, and each player ended 6600 up, once.
276. A boss the host took back from the guest's game had 225% of its health. See 269: the host scaled a copy it held for the guest's game when that copy was new to its world (a boss its game brought out as the host walked up to the guest's fight), on top of the guest's own scaling, and kept that as it took the boss over: 12420 to 18630 on the bench, the guest's log saying it "healed on the host". By reading and the bench's numbers; the sim's case (a boss the host had scaled before it changed hands) held at one scaling before and after. Bench after (11-16): the guest's game runs the elite, the host joins and takes it back: 12420 at most on both PCs before and after, no "healed on the host".
277. With three or more players, enemies by a guest away from the host stood asleep, a boss a guest went in to first too. 104 and 263 are two players' fixes: a party has no handing over (its relay carries enemy lines from the host alone), and the host ran everything within 120 m of a guest, from a game that wakes enemies for its own player alone. Now the host's line says which ones sleep there with a guest by them (by two players' distances: a guest over 25 m from the host, the enemy within that guest's 60 m and 10 m nearer them; or in a guest's boss fight the host isn't in: `handover.asleepHere`, the line's boss field 2 or 3), and each guest's game runs its own copy meanwhile, neither held nor steered: it wakes there for the guest by it as the game means it to. Its health stays the host's world's (every guest's blows land there, and the line takes each copy down with it; a boss's copy in its own fight keeps its own count), a guest's kill is told (`GK`) and taken, a boss's too, and it's the host's to run again once the host is back with that guest (15 m) or in that fight. The cost: two guests by the same enemy, away from the host, see a copy each do its own moves. Sim: a new part, 28 checks. Bench (11-16, a party's host with PartySize = 4 and one guest, which runs the party's rules on two copies): the guest by the elite of Xigu, the host at the bell 45 m off. By the optimizer's own 45 m, tried first, the elite stood 44.9 m from the host, never woken and never called asleep: no bar, 0 blows at the guest in 40 s. By two players' distances: called asleep within a second of the guest's arrival, its fight began in the guest's game, 17 blows at the guest in 40 s; the guest's blows showed on the host's bar (0.96 on both); the host walked in, its own fight began ("this game's own now"), awake there again, 11511 of 12420 in both worlds, and from then the copy followed the host's (never over 2.6 m from its place in 20 s) while the boss fought the host. Three real players have still never been tried.
278. A player stuck in the Mind's Eye lesson (a player's report, 11-11: "during tutorial when game asks you to perform minds eye client get softlocked and cannot perform it"). The lesson (Xigu's BPF_XiGuMapGuide1) cuts the player's input down to the sense's own set (IMC_OnlySense), says "hold it" and waits for the sense to start; the sense (GA_Player_Sense) won't start while the game counts the player in a fight (CharacterState.InCombat, one for each enemy in combat with them), and online an enemy by them that a partner fights keeps them counted (the bench, 11-11: a guest with an enemy 2 m off, counted in, the key does nothing). Unable to move, unable to do the one thing asked. The cause, on the bench (11-16): the copies around a guest stand in fights with them in the guest's own game (their state goes to Combat as they notice the guest) which they never fight, their minds being the host's, and so never end: three enemies of the host's by the guest, a count of 3, and it stood at 3 still 60 m away. Now, online, while that set is on the player's input: the copies by them that stand in a fight are put back to idle the game's own way (`game.calmCopy`: StopAI, StartAI), and what's left of the count is taken off through the camera's own container of that tag, four times a second, till the lesson is done (`lesson.lua`). A guest's copies of the host's enemies; a host's only of those a guest's game runs. Bench, the host's enemy attacking beside the guest: the key did nothing; with the lesson's set put on the guest's input, the count went from 3 to 0 and the key started the sense in 0.15 s and 0.24 s (0.47 s for a host in no fight). The count alone taken off is not enough: where a copy's own AI held its fight it came straight back, eight times a look. Sim: a new part, 8 checks. The lessons that hold the input to the gourd, the map, a weapon change or the options (BPF_QuestStart_1-1, BPF_LianXie_Double, BPF_CanGangDeath) aren't known to be stopped by a fight and are left alone.
279. A guest down when a boss fell never had its win. Their copy's death runs in their world with their player down, none of the win's steps (235 found the same for a host); the host's own win, a scene that can mark hundreds of flow steps at once, was taken for a load by the flow sync (over 200 new at once: "taken as this world's state, not sent") and told to nobody, so that guest's story stopped at that boss, and a guest who wasn't there had the same. For 150 s after a boss falls in the host's world, up to 1000 new at once are its win and go out (`net.bossFellAt`, flowsync.lua's hostTick); the guest takes them once up and calm, as ever. Sim: a new part (the guest up again has the win's flag and all 300 steps; before, none), and 300 at once with no boss fallen are still a load. Bench (11-16, the combined build, steps made up on the host's flow list): 300 at once with no boss fallen: "taken as this world's state, not sent", none on the guest; the host kills the elite of Xigu, then 300 more: "302 new flow steps here, told the others", and the guest has all 300 a second on.

A level's own steps ("<level>-<actor>...") wait now for the guest to stand on that level, their own load of it over; a quest's or a scene's own (a bare id) are taken as before, and if the session ends first that level is the guest's to play as it comes. Rows that set an ending's condition are never followed. Sim: 1 check.
282. A story's own QTE was cut at 12 s on a guest. 1.6.6's watch ends a QTE that runs on (a boss's grab on a guest never let go: the copy doesn't run the ability's own ending), by the clock alone: 12 s, 30 in a scene, in a fight or not. One of the story's that waits on the player (the heart-pain input, the deaths in the well) was ended the same if they were slow. Out of any fight (no boss bar up here, none of the host's enemies within 30 m) it's given a minute. Sim: 2 checks.

With Role = Local the online loop doesn't run, and the ticks that set enemy health and posture (150%, 130%), a boss's own numbers, poise and armour, and how hard they hit hang on that loop: two players on one PC fought everything at the game's own numbers, where two online fight it tougher. Read from the ini at the local start as the online roles read it, and run in a loop of scaling.lua's own while player 2's Soul is in the world (`scaling.startLocal`; a boss's moves noted as a host notes them, so one a hit cut is taken up again under its armour). CoopEnemyHealth = 100 and the rest still turn each off. Sim: 3 checks. The sense's key is dead, the camera stays in its fight mode. A guest counted in one with no boss bar up, whom nothing of the host's has gone for (no move aimed at them) and who has lost no health for 8 s, has the copies the host steers that stand in a fight put back to idle, and a count that no copy holds taken off; never while an enemy the guest's own game runs stands in a fight by them (the first try took the count off as three such came for the guest: the bench). (A first bench watch, 11-16, read "after player 1 at every one of 339 looks", and said nothing: player 2's Soul kept being set down over a drop by that camp, fell and was down through most of it, off every list as a downed player is. The rule runs there now for the two bodies (`game.steerLocal`, from scaling.lua's local loop, once a second while both are up): whose blows an enemy took is told by who was swinging, a body in a montage of its own with no hit reaction on it, both swinging half each.
