(A guest set down behind it who does nothing isn't noticed for 40 s and more: the game's own senses, as for a player alone.)
290. Local co-op, a player report on 1.7.3 (keyboard on player 1, a pad on player 2, DS4Windows with HidHide), point 1: "When a tutorial prompt pops up without pausing the game, It causes player 1's input to be stuck running in one direction", and their notes: "when the game is paused and player 2 holds a movement button, once the game unpauses player 1 will be stuck moving the same direction forever ... this doesn't apply to the attack button or any other". A menu gives every pad to player 1, so both can drive it, and hands them back as it closes. The game hears a stick only as it moves, and for the moment between the game going on and the pads going back, player 2's held stick reached player 1, who kept that push for good (bench, 11-16, keyboard on player 1: player 1's stick read 1.00 after the close and still 1.00 ten seconds on, and player 1 ran with nobody touching anything; the next menu cleared it). Since 253 a tip isn't a menu, so 1.7.3's tip case no longer moves the pads at all. Now, once the pads have changed hands, what each player holds is let go: player 2's as a menu opens, both players' 0.15 s after they're shared out again, by the input library's `FlushPlayerInput`. A controller's own `FlushPressedKeys` can't be called from the mod (a TrivialObject in UE4SS), so the F4 panel's flush with a partner linked online (since 1.5.0) never did anything; it goes the same way now. Bench (11-16): five rounds, player 1's stick 0.00 after each close, player 2's 1.00 while held, player 1 never moved. Sim: four, 2 checks (both fail on 9a811b4's scripts).
291. The same report, points 4 and 5: "mapping a controller to player 1 and keyboard to player 2 doesn't work properly, elaborate on the DS4 guide" and "when using multiple PS5 controllers player 2 controls player 1's movements no matter which controller is used even with no keyboard". The keyboard always reaches the game's first player, and moving player 1 off it cuts them off from the game's menus and the F4 panel (the real-pad test, 10-31), so a pad on player 1 with the keyboard on player 2 isn't a layout the game can do. PlayStation pads reach the game three ways (XInput through DS4Windows; raw input, which main.dll drops; GameInput, which always lands on player 1 and can't be dropped): unless HidHide hides the real pads, both pads move player 1 (10-31). And 290's stuck stick looks the same from the couch: after a menu, player 1 ran on with player 2's last push. LOCAL_COOP.md and the Nexus page's English text now say which two layouts work and what to check when both pads move player 1. No code change; not tried on real PlayStation pads (the bench has virtual Xbox ones).
292. The same report, point 3: "When player 2 dies and respawn player 1 can't move until player 2 finishes their revive animation". The game's rise-up for a Soul back from down (`GA_PlayerRespawn`, its 4.6 s `Hun_Unarmed_Respawn_Montage`) turns "cancel the player controller" on in the input component of the player's body (`NBInputComponent.bCurrentCancelPlayerController`), player 1's, whichever Soul rises (bench, 11-16: on for player 1 from 0.9 s to 5.2 s into player 2's rise-up and off for player 2 throughout; player 1's controller flags and control sets as they were, and pad 1 moved player 1 0 cm in that time). While player 2 rises (7 s), player 1's input goes back to its own mode (`ResetToDefaultInputMode`) whenever that comes on, unless player 1 is in a move of their own. Bench: pad 1 moved player 1 in every try through player 2's rise-up, 90-110 cm each 0.25 s, and "held player 1 still: let go" once. Sim: four, 4 checks (the last two fail on 9a811b4's scripts).
293. The same report, points 2, 6, 7 and 8, tried on 1.7.4's code (1.7.3 had none of 250-253): "Player 2 needs to double press A/X to traverse a context sensitive path", "When player two presses A/X to traverse a context sensitive path it locks player 1 into looking in one direction", "Player 2 shares the same health HUD as Player 1 sometimes", "Player 2 sees huge prompts A/X when around ladders despite not being close causing them Awkwardly run to a far away ladder or wall to climb it". Bench (11-16, a pad each): one Cross at a wall run started the run 0.65-0.7 s in, and at a ladder 0.19-0.68 s in, with no jump first (251). Through both, with player 1 beside player 2 and 89 m off, pad 1's right stick turned player 1's view and camera all the way round, and 292's cancel stayed off: no lock. At 6 m and 3 m from the ladder player 2 had no prompt; at its foot a small one, the size of player 1's (251: a climb only where the game offers one). The health bar is 250's, its four sim checks passing. In 1.7.3, point 6 may have been point 1's stuck stick on the look stick (a tip moved the pads then). One difference seen: at that ladder's foot, with a rubbish pile 4.5 m off, player 2's prompt showed Circle where player 1's showed Cross, though player 2's Cross climbed; not looked into. Not tried: the mantle, side run, rope swing and rappel (no spot near enough on the bench to stand at).
294. Local co-op, player 2 back over a drop, again and again (found 11-16 in the stability session's bench log of the one build; the same code in 1.7.3). At a camp on a cliff top with the drop on player 1's right, every "Player 2 is back" put the new Soul 2 m to player 1's right at player 1's height, over the drop: about 1 s later "Player 2 fell out of the world: brought back to player 1", about 1 s after that "Player 2 is down" (850 to 1 at once, the landing 38 m down), back 6 s on, eleven rounds till player 1 moved. Three causes: the spawn (`main.lua`'s `P2_SPAWN_OFFSET`) and `besidePoint` put the Soul beside player 1 with no look at the floor; the fall rescue (`moveBeside`) put it back on the same spot; and `fallingSince` outlived a down and a new Soul, so the new Soul's first slip counted as a 6 s fall at once. Now the spot beside player 1 is the first of their right, left, behind and in front with floor from 1.5 m above their feet to 2.5 m below and a clear line to it at chest height (`LineTraceSingle` on the visibility channel, as the duel's start looks), else player 1's own spot; the spawn takes that spot; a body moved beside them is stopped (`StopMovementImmediately`), so a fall it was brought back from doesn't land it hard; and the fall timer starts again with each new Soul. A far move still holds the Soul in the air to player 1's right and looks for the floor once player 1 lands; where the game won't trace, the right as before. Bench (11-16, the same camp, player 1 at -27240 285350 facing -142.7: the right 38 m down, the left 17 cm): player 2 put where the old code did fell 39.5 m and went down; then four comebacks, each "No floor to the right: set down on the left.", stood 7 cm from player 1's height at 850 health with no fall or second down; at a flat spot the right as before. Sim: four, "player 2 set down where there's floor", 7 checks (6 fail on cc685d6's scripts).

Local co-op keeps the difficulty the player chose (the user, 11-16: "I think for local coop it is fine to keep it to the same difficulty of the user that he chose since it isn't same as multiplayer coop where everyone has different difficulty"). So 272 and 283 are taken back, and one thing older with them: up to 1.7.3 the game's own mode was raised to Hellwalker with two players on one PC as well, player 2's Soul counting as a partner. With Role = Local now: the mode stays the player's own whatever CoopDifficulty says (difficulty.lua counts no partner on one PC; a mode an older version still holds is put back 20 s on, as for anyone alone), and nothing of the co-op numbers is read or run (no 150% and 130%, no boss numbers, poise, super armour or damage settings). Online keeps all of it. 288, who an enemy goes for, is no difficulty and stays, in a loop of its own (`game.startLocalAim`). 271, player 2's gourd, stays. Its copy dies the game's own death at the host's K, which pays this player the copy's own dust all the same (275), taken back as it comes; but the dust watch looked at no rise while its player was down or just up, so the copy's own stayed, and the share of the host's came on top once they were up. On the bench (11-16, the twin-blade officer of Pangzhen, the guest killed and held in the fight, the host's real blow killing the boss): 280000 for the guest, 140000 for the host. 297. "The boss can't hit me", another cause: a copy's own cooldown. The game's abilities keep a cooldown of their own in their base class (`BP_AbilityBase_C`: `Cooldown Mark Time` on the move's instance, its length in `Cooldown Data`: 30 s on a boss's big moves), stamped as each copy starts the move for itself: a moment after the host's enemy did, over the link, and again whenever a cut move is taken up. The host's enemy uses a move as it comes off cooldown there, so by the copy's stamp it was a moment early: the copy refused it, and the move played as an animation that hits no one. With 1 ms between two copies on one PC it never showed (36 of 36 and 23 of 23 moves of the twin-blade officer started). On a link of 75 ms each way, 10 ms jitter and 5% loss, the mounted officer of Pangzhen's 7 s combo (`GA_GuiChai_Yellow_MoDao_Skill_03`) was refused on the guest's copy 6 times of 6, 34 of its 40 moves starting; read then, the copy's stamp was 24 s old of 30, and the host's own boss took the move from outside at one moment and not at another; the stamp cleared by hand, the copy started it. A copy that has a move and won't start it has its own stamp cleared now and is asked again (`game.clearCooldown`): when a move may be used is the host's enemy's to say. Bench after, same link, 125 s: 67 of 67 started, that combo 3 of 3, and 111 hits on the guest. Sim: a new part, 2 checks, 1 fails before.

The host joins (flies in and strikes): the host's game's again, the same health in both worlds, 91 hits on the guest in 75 s, 36 of 36 moves started. The guest dies there twice and comes back to the fight the host is still in (its world refreshed each time, its copy a new body): 78 hits in 75 s, 23 of 23 (265). A boss that stands for both is the game's own: the snow blade boss (`sp:FengXueDaoKe_Seq126`) waits for its scene, takes no damage and fights nobody, the host alone as well. 281: the guest's log reads "259 quest steps and 22 marks followed" where it read 23. 279: the gold-masked man's real win (3 flow steps, a bell's among them, and a flag) reached a guest standing 60 m off at the bell; no boss the bench's save can reach has a win of hundreds of steps, so the lifted cap is proven with made-up steps only. F3 takes you there.", the host's only "your partner is on another map."; the real F3 key on the guest, "Going to the other player, on PBZ_Pangzhen_WP.", and 45 s on it stands beside the host there.

301. A door one player opened stayed shut for the other. The levers' word (SWO) looked at the switch's `CurSwitchState` alone, and a door of the game's own kind (`BP_BlockBase_C` and what's made of it: single and double doors, block gates) never sets it: open is its own `DoorOpen`, by a player's opening and by the word's own call (`SwitchWithoutPlaySequence`) alike. So such a door was never told, and one opened here by word read shut, never named again by the host for a guest who came later ("late guest softlocked at puppet doors and Phantom Tavern", reports on 1.6.x; 1.6.9's bench found every call leaving CurSwitchState false and took the doors for unopened). On the bench (11-16, the key door of Xigu; the user, watching: "this door needs key so it won't open"): given its key (K_01003) the guest opened it the player's way with the real E key: DoorOpen true, CurSwitchState false, the key spent; the host stood 4 m off, no word came, its door shut. A switch is open by either now. Tried live first (the guest's isOpen swapped through its upvalue): told within 2 s, the host's door open. Then from a fresh start with the file, the other way round: the host opens it with its key and the real E key, "Opened ...: open for the others too.", and 2 s on the guest's door is open, "The other player opened ...: open here too.". The wire changed and the protocol didn't (the main session's review of the build, 11-16: six lenses, each finding checked by one or two more in the code and the sim; its numbers V1 to V13). 277's party line says "asleep" in the enemy line's boss field (2, and 3 for a boss): a 1.7.3 guest read 3 as "not a boss", brought a boss it had beaten back by the thugs' path, and its save's record went back to alive. And 279's whole win, up to 1000 flow steps, reached a 1.7.3 guest on another level, which marked that level's own steps done where it stood (280's harm; its guard is a new guest's alone). The user's rule: "If the wire format must change, bump the protocol version the way the code does". PROTOCOL is 5 (`native/relay.hpp`): 1.7.3 and this version don't link, and each is told both PCs need the same version. `relay_cli test`: 248 checks. 303. A boss held up by a floor of the game's own lost it as it changed hands (V1; 193's guard undone by 263 and 277). A boss of phases ends a phase at a floor the game sets (Health_Min), and its scene goes from there. Handed to the guest's game, the guest's copy had its floor let go, its blows took it to 0 instead of to its scene, and the GK that followed was its death on the host outside its script: no scene, the body left, the trip on never opened in the host's save. In a party the same for one asleep on the host, the guard skipped for any asleep boss. A boss with such a floor isn't handed over or called asleep now (`handover.guestRuns`, `handover.asleepHere`: the floor on a copy this game doesn't hold is the game's own), the guard holds for one asleep too, and letting go of a hold takes off the hold's own floor alone. The cost, as before 263 for these few: such a boss stays the host's, and its copy won't swing at a guest who went in first till the host is in the fight. Sim: 3 checks, all fail before.
304. A world loaded from the title was sent as a boss's win (V3). For 150 s after a boss falls the host sends what its world adds whole (279: up to 1000 at once, 200 otherwise, a load's sign). A host who went to the title within that time and loaded another save, still linked, sent that save's flags and flow steps as the win, and the guest marked them done for good. The window goes with the world (`net.bossFellAt` cleared in the load hook). Sim: 1 check, fails before (300 of 300 taken).
305. A guest's lost boss fight didn't start over (V4). The host's copy of a boss the guest's game ran (263), or of one asleep there in a party (277), kept the health the guest's game last said; the guest died, its world brought its boss back at full, and the host's line took the new copy down again: the next try began at 30%, easier than the game and to be had by dying. A partner's fight over with its boss alive, one this player isn't in (not walked in, no blow, no bar of this game's own), starts over now: the host takes the boss back if the guest's game ran it and puts it to full (`handover.lost`, from `handlers.F`). A fight the host is in goes on as it was; not with PvP on or in a match; in a party not while another's fight is on. Sim: 3 checks (two players, a party of three, the host in it), the first two fail before (2250 of 7500, 3000 of 10000). Bench (11-16, two copies): the guest took the elite of Xigu down and died, the host never in the fight: "it starts over at full health", 12420 of 12420 for the next try in both worlds. In a party of three it didn't, till 312 and 313.
306. A party guest's one-blow kill of an enemy asleep on the host was told to nobody (V6). An asleep copy isn't held, so a sneak kill or an execution was its death in that guest's world at once; GK goes only for one this player had hurt before (a blow that took health), so nothing went out, the host's stood at full, the other guests' copies stayed up, and the guest's world brought it back. An asleep copy's death here is this game's to tell from the first (`net.hitHere` as its line says asleep). Sim: 1 check, fails before. Bench (11-16, three copies): player 2 killed a thug of the camp 70 m from the host with one blow, never having hurt it: dead on the host at once and told to player 3, and the three wallets rose by the same.
307. Each ask about a put-away camp searched every object twice (V8). 270's `game.sleepsHere` looked for both kinds of spawn area for each enemy asked about: a guest in a camp of twelve with the host outside its volume made the host do 24 world searches in one frame, every 10 s, for as long as they stood so. The areas are looked up once a batch now, as the spawners are, and let go with it.
308. A copy's own dust, two ways wrong (V9, V10; 275 and 296). Taken back when no share could come: with the host down, or up within its quiet after, the host's dust watch shares nothing of what the game pays it, so a guest who killed one of the host's thugs then had its copy's own pay taken back and got nothing. When the host was last seen down goes with the copy's pay (`downSeenAt`, `hostDownAt`), and within that time it's kept, unshared; not for a kill another guest told. And one copy at a time: two dead within the pay's delay (one swing through two thugs, a boss and its last guard) overwrote the first, one pay was taken back, and the other went out as this player's earnings, both players paid over: on the bench of 11-16 three riders fell at once, 861 was taken back and 866 went to the host as a share. Each copy's roll is added to what's still to take back now, spent down as the pays come, within 8 s of the last such death (5 was short: a thug's copy paid 2.7 to 5.7 s after its death, the elite's 1.1 to 1.3). Sim: 2 checks, both fail before (900 each for 600; the guest 0 for 300). Bench (11-16): the host down, the guest's kill of a rider of Pangzhen: "kept: 869". A rider and its horse dead in one moment: 860 taken back, both wallets up 878. A copy's pay and the roll it's taken back by are two draws of the game's (103 rolled and 118 paid; 860 and 865; a horse rolls 0 and pays 5): some 15 apart at the most, whatever the size, and what's left goes round as a share, the same for everyone.
309. A party guest's own copy had its tree started again in the middle of its moves (V12; 273 and 277). The watch on one that changed hands starts a tree found stopped in a fight with no move playing, and knew a move by what this PC sends of that enemy: a party's guest sends nothing of one asleep on the host, so every move of its own copy longer than a second or two had its tree started again, a long attack cut or doubled. The copy's own montage is looked at as well (`handover.moving`). Sim: 2 checks, the first fails before.
310. Two boss fights at once (V13; as before 263, in this case). While the host fought any boss, another the guest fought 85 m off was neither handed over nor called asleep: it stood held and asleep till the host's fight was over. A boss is kept back for the host's fight only within its 60 m. Sim: 2 checks, the first fails before.
311. The review's small ones. Player 2's gourd refund timers handed a body that could be gone by then to the game (271: the number is worked out at the drink now, and only the number kept). A copy's grab whose grabber the host killed meanwhile held the guest a story's 60 s, not 12 (282: a fight seen at any look of that QTE counts). The party's asleep rule asked who each enemy by the host is after, a call into the game, before the cheap tests (277: asked last, of a candidate alone). The lesson watch ran through the quiet after a load (278, 284: it waits it out, as every other slow job), and called a library it hadn't checked. 288's loop takes its enemies afresh at each look (the review's V5 was 283's loop, gone since 295: this one kept no list across frames, but a list a second old can be the map before a load's). AUTHORITY.md says a boss going for a player has them in its fight (264). And 263's "12 checks that fail on the old scripts" was 8: the rest guard the new paths.
312. In a party a guest who wasn't by a boss was in its fight all the same (the bench of 11-16, three real copies; the review's gap G6). 277 has each guest's game run its own copy of one asleep on the host, and every guest with it loaded did. A copy let go with a partner's figure beside it goes into a fight within seconds, and the player it's after is its own game's. Player 2 fought the elite of Xigu alone, the host and player 3 at the bell 43 m off: 3 s on, player 3's copy was in a fight of the game's own with player 3 (its own bar, `F 1` told), and five minutes on it stood 15 m from them. So player 2, down, waited on player 3 to respawn (32 s, 51 s: as long as that copy's fight lasted, 15 minutes at the most); the host took player 3 for a second fighter, and the boss didn't start over (305); and player 3's bar showed its own untouched copy. A copy is this game's to run for a player who's by it (`handover.byHere`): within 30 m; or walked in (E at the wall, F3); or with the game's own bar on it for the player nearest it, their own fight begun by the game from further off. Theirs from then on within the game's own 45 m, and past them while its bar is up or it still fights here. Any other copy stays held and follows the host's, with the health the host's has left. Held isn't enough by itself: the game starts a stopped AI again by itself, and in that moment a held copy, roused by the fighter's figure or left in a fight's state from before, takes its own player on and the game puts its own bar up for it. So such a bar is made the mirror of the partner's fight again, or with no such fight on taken down (`game.mirrorBar`); a held copy in a fight's state is put back to idle outside a partner's fight (`game.calmCopy`); and a bar of the game's own on a copy this game doesn't run is no fight of that player's (`net.fightingHere`). Four rules were tried on the bench before this one held: "45 m with nobody nearer" at first sight, the own bar taken at its word, "no partner's fight told yet" (the watcher's bar came first), and calming through a partner's fight (the mirror went with it). The cost, as on the host: a watcher sees the boss stand while the fighter's figure fights. Sim: a new part, 16 checks, 7 fail before. Bench after, three copies, the watcher 42 m off through seven of player 2's fights, the sixth with blows for 25 s: its copy held and still, a mirror bar, its health the host's, never told as in the fight; player 2 back within 11 s of each death, the host's "it starts over at full health" each time; and player 2's kill: 12050 to 18650 in the three wallets. Still so, and known: two guests in one such fight each see their own blows alone on their bar, the boss falling when the blows of both add up to its health.
313. A copy went back up with the host's only from 5% under it (the bench of 11-16, with 305 and 312). The 5% keeps a copy that's a blow ahead of the host's from being put back before the blow is heard there. A fight lost after a blow or two starts over on the host at full, and each guest's copy stood 3% down for good: 16033 of 16560 for the next try, the host's whole, and the fighter's new copy too, taken down by the host's last line before the fight was told over. One the host has at full is followed up from any difference now, a second on as before; not while a killing blow of this player's waits for its answer. Sim: 2 checks, the party's fails before (9700 of 10000). Bench, three copies: "healed on the host (15567 -> 16560): following" on both guests a second after the host's "starts over", and 16560 of 16560 in the three worlds for the next try.
314. Nobody had a boss's dust when a guest killed a boss its own game ran (the bench of 11-16; the review's gap G4). Such a boss is the guest's game's since 263 (in a party since 277), and its death there is the game's own: it pays that player, who shares it, and the host's copy dies at the guest's word (`GK`), its own pay taken back (275). But the host's `K` came back while the boss was still falling, at 0 and not yet dead by the game's word, and the guest's game ran its death a second time: the kill's pay, this player's earnings, was taken back as a copy's own there too. On the bench, three copies, 6600 was taken back on the killer's PC and on the host's, and the three wallets stood as they were. A death this game told itself is dead here already (`net.guestKillSent` says whose word it was). Sim: a new part, 3 checks, all fail before (0 for everyone); the stand-in's second death pays nothing, as the game's did. Bench after: two copies, the guest in first and the host at the bell: "it's dead here already", the host's copy's 6600 taken back and the guest's 6600 shared, 5450 to 12050 in both wallets; three copies, player 2's kill of one asleep on the host: 5450 to 12050 in the three.
315. Dust came by itself, 330 to everyone every 20 s (the bench of 11-16, three copies, ten minutes: some 9000 each). A guest's world brings back an enemy the host has and it hasn't (1.6.x), within 120 m of that player; a copy is paired with the host's within 60 m. In a party the host's lines reach every guest, so a guest had the camp another guest stood by, 95 m off, missing for good and asked for every 10 s; and there each one brought back was dead a few seconds on and paid that player, who shared it. What took them isn't known: tried again on the bench three ways (the camp put away, its thug killed quietly, the host's world refreshed), each one brought back lived. Brought back within this player's own 60 m only now, where a copy is paired and held from its first turn, and asked for again twice as long after each time, up to 5 minutes. Sim: a new part, 3 checks, all fail before (asked for 4 times in 35 s, 10 times in a minute).
316. 297's clear left a move with no cooldown, or started one over (the main session's second review, 11-16: five lenses, each finding checked by one or two more; its numbers V1 and V10). A refused start of a move the body has cleared the move's stamp and tried once more; refused again for another reason (a block, a break, the move still running), the stamp stayed cleared, and the copy's own AI, once it ran it again, had a 30 s move at once. A move still running was cleared too: told again in its middle (a relink's S, a flinch let go in its last moments), the game started it over from its beginning. And the same runs for the boss this PC runs itself as a cut move is taken up (`puppets.resumeHost`, with CoopBossArmor), which 297 never said: the host's own boss could have its big move again early. A move running is left alone, the stamp is put back when the second try is refused too, and the move's list is walked once, not twice (`game.clearCooldown` says whether the body has the move: `hasAbility` is gone, and pbz.lua has a local to spare again). Sim: 2 checks, both fail before.
317. Local co-op's steering kept its enemies (the second review's V2; 288, 295). One steered between the two players was let go only by its next turn, and it has none once it's dead or both players are 25 m from it: its body and target picker stayed on the mod's list till player 2 was next down, past a load too (the user's rule: no game object kept across frames), its picker's wait stayed ours, and a later enemy at its address took what the dead one was hit for. Let go at the next look now, as online's turns do at a death and with nobody near, and before every map load. Sim: 1 check, fails before.
318. F3 to a host on another map from a joiner's watch left the watch on (the second review's V3; 299 made it the default path, and the tip invites it). A guest who links while the host fights a boss on another map watches that fight; F3 took them there, into the arena, still told as watching: `F 0` whatever they struck, so the host's death wasn't held for them, and the boss kept one player's numbers. The watch ends with the trip, as F3 into a fight on this map ends it. No sim part reaches the trip (the fake game has none); the review's own stand-in did, and passes with this fix.
319. Doors, two things from the second review (V4 and D2; 301). A door this world's own game shuts again while it's loaded (a fight's door, a scene's, if any does that: none seen yet) was opened again by the host's word every 20 s. A door seen open at one look and shut at the next is left to the game, till it's seen open again; one that comes back into reach shut is still opened by word, as is one last seen open before a load. Doors only: a lever is as it was. And a door the word opens has the flags its own flow raises raised with it (its presets' AddTagWhenSuccess, as a partner's chest's are, OF): the word's call isn't the player's opening, and a quest's door a partner opened first would stand open without them. Sim: 2 checks, the first fails before. Not on the bench: no door of either kind has been found.
320. A copy's dust with the host down, the late pay (the second review's V5, on 308). 308 keeps a copy's own pay when the host was down as the kill was told; that was read once, at the K, so a thug's copy paying 5.7 s later was taken back all the same, the host down all along. The host seen down again while such a pay waits moves that time on.
321. A drink drew no enemy on one PC (the second review's V7; 288 lists a drink among its rule's parts). Online a drink is told by the link; on one PC nothing told it. Told from local co-op's own drink counter now, for whoever drank.
322. The player guides, wrong since 295 and 299 (the second review's V8, V9, G8). INSTALL.md and its Chinese copy said a partner "online or on this PC" raises the game to Hellwalker with tougher enemies: online only, and a line says local co-op keeps the difficulty you chose and the game's own numbers (the user's rule). They said F3 needs TP > Partner on: with it off, F3 still takes a guest to a host on another map. COOP_GUIDE.md listed doors and levers as each world's own. Left for the changelog the user approves: 1.7.3's local co-op players are no longer raised to Hellwalker. Not done: the party's "on another map" tip doesn't name F3 (its translations go by the whole line).
323. The F4 panel's Apply stayed grey for two players already in split screen (a report on 1.7.1, for the split layout and two monitors: "i select the option but the apply button is greyed out"; only the ini worked for them). Apply waits for each player's device, and a joystick card counts as there only when Windows shows that pad as an Xbox pad (XInput: main.dll's pads.txt). Pads the game reads itself aren't in that list, so with players on such pads nothing in the panel could be applied, a change of layout or monitors included, and grey said nothing of why. With the players and their devices as they already are, Apply is ready whatever the pads read. Grey otherwise, and a click on it says which player it waits for and what for (English and Chinese; in Arabic the line stays English till the user has checked one). docs/LOCAL_COOP.md says the same. Sim: `panelApply`, 7 checks, 3 fail before. Not on real pads: the bench has none.
324. "i cant find the dwmapi.dll file" (Nexus, 16 Nov). The file is UE4SS's ("UE4SS for Phantom Blade Zero" puts it in `platform\steam`), not this mod's, and an antivirus can take it. INSTALL.md and its Chinese copy say so under Troubleshooting.
325. A switch's `DoorOpen` was asked of every shared switch at every look (1.5 s), levers, lifts and cages included, which have none (301 added the read). A name a class hasn't got has UE4SS search all of the class's fields and functions, and one such read took the game down inside UE4SS, where no pcall catches it: the bench, the night of 11-16, a census probe reading `DoorOpen` on every switch 5.6 s after a map's load, on a rubbish pile still streaming in (the dump's Lua frames and the object on its stack say so; the other copy read the same pile safely 40 s on). The mod's own look waits 5 s after a load and reads shared switches only, but it asked them the same question for as long as they stood around. Asked of a class till it's known now: a door's at its first read, no door's after three looks a second apart; then only doors are read. Sim: `doorsOfTheGames`, 3 more checks, 1 fails before (a lever read 26 times in 40 s, now 3). The mod's own job was never seen to crash so: none in some 30 trips that night.
326. Seen at last, and fixed by 327: "for some bosses, after the host enters, the guest can't deal damage" (the reports of 11-06 and 11-09). The bench, 11-17, the first story boss (Peng Meng, row 25), both worlds at row 24. With both players inside the story's trigger box as it fires, each world plays the scene and starts its own boss, and all is well: the guest alone by him took 21 hits and dealt 8, all applied, the health the same on both copies; his own health lock keeps the last point for the host's finisher, as designed. With the guest outside the box as the host walks in (9 m off, joining afterwards by F3), the guest's world takes the host's flow steps as done, its own trigger box goes with them, and its copy of the boss is never started: AI state None and team 1, where a started one is in Combat on team 0. That copy follows the host's moves and health, and nothing the guest does hurts it: none of some 300 swings and 63 attacks by the game's own input landed, while the host's own did. Ruled out: its collision, the plain fields of the actor, its controller, AI and other components (the same on both copies), its abilities, its effects, and its team or AI state alone (set by hand: no change), and its spawner's `ClaimSeqSpawn(false, true, true)`: with the figure there it does nothing, and with the figure removed it brings a new one as unstarted as the first. What starts one is in the flow steps the scene runs (a bare id and six of the level's own, and a flag), which the guest's world was told as done and never ran: with the guest's world not taking them (its following switched off by hand), its box stayed, the guest walked into it mid-fight, its scene played, its boss was started, and its hits were applied. How to see it again: `notes/stable/bench/steps-bl1.txt`, then the host put into the TriggerBox at -50452 260352 -24594.
327. The fix of 326: a boss's start is each world's own to run. What the host tells from the start of a scene of its own till 15 s after it waits apart in a guest's world (flowsync.lua's `holdStarts`; the host tells a step up to two looks after it ran). If a boss fight of the host's began in that time, it is that boss's start, and stays out of the guest's world for as long as the boss lives in the host's and the guest's copy of it isn't started (its AI's state reads None; no copy there counts as none started). The guest's own trigger box stays, and a guest who goes in has their own scene and their own start of the boss (F3 lands them 0.8 m outside the box at Peng Meng's arena: the first step in). Taken as before once the boss is beaten in the host's world or started in the guest's; with no fight after the scene, as the 15 s end. The bench, 11-17: a guest 10 m off as the host walked in kept its box through the host's scene and fight ("8 of the host's flags and flow steps are the start of its fight with sp:PengMeng_Seq014 ... left for this world's own start of it"), joined by F3, took a step, watched its own scene, and then alone by the boss took 13 hits and dealt 19 that were applied, the health the same on both copies. Sim: `bossStartsHere`, 9 checks, 6 fail before. What it costs: a late guest sees the boss's intro as they go in, mid-fight; and what the host's flow does during a scene of its own reaches a guest 15 s after that scene, not during it. And whatever the host tells from a scene of its own until 15 s after it, with a boss fight of the host's begun in that time and that boss not started here (one met on approach too, when this player has no copy of it near), waits until that boss is beaten there or started here, and is lost for this session if it ends first (the fourth review's V9; what already waited for a calm moment as the scene began is held with it: left so, see 348). Not seen yet: a boss of phases whose later phase starts by a scene; a guest who leaves before the boss is beaten keeps their own trigger, as if they had never met it.
328. A watcher's stray bar was told as a fight of their own (the main session's third review, 11-17: six lenses, each finding checked by one or two more; its numbers V1 to V15 and D1; this is V1, high, on 312). The game puts its own bar on a party watcher's held copy for a moment as a fighter steps in; `announceBoss` told it (B) before `byHere` took the bar down, and nothing ever ended that fight: the host filed it as the watcher's, the fighter's own was never told, the walls stayed shut for a fight nobody was in, and the fighter's lost fight never started over: the next try began where the last ended (305's harm again; seen in the three-copy bench's own log). A party guest's held copy of one asleep on the host tells no fight; it is told as `handover.byHere` makes the copy this game's. Sim: 3 checks in `aPartysBystander`, all fail before.
329. A watcher was put in a partner's fight once that fight had moved away from where the boss sleeps on the host (the third review's V2, high, on 312): "nearest" is measured to the held copy, which stays at that spot, so the watcher 43 m off was the nearest to theirs. Nearest is for the race it was made for, no partner's fight told yet; with one told, a stray bar is that fight's mirror again whoever is nearest. Sim: 1 check, fails before.
330. The user's rule, "check every game-returned object before calling methods on it", on 290's and 294's new calls (the main session's review of the next build, N5). The F4 panel's flush with a partner linked (`panel.lua`'s blockPlay) called the Enhanced Input library straight off `StaticFindObject`, and 294's `besidePoint` and `moveBeside` called the body's capsule and movement components unchecked. All were inside `pcall`, so nothing crashed: the rule more than a likely fault. Each is now looked at with `valid` first, the panel's controller too; nothing else changes. The player reply now says the fixes are untested with PlayStation pads through DS4Windows and HidHide, the reporter's setup, and asks them to report back (G12). Sim: lint, events, four, pvp, two and compile pass as before.
331. 312's nearest rule took a guest's own fight begun from 30 to 45 m whenever a partner in no fight stood a little nearer (the third review's V3). A partner counts as nearer only beside the boss (within 30 m) or in a fight with it. Sim: 1 check, fails before. Taken back (346): counted only beside it or in a fight, the one about to fight, 35 m off and not told yet, didn't count, and a watcher's stray bar made the fight the watcher's.
332. A door or lever a guest's world opened by itself opened the host's copy (the third review's D1; its verifiers weren't sure the game does it): 301 tells any shared switch that turns open near a player, a scene's, a quest step's or a fight's end among them, and since 319 the word also writes that switch's flow flags into the host's save. The host's world decides the story. A guest tells only an opening of its own player's doing, one within 20 s of their walk up to a switch (`FinishMoveToAsync`, as a press begins); the host tells any. What a guest no longer tells: a door pushed open with no press, a gate that opens more than 20 s after its lever, a gate a guest-run fight's end opens. Sim: 2 checks in `doorsOfTheGames`, fail before; the guests' openings in four walk up first now. The bench, 11-17, two copies on this build: the guest opens Xigu's key door with the real E key, "Opened ...: open for the others too" 2 s after the press, and the host's door opens. Since 347 a press seen late is told too (counted from the last look that saw the switch shut), and only a walk up to a switch the mod shares counts, not a pile's, a chest's or a bell's. Since 355 a press counts from the last look at them all with a partner on the map, not from each switch's own; its two costs are named there.
333. FollowHostStory = Off, and the host's door and lever flags all the same (the third review's V5, on 319): every naming, 20 s apart, opened a story's switch in that guest's world and raised its flow flags there, though the ini says their story stays their own. A switch with flags of its own flow is left to such a guest to open themselves; a plain door still opens by word. Sim: 3 checks, 1 fails before (one is 319's own: opened by word, its flags are raised).
334. A copy's pay with the host down was kept or taken back as one pool on one stamp (the third review's V4, on 308 and 320): mixed with another kill within the pay's delay, a boss and its guard or another guest's kill, a guest was paid twice or lost dust. Each kill told with the host down keeps its own roll (`keep`), and the rest of the pay is taken back as ever. And a kill in the host's first second down counts as one with the host down (V7: the host at 0 is down for this from its first zero read, not a second on). Sim: four's dust checks unchanged; the mixed orders have no check of four's (its fake pays a copy as it dies, with no delay): the review's own six cases pass.
335. A 1.7.3 guest at a new host's door was never told it's the version (the third review's V6, V8, V9, on 302): the new host's relay sent nothing back, and the guest was told to check the address. A host answers another protocol's knock with a header of its own, once a second at most. On Steam, where a guest hears every friend in the game, another protocol's word counts only from the other role (a guest's from a host, a host's from a guest). And a party host is told a friend on another protocol can't connect, not that they're in the game and out of step. relay_cli: 6 checks. Still so: a guest of this version at a 1.7.3 host hears nothing back (that host is told).
336. Three small ones of the third review. V10: `byHere` put a partner's bar back at every turn, with none of keepBossBar's guards: once a second at most now, and not in the quiet after a load. V11: it walked every boss bar for every copy asleep on the host at each turn: a table's look first. V12: 317 let go of steered enemies only, and one player 2 fought alone kept its damage record past its death, for a later enemy at its address: let go by the record.
337. The player guides and the versions (the third review's G1 and V15). INSTALL.md, README.md, their Chinese copies and the Nexus page's draft still said 1.4.1 or older can't connect to 1.5.0: this version has a new connection, and 1.7.3 or older can't connect to it. Updating: close the game first (while it runs the old main.dll stays in place under the new files). A party's watcher is told why the enemy stands still. README.md says the co-op difficulty is for online play; LOCAL_COOP.md and AGGRO.md say whom an enemy goes for on one PC. For the changelog the user approves: both PCs must update, 1.7.3 and older can't connect.
338. What was left from the third review, and what became of it. V13 and V14 (checks missing): written, 339. G9, the first review's N8 to N11: 339 (N9, N10) and 341 (N8, N11). G1's other half (new scripts over an old main.dll): 342. G2 (two copies online on the build): the runs below. G7, the frame cost, measured 11-17: the combined build against `claude/online-1.7.4` at the same spot from the same saves, two copies on one PC by the elite of Xigu's camp, the mod's ticks, frame ticks and link calls in ms a second: idle 31 on the host and 44 on the guest (the build before: 31 and 63), the guest fighting alone 42 and 60 (41 and 49), both fighting 36 and 67 (45 and 67), 50 to 60 frames a second on both builds: no dearer. G8, local co-op in the game on the combined build: player 2's drink is told to the rule (their own gourd press: told at each of three drinks), and a bell trip with three enemies steered leaves none steered, no error, the records after it the new map's own; the enemy's turn to the one who drank is the sim's (in the game it left player 1 once, 3.7 s after the drink, for a target the probe didn't name), and 311's refund through F4's single player wasn't run (the panel wants clicks).
339. The checks three reviews found missing, written (the third review's V13, V14, G4 and G8; the first's N9 and N10). Twelve rules could be taken out with every check passing: 303's floor kept as a hold is let go (the host's own fight begun, a guest's S) and under a guest's GK on one asleep on the host; 310's party half; 320's late pay with the host down; 321's drink on one PC; 312's calm of a watcher's copy, once, less and less often, never through a partner's fight; a party guest's thug kited past 45 m while it still fights them; a load with enemies steered (317); 268's "scaled once" for a body new to the host (the old check passed with the rule gone: its body was scaled before it changed hands); lesson.lua's aimed-at guard (a guest who guards every blow loses no health). Each is checked now, in the part that owns it, and each check fails on a copy of the scripts with its rule taken out (12 of 12) and passes on the build. The fake's enemies have an AI state where a check gives them one, and its quests can be over. No change to the mod.
340. A story step the guest's world can't take any more was applied at every load, and the guest told "your story caught up with the host's: 1 step" each time. Found on the bench (11-17, the story-boss sweep): "meet Xiong Zhang again" (row 425) on a guest whose quest was complete with its side branch S1 still under way, the flag that ends the quest having come by the story flow before the table's row. The game changes no branch of a complete quest (by hand on the bench: begun and finished, S1 stood at 1; on a quest still open both calls take at once), so the row never took: applied at eight loads in a row, eight notices, nothing paid twice. A row whose every missing branch is a complete quest's, by the game's own `IsQuestComplete`, is left alone now (the done-set told to the host is as it was); and a step that didn't take is no longer counted in what the player is told they caught up to. Sim: 4 checks, 3 failing before (the fourth, a stuck step taken after a load, as before); the fake's `FinishQuestBranch` took any branch, a complete quest's too.
341. Tests that pin 285 and 286, and NETCODE.md's party table (the first review's N11 and N8). relay_cli: a hit with its flinch, 250 bytes, has its copies: with the limit back at 200 that check alone fails (254 of 255). all.ps1: three players at 120 FPS, the host's packets a second under 376: 365 now, 387 with a held place going by itself again. The table's eight rows run again (netsim, five minutes each, against 1.7.3's native): above 60 FPS three and four players' hosts now upload less than on 1.7.3 (3 at 120: 416 -> 374 packets a second, 46.9 -> 46.2 KB/s), which the old rows hid.
342. New scripts over another version's main.dll (the third review's G1, its other half). A game left open while the zip is extracted keeps its old main.dll loaded; the new scripts beside it linked through it with 1.7.3's players, in protocol 4: the asleep word unread, a boss's win taken for a thug's (302's harm), and nothing said. The scripts ask main.dll its protocol as they start (`pp_protocol`; 1.7.3's and older have no such call) and leave online co-op off with another's, saying why on screen and in the F5 panel (the panel's own line only since 356: till then a 4 s tip, once a minute): close the game, extract the zip again. The dll's other calls, and local co-op, are as they were. main.lua's `PROTOCOL` and relay.hpp's go together: eventcheck.py fails if they differ. INSTALL.md and its Chinese copy name the message. Sim: a guest's scripts over protocol 4's main.dll, and over 1.7.3's: no link, nothing sent, told once; the host's install says nothing of it.
343. A boss not started in a guest's world took no blow of theirs and landed none ("for some bosses, after the host enters, the guest can't deal damage", "my friend is host and i guest, the boss cant hit me": the reports since 1.6.6; 326 and 327 were one case of it). A boss the game hasn't started is a figure on the player's own side (`Faction.Player`, its controller's team 1) till its own start in that world makes it an enemy. A guest's copy of one the host is fighting follows that fight's moves and health and stays a figure. 327 leaves the start to the guest's own world, which works where the start is a trigger they can still walk into. On the bench, 11-17, A-Hua of Xigu: her start had gone with a flow step the host ran on its way in, told before any scene (327 holds what's told in the host's scene), and the late guest's copy stayed a figure whatever they did: in 25 s none of their blows landed and none of hers. So a guest's copy of a boss a partner is fighting, not started here, is put on the enemy's side for as long as that fight lasts, and back as it stood if the fight ends with it alive and still not started: its own start is still this world's to run. One the game has started is never touched; the host's own copy never (its game starts and runs it). The bench, the same day: by hand first (6 of the guest's blows landed in 25 s and 10 of hers; the guest's killing blow ended it in both worlds, 9000 dust each), then the file: A-Hua (the boss's blows landing on the late guest at once; their own scene came too this time, both start steps held, and after it 20 of their blows in 25 s), the same health on both copies. Chang Hong (16 landed, 12 taken) and the sword puppet of the main camp (27, 4) were fought late in the same file, but each copy had been started in the guest's world already: Chang Hong's a boss beaten there and spawned again for the fight, the puppet's started by the guest's own arrival. Nothing was switched there: they show the late fight with this in, not the switch (the fifth review's V8). The switch on other bosses: the sweep below, and Peng Meng, Xiao Xue and Feng Xue Dao Ke in the late fights of the same day. Sim: 5 checks in `bossStartsHere`: 3 fail without it, one with the host's copy switched too, one with nothing put back. The figure sweep (11-17, the combined build 0d83f4e; the guest alone at every boss spawner the 11-04 survey knows, at each of the story's 26 stages in turn): 15 bosses seen in the guest's world. The five that stood unstarted (A-Hua, Peng Meng, Hua Qianxiao, Wan Jun, Feng Xue Dao Ke) were each on the player's own side (team 1, friendly to the player), and the guest's swings took nothing from them. With this entry's own call each of the four tried became an enemy (team 0, hostile), lost 800 health to the same swings, and went back as it was; the fifth's own scene began as the guest stepped up. The other ten were enemies already: their own scene on approach (ChiLian, Xiao Xue, Wu Lengshuang), or awake where they stand. At the other stages no figure stood at a spawner the survey knows. Since 352 the switch is for the host's own fight alone, on a copy that follows it, once that fight is told as on.
344. A boss's bar that only blinked in one world left its fight in the others for good. The host's game puts a figure's bar up for an instant as its player arrives by it and takes it down again between two looks of the mod's (the bench, 11-17: Wan Jun's for 0.04 s, Peng Meng's for 0.07 s). Its B went out at once; the F 1 of a fight seen never did, so no F 0 followed, and the guest stood with a bar up and the arena's walls shut for a fight that never was, till the host's next fight ended. Four seconds after a B with no fight told since, "no fight" is told again: it ends there what the B began (a fight that is on says nothing more than before). Sim: 2 checks, the second fails before.
345. 327's hold was undone whenever the host told everything again (the main session's fourth review, 11-17: seven lenses on 323 to 338, each finding checked by one or two more; V1, high). The host sends all it has sent so far again to a player who links again: one silent 5 s or more (a load, a trip to another map, a drop), and in a party everyone as anyone does. A held start came with it as plain items, and was taken at the guest's next calm second: its trigger gone, its copy not started, 326 again, and saved. What a held start already has stays held when it's told again. Sim: a guest back from a load of 8 s still holds it (fails before); the review's own scenario passes 34 of 36, the two left being a guest with no hold of its own (linked after the scene, or sync off and on), which 327 never covered: a wait at every relink would cover them and hold all the host tells then for a whole fight, the user's to decide. Bench, 11-17, the combined build (`claude/stable-coop-trial` 94aa911), Peng Meng: the late guest held 9; its game held still for 7 s, then away to Pangzhen's bell and back: the host told its 26 things again each time, three times, and the guest held its 9 and took none; in by F3 and a step, its own scene, the 9 taken then, 12 of its blows applied on the host, the health the same on both.
346. 331 taken back (the fourth review's V2, high). With the fighter 30 to 45 m from a boss asleep on the host and not told yet, a watcher's stray bar 43 m off made the copy the watcher's: woken, told (328's own re-tell sent its B), the fighter's own fight never told, their respawn held for the watcher, and their lost fight not started over: easier than the game. Any partner nearer counts again, in a fight or not. The cost, 331's case: a guest's own fight the game begins from over 30 m, a partner in no fight standing nearer, waits till they're within 30 m, and since 328 nothing of it is told meanwhile, if the boss sleeps on the host as the bar comes. With the host by them (the boss awake there) its B goes out, and the others show that fight about 4 s, till 344's "no fight" ends it. More than a wait while the fighter struck from there: 354. Sim: the 40 m check now checks that cost; the race twice (the stray before the fighter's bar, and in the same frame): 5 checks fail before.
347. The fourth review's other code findings, each with its tested fix. V3: 328's re-tell went by "in it", which a blow from near alone says: a guest who struck a copy with no bar, went off past 45 m and came back told a fight nobody was in (the host's bar up, its walls shut); told only with the game's own bar on the copy. V4: 325 learned DoorOpen by class and not CurSwitchState: wharves, stinger and weather triggers, subtitle boxes, cages and broken bells have neither, and were asked at every look; a class with neither is asked nothing once that's known (26 reads in 40 s to 3). V5: a start that brings two bosses was kept under one, and went as that one fell: every boss begun in the window is kept, and the start taken when each is beaten there or started here. V6: a phase's win, told in its fall's scene, was held as the next phase's start, and lost if the session ended before that phase fell: a scene begun within 10 s of a boss's fall is that fall's, not a start. V7: 332 never told a guest's press seen late (alone on the map, or in a quiet): counted from the last look that saw the switch shut. V8: 334's kept pay stood at the last death's roll through a death that rolls nothing: a boss's dust twice with the host down; such a death keeps none. V10: any walk up opened 332's 20 s, a pile's or a chest's too: only one to a switch the mod shares. V11: an Off guest's flagged switch was searched for again at every naming: passed over as one this world's game shut. Sim: a check each in four for V3, V4, V5 (3: held under both, and still held as the first falls, in whichever order the old code took; the third, both beaten, is the release as before), V6, V7 (2), V10, failing before but for that one; V8 and V11 by the review's own scenarios (zeroroll, offsearch: all pass), and in four since 357. V6's rule had no end (353), V5's wait none for a boss left alive (353), V7's anchor let 332's case back in (355).
348. The fourth review's tests and guides, and what's left of it. relay_cli: 335's role rule had no check that could fail (the test's guest was given a host, and the link dropped the knock first): the guest built as on Steam, and a host's half; 257 checks. four: another protocol at a party's door (335's status tip), the mirror with no HUD (336), a damage record that dies with its enemy (336). NETCODE.md cited 334 for 335. The Nexus page's draft: close the game before updating, the co-op difficulty is online's, the version first under Not Connecting, an update line in Chinese. The review's gaps, on the bench of 11-17 where it could be done. G1 (a start step told before the host's scene): 349. G3 (saves the older versions left with a boss's start spent): one made with `claude/online-1.7.4`'s scripts (the guest took 8 of the Axe chief's 9, its trigger box gone, saved at the bell), then loaded on this build: the box is back; alone, its scene starts on stepping in, the boss fights and takes the blows (18720 to 18400). Such a save is whole again at its next load; in the session that spent it, it stays spent (A-Hua, that morning). G4 (ChiLian, a boss its own scene spawns): after the mod's late spawn let the hold go, one figure of it in the guest's world, its blows applied, the health the same. G8 (323's Apply in the game, one copy, the pads read as not there): top and bottom, two monitors and side by side again each applied; with player 1's pad there, down to one player; a second asked for whose pad isn't there: Apply grey, the click's line readable under the open panel; the pad in: applied, player 2 back. Not done: G5 (335 on real Steam), G6 (what 325's crashing read needed: no crash in this round's loads and trips either), G7 (a key device's press: Xigu's needs a key item the bench save hasn't got).
349. A boss's start step told before its scene got past 327's hold (the fourth review's G1). A story trigger runs its own step a second or two before its scene begins, the host's look comes every 6 s, and a guest took what it told at the next calm second: about one start in three. The bench at A-Hua, 11-17: the host's look fell 0.2 s after it stepped into the trigger, the guest took that step 0.6 s on, the scene's flag came 1.2 s after that and only the rest was held: the guest's own trigger spent, its own start not to come for as long as that world stays loaded (326, for one who hosts next without loading the save again: a load puts the trigger back, 348's G3; 343 only makes the partner's fight work). A guest takes what the host tells once it's 4 s old: a scene of the host's begun meanwhile has it in its window, held with the start if a boss fight follows. The cost: whatever the host's flow does is in a guest's world 4 s later than it was. Sim: 4 checks in bossStartsHere, 3 of them failing before; one check's wait lengthened by those 4 s (a party's flag by a bell). Bench, the combined build: three starts with a late guest, the host's look made to fall at its trigger: Peng Meng, A-Hua (6 steps told 1.9 s before the scene) and ChiLian (2 told 1.0 s before it): none taken, all held (9, 8, 2). At A-Hua, where the step slipped that morning, the guest's own scene came on stepping in, the start taken then, and its blows landed after it (the health the same on both). The bench's earlier logs, by `notes/stable/bench/slipscan.py`: 8 starts with a hold, 2 of them with a step taken before the scene. Since 353 the wait reaches a guest on another map too, and costs no searches while it lasts. Since 365 it is 6 s.

A copy handed back to its own AI with no movement part at that moment wrote two error lines into the game's log (`[Lua][Error] Tried setting member variable 'bOrientRotationToMovement' but UObject instance is nullptr`, and MaxWalkSpeed's; the bench, 11-17: the host taking A-Hua back as the guest's own scene began). 351. A guest back in the same run of the game didn't get the story things the host had found meanwhile (the bench, 11-17: the guest's co-op off and on again; an elite's Sha-Chi Amber Shard, a piece of four that raise the Sha-Chi, stayed the host's alone, and the guest's copy of that elite was dead by the host's word). The host sends its bag at every joining. A guest counts it once a run, so that the bombs and herbs used since aren't refilled when a sync starts again (a drop, LEAVE and GUEST), and after that first minute it took no counted thing at all, a key or a piece with them. One it has none of comes now if the game's item table calls it a story thing or a piece that adds to the player (ItemDetailType NarrativeItem or AddAttribute: 85 and 7 of its 235 plain items; its 110 materials stay as they are). Sim: 3 checks in followTheHost, 1 failing before; the other two pin what's counted. The bench, 11-17, two copies on the combined build: the guest's co-op off, the host alone killed the twin-hoe elite and took its shard, the guest on again two minutes after the first joining: "Shared loot: Item_Piece_002 x1 (0 -> 1)", and the tip that the bag has what the partner's has. Since 363 only a story thing there's one of, which this bag never held, comes so: this rule gave back what a guest had spent, at every sync, and gave some finds twice, and that shard is the host's alone again.
352. 343's switch went further than the fight it was for (the main session's fifth review, 11-17: six lenses on 339 to 350 with the local co-op fixes merged in; 13 findings confirmed, none high after its checkers; each fix below is a checker's own, tested on their export, and run here again). V1: in a party it also switched a third player's copy in another guest's fight. With the host out of that fight the boss sleeps on the host, so that player's own game runs their copy: it follows no moves, and on the enemy's side it was a figure that never fought back, whose death was the boss's in every world: a fight won by hitting a dummy. Only a copy that follows the host's own fight is switched. What that leaves as it was before 343: a third player whose world hasn't started a boss lands no blow in another guest's fight with it, till their own start. V7: a bar that only blinked on the host (Wan Jun's and Peng Meng's, on the bench) switched the guest's figure for the 4 s till 344 ended it, and a guest's blows in that time came off the host's figure, not started and in no fight: switched only while that fight is told as on (`F 1`), about a second into it. V2: the put-back asked a copy kept from before (the bar's own actor) for its health, IsDead and controller before checking that it's still that copy, on whatever has its address after a trip or a load: its name first. D1: a guest's own death let go of a partner's fight that wasn't on any more (its player down and waiting) without ending it, so a figure switched for it stayed an enemy with no fight, alone after co-op too: ended as any fight is, the figure back on its own side. (Its checkers weren't sure the game keeps the same figure through the refresh a death makes; the fix does nothing if it doesn't.) Sim: `bossStartsHere`: a party of three both ways (a guest's fight: left; the host's: switched) and the blink, 2 failing before; V2 and D1 by the review's own scenarios (stale, wipe), not in four: the fake's bar never comes back for a dead guest, which D1 needs. The bench, 11-17, the combined build with this in: a late guest at Xiao Xue: its figure switched a second after F3 (the log's line), its own scene after that, her blows landing on the late guest (9 and 11 in two passes) and the guest's on the host (12 and 21 applied). Left (the sixth review's V7): `F` tells a player's fight, not a boss's. A figure whose bar blinks while the host is in another fight, or in one begun within 4 s of the blink, is switched till that fight ends, and a guest's blows on it come off the host's figure, which hasn't started. Not seen on the bench: every blink there came with no fight on. D1's case at a wipe (the sixth review's G4; run141, the night of 11-17, the combined build): a late guest at A-Hua, in by F3 and outside its own trigger, its figure switched; the host died, its respawn held for the guest; then the guest died. The host's respawn came 2.3 s after that, and its R ended the fight on the guest as it did before 352 (forgetDeaths): the figure said once to be as the game had it, 2.7 s before the guest's own respawn, whose D1 found no fight left. The game made the figure anew at each respawn: the one read after a relink (not started, the player's own side, team 1) is a new one. No error line. D1's own path, the guest's respawn before the host's R, is still unseen in the game: in the sim the bare drop of before fails only in that order (the review of 369).
353. The held start (327, 345, 347, 349), three ways past it. V3: 347's rule for a phase's win counted any boss's fall from 10 s before the host's scene on, with no end: the late guest's own elite beaten while the host's start scene played, or in the 15 s after it, let the whole start go, taken at the next calm second, the guest's trigger spent (326 again); in a party any other guest's kill in that time did it for every late guest. Only a scene begun within 10 s after a fall is that fall's. V9: 347 keeps every boss begun in the window. Another's bar there that the host then left alive (an elite by the arena, a bar that blinked) was waited for like a start's own second boss, and nothing ended that wait once the real one was beaten: held for the rest of the session. Once one of them is beaten there, the host not down since, another whose fight there is over isn't waited for; one whose fight ended with the host down is still the host's to come back to (the reviewer's simpler rule let a start of two go when the host beat one and lost to the other). The log says which way a start was let go. The cost: a start that truly brings two bosses (none known), the host beating one and walking away from the other alive, gives the guest its start. V4: 349's 4 s wait didn't reach a guest on another map: a step of the host's level was put by for their arrival at once, where the scene's window never saw it, and taken the moment they came over by F3 or a bell. Put by only once it's 4 s old; the tick reads one clock. N5: while only such young things waited, the guest's tick made its world searches every second all the same: the bells' and the pipeline's 6 times for each thing told, 2 now, taken 5 s on as before. Sim: `bossStartsHere`: the elite beaten 4 s into the scene; another's bar left alive, and a start of two with the host losing to the second (held: as before); the guest on another map: 3 failing before.
354. 346's cost was more than a wait (V5). With a partner standing nearer, a fighter's own fight begun by the game from 30 to 45 m stays held. Their blows from there (a thrown blade's, a cannon's) still counted as their own, went to the host and came off the boss asleep there, which never fought back, nobody in its fight, to a free kill. A copy this player struck from within the game's own 45 m, no partner's fight on, is theirs from that blow, whoever's nearer. A stray bar is no blow, so 346's race is as it was, and nothing is told without the game's own bar (347's V3). Not benched: whether a shot reaches that far. Sim: `aPartysBystander`, the 40 m case with a blow: fails before. Since 366 the blow that counts is one of the last 5 s, not the mark of one, and a blow marks any copy asleep on the host.
355. 347's per-switch anchor let 332's case back in (V10). A door seen shut that went out of reach, the guest walking up to any shared switch meanwhile, was told when it loaded back in already open by its own world: after F3, a bell or a respawn within 30 m of it. One anchor, as the fourth review's verifier had it: a guest's press counts from the last look at them all with a partner on the map. Its costs, both narrow: a walk up to another switch while alone, then a door that opens by itself, is told; a door pressed while alone that is out of reach when the partner is back, and next seen after a trip, isn't (nor was it before 347). Sim: `doorsOfTheGames`: a door out of reach while the guest walks up to a lever, back in reach open: fails before.

The panel's own line says what stopped the start, at every press (the older "can't be reached" and "can't be written" with it). The start file carries the scripts' protocol now, and main.dll starts no relay for another's or for none, writing the reason where those scripts show it. The built main.dll, 11-17: a start file as 1.7.3 writes it gives "stopped=yes error=dlls\main.dll is another version's" and no relay; with `protocol=5` the relay runs. `net_start.txt` never leaves the PC: nothing on the wire changes. G1 on the bench, the same day: a guest's real E press at Xigu's key door: "Opened ...: open for the others too" 2 s on, the host's door open (two presses before it did nothing: one 1 s after four thugs by the door died, one 19 s on from the door's other side, a door that opens one way; what the game offered wasn't read at either; the press that worked was made from the first one's spot, with the door read as offered first); and a pile searched with E, then a gate 14 m off opened by the word's own call in the guest's world: nothing told, the host's gate shut.
