358. A report on 1.7.3 (GitHub issue 18, 11-16): three players with late saves, the host's game gone with an access violation every 5 to 15 minutes, five times in an evening, no Lua error and no dump, each time a few seconds after a guest was back (a sync start and the host's bag of 546 things; once a partner's bell sit after a 12 s silence). Not reproduced. The bench, 11-17, three and two real copies: the reported sequence itself (a guest starting sync again every 22 s, the host's bag of 229 kinds going out each time, 12 s silences made with netem, the guests by live enemies asleep on the host) 32 times on 1.7.3 as it shipped and 27 times on this build; a guest putting on 15 main weapons and 19 Phantom Edges the host had never loaded, each drawn and put away, the host dressing the figure with each in the tick it loaded it; a host restarted alone meeting a guest with the Scarlet Wraithblade in hand; the skills of 18 Phantom Edges started 3 m from a fresh host. Both games up every time, no error line. The main session read the native relay through a slot's leaving and coming back (the real Party and Relays under checked iterators and ASan, 86 flaps: nothing) and the hooks (none registered again at a sync start), and "Freed invalid callbacks" in the report is UE4SS's own start-up pair: that session crashed 2.5 minutes after the host's game started, as its guests joined. What stays likely is ledger 1's kind: something given to a returning guest's figure the moment it's loaded. So a weapon this PC had to load is given to a figure only once it has been loaded 6 s (a partner's new weapon shows that much later, each time this PC loads it: again once the game has let it go, a map load or minutes after the figure put it down), and the log says "The ghost takes X, new to this PC" before the call that gives it, so a game gone in that call leaves the line as its last word. In the game: 14 new weapons each taken 6 to 8 s after the guest put it on, and by a fresh host the figure's weapon 15 s after the figure. Sim: `newWeaponWaits`: a spear and a cannon the host's game has only after LoadAsset (the fake finds every other weapon as loaded) go on the figure 6.4 s after the load, the line before the call, and at once the second time; fails with the wait's test out. The line is said at each load, not once a run: one loaded again after the game let it go waited with no line (the seventh review's V7 and V8). Not in a match: 372. The player's own logs are the next step: a reply asking for them is the user's to post. The report's sequence again on the combined build with 359-382 in (the seventh review's G1; run176 and run177, 11-18, three copies, the third through bursts of loss): 28 sync starts in ten minutes with the host's bag of 229 kinds, then 8 more with the third in a weapon and a Phantom Edge new to the host each time. All three up, no error line, no fast read caught or passed over; the bag's things taken once, at the first sync after it was filled (82), and none after.
359. The fast reads went through addresses that were gone (the main session's lead on 358's report, 11-17). main.dll reads a character's place, health and posture straight from memory by its address, an access violation caught (lua_link.hpp). The reach walk goes through a list of enemies up to half a second old, older on a host, and asked nothing of an enemy before reading it by its address: gone by then (a rest's or a death's refresh, a trip, a camp put away), the read went through whatever that memory holds now, and what's behind a pointer read off it can be anywhere. Counted on the bench, 11-17 (two copies, the scripts as they were over a main.dll that counts): the host on eight bell trips in 4.5 minutes had 145 reads caught at memory that was gone, the guest none. A caught read does no harm but at a guard page: the touch takes the guard off, and where that was another thread's stack guard, that thread can't grow its stack past it (Windows 10, the reporter's; this PC's Windows 11 grows past one, the main session's test): an access violation some seconds on in nothing of ours, which is how 358's report reads, though none of the 145 was at a guard page here and the report's cause stays unproven. So main.dll looks at each page before it reads (VirtualQuery: committed, readable, no guard) and never touches one that isn't. A look is kept half a second (a page found good gets a guard only by being freed and handed out again as a stack; a thread's stack itself isn't kept, 377): a character's read is 18 ns against 12 before, and 2100 with a look every time. And the reach walk asks valid() of each enemy first (1.2 microseconds, twice a second an enemy; the game's own call is 27). The log says once a minute what the reads met, when that changed. The bench after, the same eight trips: 0 caught and 26 passed over, the log's line with them; with the native half alone 0 caught and 81 passed over. relay_cli: 14 checks (11 at first, three more with 377), a guard page of the test's own among them: passed over with its guard kept, and with the look off caught with its guard gone. `pp_read_look(0)` puts the reads back as they were, for such a count alone. No wire change.
360. The game's own calls on an enemy's parts that may not be there (the first suspect of the main session's ranking for 358's report). When a partner goes silent for 3 s, or their figure is hidden, the host takes the figure off every listed enemy's hatred list (`game.ghostInFight`) and asks each one's target; the reach rounds ask each enemy's target too. An enemy with no controller (a camp put away by its spawner, a body between two lives) went into the game's `GetTargetActor` as nothing: what the game's code does with nothing there no pcall catches. Its hatred part and its picker were used unasked too, though a call on either when missing is only a Lua error the walk's pcall catches (362). Each is asked valid() first: the controller in `currentTarget` and in local co-op's two calls of the same, the hatred part and the picker in `game.ghostInFight`. Not shown to crash on the bench; a microsecond a call. Sim: an enemy's controller swapped for a gone one, in `hatred` (a partner silent and back) and `localFight` (player 1 far off, the bar by player 2): nothing handed into the call; each check fails with its guard out (the seventh review's V9). The bench, the night of 11-17, the combined build (run143, the guest's link dropping for seconds at a time): the walk ran 15 times over 197 enemies, 12 of them with no controller, passed over; no error line.
361. Two of the fifth review's gaps, the tests' own (its G4 and G5). No change to the mod. G4: four couldn't see a blow land or miss. The fake took any blow whatever the side, so 326 and 343 showed there only as a side set and put back. The fake goes by an enemy's side now, as the game does (`Fake.lands`: nothing lands between a player and a figure on the player's own side, `Faction.Player` with its controller's team 1; with the team alone an enemy's nothing landed on the bench either, 326, and the faction alone, never tried, is taken for not landing), and four's `swing` says whether its blow landed. `bossStartsHere`, 3 checks: on a figure nobody has started no blow lands either way and nothing is told; with the host's fight on, the late guest's blow lands on their copy and is off the boss in the host's world, and the copy's lands on them. With 343's switch taken out the last two fail (the blow not landed, 0 off the boss), and five of the older ones with them. One older check had its guest strike a figure to be in the fight: in by the wall now (`net.joinedBoss`), as a late guest is. G5: the checks of 339 and 348 that nobody had run against their rule taken out. Eleven copies of the scripts, one rule out of each (303's floor of the game's own: kept as a hold is let go, kept as every hold goes back, and standing under a guest's GK on one asleep on the host; 310's party half; 321's drink on one PC; 268's scaled once; 312's calm, and its said once; 335's tip at a party's door; 336's mirror with no HUD, and its damage record), each run against the part that owns its check: 11 of 11 fail, and each part passes on the build.
362. Nothing of the game's is kept of a steered enemy between turns (the fifth suspect of the main session's look at 358's report, with its patch). The rule for who an enemy goes for (288) kept each steered enemy's body and its target picker in its record from turn to turn, each use behind valid(): against the standing rule that no object of the game's is kept across frames, and in the main session's strict sim a write went through a kept picker after its enemy was gone. The record holds numbers and names now (the picker's address, the enemy's name, the waits); the picker is asked of the enemy at each turn, and an enemy let go is handed in by whoever lets it go, this turn's own. At a rest, a sync's end or a load's beginning the ones still there are found by the list as it stands (no search of the world as a map goes: the one change made to the patch), and one gone is only forgotten. Not shown to crash: UE4SS looks a field up by name, so another class's object at a kept address took no write. And pbz.lua's comment on what a pcall catches said too much: a call on nothing is a Lua error, and a pcall catches it (read from UE4SS 1.0.3's code by the main session); what none catches is nothing handed into one of the game's own calls (360) or to one of UE4SS's own. Sim: 3 checks in `localTargets`: nothing of the game's in a steered enemy's record (fails before: 2 kept), its picker's own wait back as both walk off, and as a load begins (fails with the records only forgotten). No wire change. The bench, the same run: three thugs steered between the two players (their pickers' wait 15 s, the others' 1.5), nothing of the game's in their records, no picker left at 15 s once none was steered; no read caught or passed over. And in local co-op, where the load's own hook lets them go (run148, the combined build with 370): three thugs steered between the two, player 2 swinging for real, nothing of the game's in their records; a trip to another map's bell with them steered: nothing kept past it, player 2 there, no error line.
363. A guest back in the same run was given back what it had spent, and some finds twice (the sixth review's V1 and V2, on 351). 351 gave whatever story thing or piece the guest had none of, at every sync after the run's first minute. None in the bag isn't never had: a key goes at its door (the partner's door opens by the word, its key kept), a thing is handed in, four shards are an amber as the fourth comes. So a spent key or a hand-in was back at every drop or LEAVE and GUEST, its held flag raised again, and shards came back after the amber made of them: more than the game gives, as often as the guest liked. And a piece the host found meanwhile came twice: by the bag, and by what pays that find anyway, the host's chest by its word or this world's own copy of it. On the bench of 11-17 (run118) a third player back after 25 minutes took 90 things in one frame, three ambers among them. Only a story thing there's one of (`game.oneOfAKind`) that this bag never held comes by the bag now: its entry stays, at 0, once it's taken out (the entries read once a merge). A piece, what pieces make and a token's half come by what pays their find, once. The elite's shard of 351's bench is the host's alone again when the guest was away for its kill: one short, never one over. The item table's detail column isn't read any more (the review's G7: read in the petals' own loop, where a game update without it would have emptied that set). Sim: `followTheHost`: 3 checks failing before (a piece and a token's half not given; a key spent and a thing handed in still gone three syncs on; a chest's shard once, by the chest); the fake's table has a piece (the review's V10). The bench, 11-17 night, the combined build bdb9a82 (run131): both given the key of Xigu's single door; the guest opened it with the real E key: the door open in both worlds, the host's key kept, the guest's gone, its entry still in the bag at 0 (what the rule goes by, seen for a key a door takes); then the guest's co-op off and on twice: the host sent its bag each time, the key stayed gone and nothing came.

Xiao Xue's game cuts her health to her own most at that world's player's blows in the state the bench reaches her in (16482 under co-op's 24723: 378 has the rule), and a difficulty set again does it to every enemy. On a guest swinging just then that drop of 8241 went to the host and came off the real boss, a third of her health with no blow, and half her guard at the co-op posture when that was written too; she stood under the game's own health (15922 and 15762 on the bench, runs 113 and 100: read at the time as the game's doing). Two guests' games writing in one turn could end a boss. A drop with the copy's most health down too is this world's own write and no blow: not sent; the mod's own write of the most is kept as the last seen. (The host's line was to put both numbers back: it lifts a copy only from 5% under. Put back at once since 374, or the copy kept at its own game's most, 378.) The same on the host for a copy the guest's game runs. Still so: a write that lowers the health alone reads as a blow. Sim: a new part, 6 checks, 2 failing before (7450 to 5000 with the guard 800 to 400; a party's 9900 to 100); the host's half and the kept own write since the seventh review (V10: each fails with its line out). The bench, the same night (run132, run133): a late guest at Xiao Xue swinging for 45 s, the host parked above her. The guest's own game wrote its copy back a dozen times (its health seen at 16482 under a most of 24723 till the host's line healed it), and the host's boss lost the 7 real blows and no more (24563 to 24003), never a third at once; on the build before, 16482 of 24723 twice in 10 s.
365. 349's wait is 6 s, not 4 (the sixth review's V4). A step told up to 3.9 s before its scene was held; told 4 to 5 s before it, more and more often taken, the late guest's own start gone for that sitting (326; 343 still lets its blows land in the host's fight). On the bench the gap from trigger to scene was 1.3 to 3.3 s at the bosses whose start is held, each the same within 0.2 s run after run, and 5.4 s at the hook man's; a hitch on the host at that moment adds to it. 6 s holds a step told up to 5.8 s before. The cost: what the host's flow does is in a guest's world 6 s later than it was, 2 more than before, and what's told in the 6 s before a scene of the host's waits out that scene. (The review tried 8 s too: twice the delay, for gaps nobody has measured.) Sim: `bossStartsHere`: a step told 5.5 s before its scene is held, failing before; three waits in four lengthened. The bench (run138, the combined build): a late guest at A-Hua, the host's look made to fall at its trigger: its 7 steps and the scene's flag all held for the guest's own start, none taken, and her blows landed on the late guest. The steps were told 0.7 s before the scene there: no hard case; the margin is the sim's.
366. 354's rule went by the mark of a blow, which stays till a death or a rest, and missed bosses the codex doesn't name (the sixth review's V5 and V6). V5: a party's guest who had struck a boss, walked off alive past 45 m and come back 43 m from it had their copy theirs again at once, woken with no bar and no new blow, a nearer partner about to fight it; its stray bar then told the fight as theirs, the fighter's own untold, and when they left, the fighter's fight ended on the host and their loss didn't start the boss over. A blow of the last 5 s counts, not the mark (`net.struckAt`); once theirs, the copy stays theirs within the game's own 45 m as before. V6: a blow set the mark only on a boss, and one the game gives a boss's bar that the codex doesn't call a boss (the hook man) is a boss only while its bar is up; a held copy's bar is taken down within a turn, so blows from 30 to 45 m set no mark and wore down the copy asleep on the host, which never fought back. A blow marks any copy asleep on the host, a thug's too. Still so, and older than 354: within 100 m the old mark counts its player in a partner's fight with that boss, so the fighter's respawn is held for them and a loss doesn't start it over till they die, rest or leave. Sim: `aPartysBystander`: 3 checks failing before.
367. 353's fall rule still took a late guest's own elite for the host's (the sixth review's V8). A fall counts as the scene's own from 10 s before the guest first sees the host's scene to 2 s into it. The late guest's own elite, 40 m off in a fight the host never joined, falling in those 12 s let the whole start go: taken at the window's end, the guest's trigger spent (326 again). At its K a fight of this player's own that the host never joined is noted (`net.ownFell`: told from here, no partner's bar on it), and such a fall is no scene's, whenever it came. Not by `ownAnnounced` alone: a fight both are in, the guest's bar up first, is the host's too, and leaving its fall out held a phase's win under the next phase again (the fourth review's V6; the review's own fix did that). Still so: in a party another guest's elite, one this player isn't in, falling in those seconds takes the start (the late guest's own with another guest's bar on it is 376). Sim: `bossStartsHere`: the elite a second into the scene and 5 s before it, both failing before; and the shared fight with the guest's bar first, which fails with the plainer guard.
368. The sixth review's tests and guides, and what's left of it. Its notes in art/arabic.tsv begin UNCHECKED; tools/make_lang_ar.py leaves such a line out of lang_ar.lua (the game says it in English till then), and four fails if one is in: `tip.conn.other_dll` (356) is out till it's checked. V12: 357's line on the door presses, put right there. V13: INSTALL.md and its Chinese copy say that a story boss another guest began without the host takes no blow from a third player whose own world hasn't started it. V14: the local co-op guide has the update line (close the game first: with it open the old main.dll stays, and pad 2's Options opens player 1's menu). V15: AUTHORITY.md's story-flow row: a told bell isn't under the wait, it's lit once the guest has been by it and gone on; the wait there is 365's 6 s; and its boss-start row has 352's limit. V7 is 352's cost, written there. Of its bench gaps: G1 is in 363 (a key spent at its door), G6 in 369 and 378 (her guard bar; her poise as set through every cut; her fall and rise with co-op on, run146 and run156). Not done: G3 (a phase's K against its win scene, at the bosses of phases), G4 (run141's wipe was ended by the host's R before the guest's respawn, so D1's own path never ran: 352), G5 (the party half and the blink, with three copies), and a shot from 30 to 45 m.

In the state the bench reaches Xiao Xue in, her game cuts her health and its most back to her own at each blow of that world's own player while she has more than her own most, her posture left as it is (378 has the rule; in the host's world none came while only the guest swung, run133 and run136). To the scaling a body under the most health it was given is a new one, as a placed enemy back after a rest is, and it was scaled again from the start, its guard bar with its health: 34443 to 72 billion on the host (21 doublings: 4 as her fight began, 17 in run133's 37 s of the host's blows), a bar nobody breaks, and each guest's copy shown the host's guard, 562 million, over its own most. A body that still has the most posture it was given keeps it (`scalePosture`: scaled by what's missing between the numbers it was given and these, most often nothing); one whose posture is back at its base too, a new body, is scaled as before. Her health was scaled again each time, as before, full as the cut leaves it: in that state the host's blows never stuck (run136: 23923 to 24723 in its 45 s), where with her own numbers they did (run113, 17:23:22-36). That half was wrong too, and is 378. Whether real play cuts her so isn't known; her rise is a new body, scaled once (run147); every other boss fought on the bench kept its numbers. A camp put away and brought back (the seventh review's PG6; run179, 11-18, both players 300 m off for 14 s and back, twice): new bodies each time, new names at new addresses, at the game's own numbers, scaled once (880 and 272, three scaling lines a round, the same on the guest's copies): nothing there for a guard bar to grow by. Sim: `aCopyWrittenBack`, with two players and with three: five writes, the guard bar the same (before: 800 to 25600, 1200 to 291600); a write with the posture too: scaled again, once; another body at that address, its guard bar as high: told by its name, scaled from its own (the name test had no check: the seventh review's PV3). The bench after, the same night (run135, run136): through 13 of the game's writes in the host's world, 20 scaling lines in 45 s, her guard bar stood at 34443 on both copies and took the host's blows (34043 to 33363). Her fall and rise in that state, the guest in the fight (run146): the host's kill told, her death run in the guest's world too, a new body four seconds on in both, the same health and guard bar on both (24723, 34443), the guest's bar the mirror of the host's fight again; no scene of the host's followed the fall there, so 353's window had nothing to hold (the sixth review's G3, as far as the bench reaches). Seen there: at her rise the guest, standing in the arena, was told to press E at the black wall or F3 to join: 371. And with three players (run153, three copies, the combined build with 370): 200% health and a guard bar of three (32964, 51664), her bar steady through 14 writes and 21 scaling lines in 40 s, the host's boss losing the guest's real blows and no more; no error line.
370. Three more Arabic lines and a picture the author hasn't checked would have shipped (the seventh review's docs lens, as its checks began). Player 2's menu tips (`local.tip.own_menu`, `local.tip.talisman_taken`, `local.tip.talisman_back`) and the PLAYER 2 badge on the game's menu (`local.art.badge2`) came with player 2's own loadout on 11-11, their notes in the tsv saying "not yet checked" without 368's word, so the tools took them for checked. Marked UNCHECKED now. The three lines are left out of lang_ar.lua (said in English till then). The badge's Arabic picture is out of the mod, in art/unchecked: loadout.lua shows the Arabic badge only if its picture is in the mod and the English one otherwise, and art/make_arabic_art.py writes a picture with an unchecked word there, not into the mod (run for that one picture: the same bytes, nothing in the mod). four: an unchecked picture's row is checked by the picture its notes name (not in the mod's art), and the badge's choice both ways; the rows' check failed for all four with the rows marked and the files as they were. To ship one: take UNCHECKED off its notes, then run tools/make_lang_ar.py for a line or art/make_arabic_art.py for a picture. The four two-monitor tips of 1.7.2 (`local.tip.mon_*`) still said "unchecked" in their notes: the user checked them on 11-11, before that release, and the notes say so now.

With co-op's health at the game's own (CoopEnemyHealth = 100) and the guard bar raised, a write of the copy's base lowers its guard and no most health, so 364 never saw it: half the host's guard gone with none struck, the guest swinging or not. 376. 367 in a party: another guest's bar counted as the host's joining (the seventh review's V5). Two guests charging the late guest's own elite in the same moment each tell their own bar, a relay's delay apart, neither having heard the other's. A partner's bar was then on it, the fall was no longer the guest's own, and it let the start go as before 367. In a party only the host's bar is the host's joining (`net.fightOf`). 377. A kept look of a thread's stack could cover the next thread's guard page (the seventh review's V11; 359's own guarantee). main.dll keeps a look half a second, by its region's bounds. A thread's stack isn't like other memory: when its thread ends the next thread's stack takes the same place at once (12 of 12 on Windows 11, the review's harness), its guard page inside what was looked at, and a read there within the half second touched it. Reached only through a stale object's stray pointer into a stack, and one touch leaves two of a stack's three guard pages here: unlikely, and no cause of 358's report. A thread's stack isn't kept at all (`in_stack`: an allocation that begins with pages only reserved, then the guard); a miss costs two more looks. And the two things the look's safety rests on had no check (V12): a look kept for ever, or kept from its allocation's start, passed all 11. relay_cli: 14 checks now: the guard page below a kept look, a look half a second old looked at again, and a stack read with the next thread's guard put where it was read (fails with the rule out). No wire change.
378. A boss whose game holds no more health than its own most couldn't be beaten (the review of 369, PV1; in 1.7.3 too). Xiao Xue, the bench of 11-18 (run164: co-op off on both copies, her numbers written by hand). Her most and health written to co-op's 24723 stood through 8 s with no blow, and the host's first damaging blow cut both to 16482. Written to 12000, under her own, the most stayed and ten blows stuck. At 24723 with her health at 10000 the blow took its 80 and the most stayed, and her self-heal ran at 1% of 16482 a tick. So her game holds no more health than her own most, and cuts what's over, the most with it, at the next blow of that world's own player. The scaling took each cut for a new body and scaled her again with the share the cut left, a full one: every blow of the host's healed her to full (run137: 23923 to 24723 over 45 s of them), and since 364 a guest's blows, cut the same way on its copy, went to nobody. In 1.7.3 each cut on a guest's copy went to the host as a blow of a third of her health, which is likely how she fell online. Not through the Taken multipliers: her own effect at each grab (GE_ExecutorInvunerable) puts a modifier on TakenReductionMultiplier_Physical, ours read 1 again when it left, and set each pass we'd write over her invulnerability. So such a boss keeps its game's most, and its health stands for co-op's. On the host, a boss with its bar up whose most fell to what it was before the scaling, its guard bar still as given, between two ticks of its fight, is kept there with the share it had (`scaling.lua`'s capped); an enemy with no bar the same at the scaling's next look, a second on at most, with what it has then (her summoned thugs have her rule: run167, where every blow of the host's healed them to full, four of them standing at 8241 of 8241 after a minute of it). Co-op's part of every drop is given back: a blow takes 1/f of what it did, from either player, which is what f times the health comes to. Never easier than co-op's number; her heals stay her own. Not given back: a rise; a drop that leaves it dead, or on a floor its game holds it at; one of more than half its most at once (the game's own set, no blow). Its line still says co-op's numbers, and it's looked at before the line goes. A guest's copy cut the same way (its most down, its guard bar as given) is kept at its own most, its health the host's share of it, and its own blows give back the same part there: they count though the host never strikes. A guest's killing blow on such a boss is a blow unless the host's has no more left than f times it (LR says what it has, in co-op's number). Local co-op has none of it. No wire change. Limits: the blow that came with the first cut is in it and lost, once on each PC; the last blow can end it up to (1 - 1/f) of a blow early; a boss the guest's game runs keeps the old way (its first cut there leaves it at the game's own numbers), and the host's copy of one gets 374's put-back; an enemy with no bar is known up to a second late, and a line with its game's own numbers can reach the guests in that second. Whether real play cuts her isn't known: the bench reaches her by a story jump, though her kit does it with co-op off and other bosses under the host's blows kept co-op's health. Sim: `aBossHeldToItsMost`, two players and three: the host's blows, the guest's, both at once, a heal, a set of more than half, worn down at 1/f, her death; the guest alone with the host never striking; a copy that ran ahead (LR) and its death in step; an enemy with no bar, and the partner gone mid-fight; co-op's health at 100%; and local co-op, in `localDifficulty`. Fifteen of its rules taken out one at a time each fail it. The bench after (11-18, runs 167-172, two copies at her, a late guest in her fight): the host's first blow cut her once, said once, 24723 to 16482, and no scaling line followed; each blow of either player took 53.3 where it took 80 (16 of the guest's: 853.3; 30 of both: 1600.0), her health the same on both copies to the hundredth, her guard bar the same. Her summoned thugs the same once the host struck them: kept at 5494, 5387 after two blows, where on the build before they stood at 8241 of 8241 after a minute of blows. Her fall and rise: a new body four seconds on, scaled as new on both (24723, 34443). The guest alone struck it first: its copy cut and kept at the host's share of its own most (16268.8 of 16482), its four blows off the host's whole (24723.2 to 24403.2). Then the host's first blow: kept at 16482 with the share it had (16428.8 after her heals), both copies the same from there. No error line. Her heals at her grabs are her own: a lone bench swinger's light blows don't outpace them, with co-op off either (run164). Six other bosses with a late guest and the host itself striking for 12 s each (run173: the Emei swordswoman, Bi Yuan, Feng Xue Dao Ke, Xiong Zhang, Lei Dang's second form, Ren Ou Jiao): every blow its 80, no cut, no scaling line, the same health on both copies: they keep co-op's health as health, as before. Since 385 (the eighth review): a body with no bar is looked at before its line, so a guest's blows on it before the host's first are kept with the rest; a kept body takes a party's new count as what its health stands for. And her heals, said the right way round: they are her own in her game's health, so in co-op's number each counts f times, and her heal phases last longer than co-op's health alone would make them (one window of hers, 30 ticks of 164.8, is 30% of her bar here, as alone, where co-op's health would make it 20% with two). Left so: counted once they would make her easier than this.
379. A guest who hosts next in the same run of the game lost co-op's enemy health (the seventh review's PG1, in the sim; 1.7.3 kept it). HOST on the panel starts anew with no load, and nothing told the scaling. Since 269 a guest judges a body it has scaled by its guard bar's most and a host by its most health, so the new host held a guard-bar number against health and took every body it had scaled as a guest for done, for good. One healed to its base by a difficulty set again (25 s alone does that), or a new body at that address after a rest, fought at the game's own numbers for the rest of that run. Both numbers are kept with the role they were taken in, and the other role reads its own. Sim: `aGuestHostsNext`: nothing written (as it was), a difficulty set again, a rest, health alone: 3 fail before.
380. A guest's copy judged new by its guard bar was given its health twice (found by 374's check, 11-18). A difficulty set again writes a copy's guard bar back; the guest's scaling took it for a new body and scaled its health again with it. A held copy's health is the host's line's, so nothing showed; one in a party guest's own fight kept twice its health (19000 of 10000 in the sim) once 374 had put the write back. A guest gives a body its health only when it hasn't the most its scaling left it at. Sim: the party guest's own fight with every number written: fails with the test out.
381. A guest's copy aiming still kept its picker between turns, and handed its blackboard into the game's call unasked (the seventh review's G6: the shapes 360 and 362 took out on the host). A copy's target picker was kept by the copy's address and written through at its release after valid() alone, with no word that it was still that copy's; and `actor.Controller.Blackboard` went into the game's SetValueAsObjectByTag as it was, nothing there being what no pcall catches. What's kept now is the picker's address, the body's name and what the picker waited. The picker is looked up again at each aim (one comes with a move of the enemy's, so the game is asked what it was asked before) and at the release, and written there only if it's the one that was told; the controller and the blackboard are asked valid() first. On every guest, and on a two-player host for a copy the guest's game runs. Sim: `aCopysPicker`: nothing of the game's kept, a copy with no blackboard, another picker there at its release: 3 fail before.
382. A body the game is still making has no attributes for a moment, and the mod wrote on nothing (the bench, 11-18, run173: a boss coming into a guest's world as its story caught up, ten error lines in the game's log in a second; none in the sweep of the build before). A copy's floor is written every turn (`game.setMinHealth`), its most health when the host's differs, and a boss's poise once. A field set on nothing throws no error in UE4SS, but each is a `[Lua][Error]` line in the game's log, and the poise was marked done and said so with nothing set: that body kept the game's own poise for good. Its attributes are asked first: nothing is written till they're there, and the poise is tried again at the next pass. Seen there and left: Xiong Zhang's poise read 1.0 over the 0.5 set here: his own effects put the game's number back, as Xiao Xue's grab does one of hers (378), and set again each pass it would write over what his kit means. Sim: `aBodyStillMade`: a copy's floor and a new boss's poise, each failing with its guard out. (The eighth review, R1: that body was no new one. It was a stand-in the mod had destroyed 22 s before, still listed till collection: run175's log, dropped at 05:43:18, the error lines at 05:43:39 to 40, its name the dropped one's, and names come from one falling counter, never used twice in a run. Every new boss body on the bench had its attributes at its first look. The guards stand; the words in the code say what the body was.)
383. With the host down, a boss only the host's own player can finish could never be beaten, and nothing said so (a report on 1.7.3, Nexus, 11-18: "we are at the first axe master boss, the host died and i was able to kill the boss alone but when it came to combat finish i was glitched and just flying over and over in a loop"). The first map's Axe chief (Peng Meng, row 25): from a fifth of his health the game locks it at 1 (GE_PengMeng_LockHealth, Health_Min), and he falls only to the game's quick-time press, made by that world's own player as one of his moves lands (the user, 11-18: "the boss will not die unless you press "F" in the exact moment he is hitting"; IMC_QTE: F or the pad's top button). Missed, he throws the player (GA_PengMeng_Skill_BEd_Fail). With the host down every move of his goes for a guest, whose press can't answer it: the loop of the report (that throw isn't replayed on a guest on this build). On the bench (the build before this, run180 to run184): the host down, the guest brought him to his floor and every killing blow after was answered "it has 2 left there", for as long as the host's respawn was held, a quarter of an hour. Killed from outside on the host there (his lock taken off, then the game's kill effect) he died with his loot, his walls open and the host back; the story's point stood where it was four minutes on, which says nothing either way: it stands there after his real end too (run188), its row asking for side quests a bench save hasn't. Whether such a death leaves the story whole wasn't taken further (193 found it didn't for another boss): a partner's blow isn't made his death. Now the host, down and held in a fight whose boss has stood at a floor of its game's own (`game.atOwnFloor`: Health_Min 1 or more, its health at it or 1 over) for 20 s on end since a partner's killing blow wasn't taken there (`net.floorRefused`, noted in `handlers.D`), no sequence playing in the host's world and the boss not one a guest's game runs, lets its respawn go (`net.finisherWaits`, asked in `holdForFight`): the fight starts over as the game has it for its own player's death, and each side is told why, a tip each (FN to the guests, a new event: an older version doesn't know it and drops it; no change to the wire). With the host up nothing changes: taking him past his floor is theirs to do. In an arena where a sequence runs all fight long the look never says so, and the wait is as it was. A first build asked for the game's own mark for an execution as well (GE_CanLowHealthExecute on the boss). On the bench that mark was on the host's boss in one run and not in the next, the host down both times: run185, the hold ended 16 s after the guest's first killing blow; run186, at 2 of 28080 with the mark on the guest's copy alone, thirteen killing blows turned down in 80 s and no end. So it isn't asked. Bench, the rule as it stands (run187, the mark absent again): the guest's first killing blow stopped at 10:14:22.9, the host's hold ended at 10:14:43.8, 20.9 s on, the tip on each side; the host back at its bell in the same second, enemies and fog walls put back in both worlds, the guest brought to the host; the fight begun again as the host walked in, a new body at 28080 in both worlds, the guest in it; and with the host up, 40 s of the guest's killing blows at his floor said nothing. His real end on this build (run188): the host, kept whole beside him and tapping the quick-time actions, took his eighth move (`GA_Hun_BEx_Common_PengMeng_V2`, 2.9 s to his death); the guest's copy died 0.01 s after the host's word of it, its walls opened, 9000 dust and the Dread Axe came to both with the game's card, the host's flags, flow steps and bells were taken as they came (7, then more), and the stone crane bell by his arena was rung in each world; no error line. So the report's other half ("the boss is still standing up then dies after moments without giving the memory bell story cutscene and stuck") didn't show on this build; no scene came at that bell for either copy on the bench saves. A boss its game holds at a floor for something that comes without its player and takes over 20 s to begin can't be told from this; none is known. Not done, the user's to decide, both easier than this: the host standing up again to make the press (that would bend "down stays down"), or a guest's killing blow at his floor being his death with the host down. Two new tip lines, English and Chinese; their Arabic waits for the user's check (tip.boss.finisher_host, tip.boss.finisher_guest, UNCHECKED in the tsv). Sim (`finisherWithTheHostDown`): 22 checks; each of eight rules taken out fails at least one (the rule itself 9, the blow not taken 3, the floor 1, the sequence guard 2, the note dropped off the floor 2, the count starting again 2, the word to the guests 8, the guest's note of a lost fight 1); "the host's game alone" and "not one a guest's game runs" have no check. (The ninth review: on the bench each of the two tips had the tip bar for under half a second, the respawn's own tips over it, as run187's pictures show; 388 keeps it there its four seconds. In a party the hold waits while another player's own boss fight is on: 388.)
384. A boss looked at while its game was still making it kept the game's own health and guard for the whole fight (the eighth review, sent ahead of its report: two of its lenses found it, each with the same fix tested; since 382, 11-18). A body's first look can land in the 0.9 s it has no attribute set yet (run175's log: the write errors at 05:43:39.4 to .57, a look at 40.276). Before 382 a boss's poise write on nothing came back ok and ended that look, so its health and guard were scaled a pass later; 382's refusal let the look go on, and it marked the body scaled (`scaled[addr]`) with nothing written and no most noted, which `same` then took for the same body for good. The boss fought at the game's own numbers (5000 and 400 in the sim for 7500 and 520), the host's line pulled the guests' copies down to them, and on a guest 378 never kept such a copy (its guard bar never what scaling gave). An enemy with no bar had the same gap before 382 too. Now a body with no attributes yet is looked at for nothing this pass (hits, poise, health, guard, promotion) and again at the next (`tick`). Two smaller ones from that review. LR, the word to a guest whose killing blow wasn't taken, read a kept boss's health before co-op's part of that blow was given back (378), so the copy was put (1 - 1/f) of a blow under the host's boss and its next floor came that much early (440 to 307.3 on the host, the copy at 241.0, in the sim): the give-back first now (`scaling.look`, as before a line of it), and four's check of it is to the point where it allowed 100. And 378's give-back said "not at 0" twice, the second never asked: said once (not at its floor, and 0 is the floor of one with none); its floor rule had no check and has one. Sim: `aBodyStillMade` 3 more checks, its bodies with no attribute set at all for the while, as in the game (the fake's dead set still took writes, so a body marked scaled with nothing written couldn't show); `aBossHeldToItsMost` 4 more and 1 tightened. The three rules taken out fail 2, 1 and 2. (The check of a body at 0 holds with the floor rule out as well: nothing in the sim looks at one there.) (The review's own checkers then refuted the window this closes: see 382's note. No living body has been seen with no attributes; the guard is harmless and stays, and a party's look has it too since 385.)
385. The eighth review (24f9545..d0412af with 6b62405): nothing high once checked; four of its findings taken in here. V1 (378): a body with no bar kept at its game's most, her summoned thugs, went back to full at the host's first blow, a guest's blows before it undone on both screens (in run170's log: the guest's copy "healed on the host (5334 -> 8081)"). Nothing looked at such a body before its game's cut, so the scaling's pass kept it with what it had then: whole. Its line now looks at any body scaled here first (`scaling.watched`, in `shareEnemy`, with the numbers the turn has just read), and the pass's look weighs a cut by what it had before it (`cutAtLook`): kept with its share, as a boss is (630 of 900 becomes 420 of 600 with two players, 930 of 1200 becomes 465 with three). V2 (379, 380): a guest's notes of a copy kept the host's line's most multiplied again (1350 of 900). The line gives a copy its most before the guest's scaling first looks as often as not: the quiet after a load holds the scaling, not the enemies' turns. Hosting next, that guest took its bodies for ones their game cuts, 225% for two; and a party guest's copy was given its health twice at a write of its guard bar (19000 of 10000). The notes keep the most the line says (`scaling.lineMost`, from `syncEnemy`). V3 (375): a party's copies keep the most guard they were scaled to when a player joins later, and the host's line lifted a copy's bar over it (1200 of 800); its game puts such a bar back at the next blow, and 375 then dropped that blow's guard. Never lifted over its own most now (`syncEnemy`). V5 (378): a kept body took a party's new count on its game's numbers, was dropped as kept and stood at 133% where three get 200%, or healed to full at its next cut: the new count is what its health stands for (`scaleForParty`). And 383's note of a blow not taken goes when the hold ends. Sim, 28 more checks: `aBossHeldToItsMost` (V1 with two and three players, V5 as a third joins), `aGuestHostsNext` (its three cases again with the host's line first, and what that order broke), `aCopyWrittenBack` (the party guest's fight the same way), and a new part `aGuardOverItsMost` (V3). Rules taken out: V1 fails 4, V2 4 (its two halves 1 and 3), V3 1 (2 since 388's V10), V5 1. The pass's look reaching a body before its line does (1 timing in 60, by the review's count) has no check. Not benched: V1 at her thugs (the guest five blows, then the host one: 5280.7 where it read 5494). Tried (run189): she summons in the world whose own player fights her, her thugs die within 3 s of that player leaving her fight (the host 30 m above), and she summoned twice in that session (four as the late guest joined, one 9 s into the host's fight begun again at 12216 of 16482) and not again in four more tries, her health set to 11500 and to 8000 (back at 15983 a minute on). Seen there and left open: a guest's own game summons her thugs too, for its copy of her (four in the guest's world with none on the host; with the host's own summoned, the guest had its copy of the host's and one of its own beside it).
386. The eighth review's lows. V6 (378): a copy kept at its own game's most went over to the guest's game (RUN 1) still at that most, co-op's part of what it had left gone and its blows there whole. It's put back at the host's numbers as the guest's game takes it over, to run the old way (`handlers.RUN`). One narrow way there, by the review's checker: the two fights' words crossing at the start, then the host down over 60 m from her; no check of its own. V8 (373): a figure's Phantom Edge slot, hidden for a partner with none, was shown as the figure came back into view (a scene, a map, down), so with a new edge of theirs still waiting its 6 s it showed this player's own: kept hidden till it holds theirs, as dressing has it (`moves.hideGear`). D1 (381, uncertain): an aim that found no picker once made the copy's record anew, and the picker's own wait with it; found again, its wait read as this mod's, and the copy let go never turned to another target by itself. The record is kept when none is found (`game.aimCopy`); no check of its own. V7 (378), words: her heals are her own in her game's health, so in co-op's number each counts f times (scaling.lua, and 378's note). V10 (375): its host half, `handover.follow`, had no check: one now (the host's game writing its copy of the guest's boss back to base, the guard alone: 800 to 400 on the guest's boss with the rule out). V11: the guides' line on a boss kept at its own health leaves out one a guest went in to first, which stays at its game's own numbers till it falls: said, English and Chinese. V12: this ledger's closing paragraph gave the old account of Xiao Xue's numbers (her game writing her back by itself): with co-op off one was her heal at her grab and one the difficulty set back, and with it on her game's cut and the scaling's raise, as 369 and 378 have it. Not taken: V9's other rules with no check beyond 384's floor (the review counts 6 of 25), and R2, R3 (refuted). For the user, G7: whether 358's 6 s weapon wait stays in PvPvE. Sim: 2 more checks (`aCopyWrittenBack` for V10, `newWeaponWaits` for V8), each failing with its rule out.
387. A play log (the user, 11-18: "can you put something that will track everything during my play like a log that will be useful to you to find issues and fix them", then "I am planning to play the whole game just letting you know so the log recorder you give me better be able to help you debug the game"; one player, on a friend's ASUS ROG Ally). UE4SS.log is emptied at every start and holds only what the mod said: a report made after a restart came with nothing (the Nexus crash post of 11-18 had no log), and a session alone left nothing on how the game stood. playlog.lua, loaded before the rest of the mod, keeps a file of each session's own: playlog-<date>-<time>.txt in the mod folder's logs folder (the zip ships it; the mod folder itself where there's none, since Lua can't make one), the newest 12 kept by a list beside them (PlayLogKeep), a session going on in a next file at 8 MB. In it: every line the mod says (main.lua's log passes each on; those said before the file opened are kept for it), written through line by line up to 300 a second so a crash keeps its last line; UE4SS.log's own lines worth having, read on every 2 s (its version, the mods it starts, other mods' lines, Lua errors and the lines under them; not its hook and offset lines, nor the mod's own a second time; 60 a pass, the rest counted); STATE every 10 s (frames a second and the longest frame, the mod's time on the game thread by coop.lua's own stage count, which the log turns on unless a developer's is, Lua's memory, the map and place, health, down / scene / fight, the party, the relay's line once a minute); PLAY for what changes (in the world, the map, down and up, a scene by its name, a fight, the player's own boss fight by the boss's name with its health, the story's point by story.lua's own read every minute and soon after a scene, a boss fight or a map, a frame of 0.5 s or more); MARK, the player's own mark by F2 (F7 since 391) or both sticks pressed in for 0.7 s, with "marked in the play log." on the screen (zh in lang.lua; the Arabic row UNCHECKED and left out till the user checks it); and SNAP at a mark and as the player goes down: the game's screens that are up, the body's movement mode, montage and four state tags, and, while co-op is on (the mod knows the enemies only then), the enemies within 40 m with their health. No addresses: an IP is written as its kind of network, a Steam ID, a Windows user's folder and a host's or friend's name not at all, Secret and HostAddress as set or not. PlayLog = Off: no file, and nothing counted. It reads the game and writes its own files, nothing else; each part of its look runs in its own pcall and is left out after three failures; a file that can't be written ends the log, never the mod. Found on the way: its first draft asked game.sequenceOn for a scene, which spaces its searches for the game's sequence manager, and the log's look used one up: the host saw its scene two seconds late and its skip was never told (four.lua's "a boss's scenes" caught it); the log asks the manager itself now, and nothing it calls changes what the mod goes by (story.point puts the mod's turn at reading back as it was; one thing a read of its does leave behind: story.lua's set of finished quests is that read's from then on, a fresher one than its own last). The sim's files learned flush, append, read(n) and seek for it; make-release.ps1 ships the logs folder. Checked: four.lua's thePlayLog (29 checks: the file, the settings line, every line the mod said, the list of twelve and PlayLogKeep, the masks, STATE, PLAY, the boss fight, the story, UE4SS.log read on and its flood, both marks, the snapshots, a full file, a file that can't be written, a line said before the log began, Off); `two` unchanged. Bench, Steam offline (the game starts and plays that way): run191, two copies linked for 26 min: both files written, STATE every 10 s, F2's mark, no failure line of the log's own; the mod's time read 95 ms a second on each copy (31 to 112; most in enemyTurns, watchSlowly's longest step 35 to 53 ms). run192 and run193, a host alone as the test build for one player has it (Role = Host, ThisPcOnly = On): 46 to 49 ms a second with nobody joined, 60 frames a second; F2's mark with its snapshot (the four screens up, the body, an Iron-Masked man at 35 m with 1214 health) and the word on the screen; the story's point as it moved (row 2, then row 24 with 20 steps done); the Axe chief's scene by its name (Seq014, 41.5 s), then "a boss fight begins: sp:PengMeng_Seq014, alive, health 18720 of 18720"; no error line. Not made in the game: a death (the bench's player is kept at 1 health; the sim has it), a file's second part, and a real pad's two sticks. Memory, read on the way: a copy takes 6 to 11 GB of this PC's 24 GB (12 to 14 GB committed) and 4.5 GB of the graphics card's 24: memory, not the graphics card, is what holds the bench to two or three copies.
388. The ninth review (REVIEW-9: d0412af..50221b8 with 6b62405, the trial's 5bdfc19; nothing high, nothing refuted, the safety lens found nothing), its mediums and what else was code. V2 (383, medium): the two tips of the fight starting over had the one tip bar for under half a second: the respawn's own tips took it ("your partner is 399 m away." 0.04 s on for the host, "your partner is back." 0.39 s on for the guest, in run187's log and its pictures), so neither player read why the fight began again. status.lua's notify takes a third word, keep: the bar is that tip's own for its 4 s and what's said meanwhile waits for it; 383's two lines pass it. V3 (385, medium): a body made at a dead body's address (addresses come back) found the dead one's name in its line's record, so its game's cut at the host's first blow wasn't weighed and it was kept whole, the guest's blows undone: 385's V1 again for that thug. The record goes as the scaling takes a new body there (tick and scaleForParty). V1 (383, low): in a party the hold ended while another guest was in a boss fight of its own on the map, and the host's respawn ended that fight (a host's death is everyone's) with a tip about a fight that guest wasn't in. net.finisherWaits waits while another player's own fight is on, as the hold did before 383. V4 (385, low, CoopEnemyPosture 100 only): a worn body whose numbers the game wrote again (a difficulty picked lower in the menu and raised again by the mod) looked like its game's cut with its guard bar its game's own, and was kept at a share of a share (133 of 600 for the 300 it had); by its share only for a boss or a body whose guard bar was raised here, else kept with what it has, as before 385 (harder there for a guest's blows before the host's first, never easier). V5 (385, low): the guard lift read a copy's most every turn of every copy the host's bar stood over (2 reads a frame with 6 such copies, 6.7 with 20); it's read as a lift is due, and a copy found at its most is left be till its bar moves (net.postureFull), the full second's wait after a blow kept. Checks: a new part `ninthReview` (13 checks; on the scripts before these, V1's two, V2, V3, V4 and V5 fail), and three that couldn't fail or stopped the run put right: V9 (four.lua on scripts from before 383 no longer stops at game.atOwnFloor), V10 (aGuardOverItsMost's blow is made as its game makes one, a bar held over its most ending at it: 385's "V3 1" is V3 2 now), V11 (aBodyStillMade's stand-in set notes what's asked of it: nothing of its most). Benched: V2 (run199: see 390). Not benched: V1 wants three copies with two fights on one map, V3 a summon of hers on a used address (she summoned none again, run189), V4 a setting nobody ships.
389. The ninth review's words.
