390. A host's story step that didn't take on a guest is asked again. Found by the last game checks of 387's build, the bench with Steam offline (11-18). run196, the story followed by a guest 25 rows at a time to its last row, 650, on two real copies: the guest's point was the host's at all 26 jumps, no error line, both copies up at the end; but from the jump over rows 100 to 124 on the guest stayed five done steps behind, 241 against 236 at the end. Its log there: "1 of the host's story steps didn't take here" and "4 ... didn't take here: left as they are". The PC was out of breath (two copies of the game and four other test jobs in 24 GB, under 1 GB free): the game's calls went through, the steps weren't done at the look two seconds on, or ever, and a step that didn't take was left till the guest's next load. run197, the same five jumps with the PC to itself: the done bits of the two copies the same after each, 58 of 58 at row 124. So a step that didn't take is asked again 10 s on, three times in all (TAKE_TRIES, AGAIN_S), and only then left as it is; one the game truly won't take (340's) is asked twice more, with nothing more told to the player. Sim (followTheHost): a step the game won't take is asked three times, then left, and tried again after a load as before; one that takes at its second asking is done with no load waited for and told with the rest. Not made in the game: a PC out of breath isn't something the bench makes twice the same. The other checks of that round: run195 and run198, a late guest at the eight bosses of sweep8 (the guest's blows applied on the host at all eight, the bosses' blows hurting the guest at Jing Ye, Xiong Zhang, Lei Dang's second form, the sword woman and Bi Yuan; no error line). In run195 the three on Zhengdaodaying started no fight: the host fell through ground not loaded yet and died twice, the PC out of breath as above; in run198, the PC to itself, all three began and were fought. run199, 388's V2 in the game (383 at the Axe chief on the trial's c5b0487): each side's line of the fight starting over stood on its screen in the picture taken as the hold ended, the host's over the death screen, and the next tips came 4.0 s later on both (15:21:50.05 and 15:21:54.07). The ninth review's G1, the guest's own press at his quick-time moment (the Nexus report's case), tried four times and not answered: in run200's first 29 s, the host up in the fight and the guest by its copy tapping the quick-time actions, three of his moves on the copy each ended as the throw for a missed press (GA_PengMeng_Skill_BEd_Fail, the guest broken), with no finish, no death in either world and the story where it was; then the host, parked 20 m off, was out of his arena, its game ended the fight and put him away, and the run was over. run201, run202 and run204 failed before a press (the guest fell through the map; the guest still in its own scene of him, and his throw's camera taken for an end; the host, with nothing keeping it whole as it stood by him, struck down as he reached his floor, and the fight over with it). Three moves aren't the eight the host's own finish took in run188. The fifth, staged whole (run205), answered it: 393.
391. The play log's own review (the main session's, five lenses on a80744e), its first findings taken in as they came. High: over Steam the log wrote a partner's Steam name, though the guides, the ini and 387 say it holds no name: in the relay's own line (" peer=<name>" with via=steam, in a STATE line once a minute and at each mark) and in the panel's lines ("Connected to <name>.", "Looking for <name>...", "Host address set to <name>.", "couldn't find the host address <name>"). mask() writes them as "<a Steam friend>", "<the partner>" and "<the host>"; a peer that's "-", an address's kind or "<Steam ID>" is left. Medium: a user's folder was taken out only as "Users": any case now (c:\users\...), and a Linux or Proton login's (Z:\home\<login>, /run/media/<login>). Medium: the mark was on F2, where both guides tell Gauntlet of Masters' players to put that mod's menu: it's F7 (F3 to F6 are the mod's own; the guides' Keys line says so). Low: the story line carries which steps are done (story.lua's bits, one a quest row of the table; notes/stable/bench/storygap.py reads two copies' and names the rows one lacks: how 390's five were looked for); the guides, the ini, the logs folder's note and the GitHub bug form ask for the play log of the session it happened in, by the date in its name, not "the newest" (after a restart that's the new session's); lang.lua's line sits under its own heading after the connection's. Checks: thePlayLog 30 (the names and folders; F2 no longer marks, F7 does; the bits). The test zip the user plays on (f6f9756) is from before this: a session alone on one PC over UDP writes no name, and on a handheld the mark is the two sticks. Its cost and file findings, and three places where 387's line said more than the code: 392.
392. The play log's review, its cost and file findings. Medium: a long session's files counted against the files kept like any others: a session that fills one 8 MB file after another (UE4SS's own error at every frame passed the log's reading of UE4SS.log at 30 lines a second) deleted an older session's file with each new one, and past twelve its own first file, the one with its head and how the trouble began. keep() never deletes this session's first file nor the one just begun: the older sessions' go first, then its own middle ones. And the same line of UE4SS.log over and over is said once and then counted ("the line above 199 times more"), the lines under its repeats left out: an error at every frame is two lines every two seconds, not thirty a second. Low: a write that failed never ended the log: Lua's write and flush return nil and a message, they don't raise, so the pcall around them saw nothing; they're asserted now, and the sim's file says a full disk as Lua does (387's check of it had passed only because the sim's write raised). Low: a kept file that couldn't be deleted (open in a viewer, under a scan) fell off the list and stayed for good: it stays on the list and goes the next time. Low, unproven in the game: UE4SS.log was opened anew every two seconds, and a file another program wrote since its last opening is one a virus scanner looks at each time: it's read through one handle kept open (the file only grows in a session). And STATE says what the log itself costs ("this log N ms a second": its look and its keys by the mod's fine clock), which "the mod N ms" never counted. Said right now: 387's line (29 checks, not 30; what a read of story.point leaves behind; the snapshot's enemies only while co-op is on) and 391's (30, not 31). Checks: thePlayLog 36 (the session that fills its files, the file that can't be deleted, the line over and over, one opening of UE4SS.log, the log's own cost in STATE; the full disk by what Lua's write returns).
393. A guest's finishing move at a boss only the host can finish, the host up: each side is told. The two things still unproven at the first map's Axe chief, staged in one fight with what broke the tries before put right (run205, the trial's e3ae6c6, the PC to itself: notes/stable/bench/pmfloor.ps1; the host kept whole and inside his arena, the guest's scene waited for). The user's "the boss animation hit did not do damage" (11-18): at his floor, a minute each, pressing nothing, the host beside him lost health 16 times (1933) and the guest beside its copy 10 times (1208), every fall in his throw for a missed press (Hun_PengMeng_GEd_Fail_Montage); with run190 at his higher health, his blows hurt both players on this build, each in their own game (a partner's figure isn't where a blow is decided: what's seen on it is the picture). The ninth review's G1, the Nexus report's guest who "was glitched" at his finish: the guest tapping the quick-time actions by its copy for 150 s, then real F keys for 60 s, the host up 8 m off. The guest's game does make the finishing move on its copy (GA_Hun_BEx_Common_PengMeng_V2 on the guest's player, GA_Hun_BEd_Common_PengMeng on the copy, Hun_PengMeng_BeKilled_By_Hun_Montage: 12 times by the injected taps in 150 s, once by the keys in 60 s; and the copy's being-killed montage came once in the minute with no press at all). In the run the guest's game told 72 killing blows, none taken at his floor ("it has 1 left there"), and each time the copy stood at 2 again and fought on: no death in either world, the host's boss at 1 of 28080, the story where it was on both, no error line. So nobody is stuck (on 1.7.3 the copy died for the guest alone), but the guest sees its finishing move play and the boss get up, over and over, and nothing said why to either player. Now a host who's up asks, a second apart and only while such a blow is noted, whether that boss waits for its finishing move, by 383's own rule (net.finisherAt, taken out of net.finisherWaits: a boss of its fight at a floor of its game's own, a partner's killing blow not taken there, no sequence playing here, not one a guest's game runs). Once it has for the same 20 s, it tells the guests (FB <boss>) and its own player: "only the host can finish this boss: your killing blow doesn't count here." there, "only you can finish this boss: your partner's killing blow doesn't count here." here, each kept on the tip bar its four seconds (zh in lang.lua; two Arabic rows UNCHECKED and left out). Once in two minutes a boss, and then only for a blow not taken since. The lines say whose it is to end, not how: what lifts such a floor isn't known for every boss (the ninth review's G2; the tenth's reading of this design before its commit). The guest's game can't tell by itself: every copy there is held at 1 till the host says. (The first make, never committed, said it from handlers.D at the first blow not taken at any such floor: a phase's scene starts from one too, and there it would have been wrong. Found reading it over before the commit. A host who goes down after is counted from then, as before: this count is its own, and 383's is as it was.) Sim (finisherWithTheHostUp, 17 checks): nothing said at once, nor 13 s on; said to each side at 22 s; not again in two minutes; again only for a blow stopped there since; nothing through a scene in the host's world, nor into the phase after it; off his floor and back, counted anew; no floor, his death and nothing said. And in local co-op (localDifficulty) nothing of it is said of a boss at such a floor. Made in the game (run209 and run210, the trial's 15d351a, the fight after 383's restart, both up): 20 and 21 s after the first of the guest's killing blows wasn't taken, each line on its screen (notes/stable/runs/run209-393-told-guest.png and -host.png), FB once, 56 blows not taken in the minute.
394. 383's fight starting over, and a boss in the guest's world that one blow killed for nothing. Found by the bench (11-18, run207, the trial's 15d351a): the Axe chief with the host down, the guest striking his copy at his floor all the while, as a player would. The host's hold ended as 383 has it, FN and its R; the guest's game had stood still 5.7 s just then (two copies on one PC, the host loading its respawn: both logs "No packet for 5.7 s", the guest's play log "a frame took 5.70 s"). The relay hands the game every line that came since its last read (relay.hpp's take_enemies) and a frame takes its events before its lines (coop.lua's tick), so the guest handled the R, its world's refresh with it, and then read 5.7 s of the host's lines of him from before, 2 health, stamped as just come; handlers.R never dropped the host's records, and a link just back keeps them from going stale. The body the guest's refresh had made was written down to that (the play log: "a boss fight begins: sp:PengMeng_Seq014, alive, health 2 of 28080"). The guest stood in his arena, so its own fight with it began, the host at its bell handed it over (RUN 1), handlers.RUN took the hold's floor off, and with no lock of the game's on a health that was written (the lock comes by a blow crossing a fifth of his health), the guest's next blow, 1.9 s on, was his death in the guest's world: 9000 dust and the Dread Axe to both players, "beaten" in the guest's save. In the host's next fight the guest's world had no boss of him to strike (brought back for the partner's fight, two bodies, the bars changing hands), and his real end paid both a second time. run187 and run199 went the same way with no stall and were fine: the new body stood whole. The way is there in 1.7.3 for any host's respawn with a guest by a boss; 383 makes it likely, a guest by definition striking a copy at 2 as the fight starts over. Now: (a) a guest whose world refreshes by the host's word (R, RH) forgets the host's lines so far, the ones waiting unread too (net.dropHostLines: the queue drained, net.remoteEnemies and net.lethalPending emptied; handover's own were forgotten there already, forgetDeaths); (b) it takes none for half a second more, a late packet's being from before it; (c) a guest's copy of a boss isn't let go at the hold's floor as it stands, by whatever way it came there (net.letGo, for a handover's release and a round's): the host's share where the host says just now that it has more than that floor, whole otherwise. Whole too where the host's own stands at that floor alive: it can only do so through a partner's killing blows by a floor of its game's own, and a copy let go at 2 has no such lock here. (b), (c) and that last are the tenth review's readings of the fix before its commit; the host's own numbers can't ride on RUN 1, it had no body of him then. Sim (restartWithOldLines, 18 checks): the R with the host's old lines arriving with it, the same lines a moment later, the guest's own fight after, a blow that's a blow and nothing paid; RUN 1 handed to a copy at 2 with the host's own at 2 (whole) and healed to two fifths a moment before (two fifths); the host's lines run out (whole). On 15d351a's scripts the same checks read "2.0 of 7500" after the R and "2.0, its floor 0" in the guest's own fight: run207. In the game the stall was asked for on purpose, a 9 s busy wait on the guest across the R (notes/stable/bench/pmrestart.ps1): run208 was spoiled by the script itself (the wait came again and again), and in run209 and run210, on 15d351a, no false kill came: the guest's blows had stopped before the R in one, and in the other its copy was one spawned there for the host's fight and went with it. So in the game the false kill stands on run207 alone, and the fix on the sim. four 2373, pvp 162, two unchanged, chaos 19.
395. A guest's own finishing move ends the boss for everyone. The user, 11-18, of the first map's Axe chief: "why the guest can't kill the boss by pressing F and playing it's animation he should be able to do that". Till now a partner's killing blow wasn't taken at a floor of the host's game's own (193: the forest's last boss, forced past its floor between phases, died outside its script and the host's story stuck), the guest's finishing move played on its copy and left him standing (run205), 383 started the fight over with the host down and 393 told each side that only the host could finish him. First the host's side was read on the bench (run211, the trial's 70dfe35): the host's Axe chief at his floor, his game's floor lifted (Health_Min to 0; what holds it is GE_PengMeng_LockHealth) and the game's own kill made on him (game.applyDamage, real). His death ran whole, as at his end by the host's own finishing move (run188): "Enemy died here", 3 flow steps, 2 flags, the Dread Axe on the game's own card, 9000 dust to the partner, the walls open, his body gone. Now a guest whose killing blow on a boss's copy comes inside its own finishing move tells it as that (D's lethal field X, not L: net.finishingMove). By the game's own two signs: an ability of its "BEx" family running on the guest's player (GA_Hun_BEx_Common_PengMeng_V2: some 8 s, the killing blow 2.9 s in), and the copy marked by the guest's game, within 15 s, for an execution at low health (GE_CanLowHealthExecute, looked for a second apart on a boss's copy at the hold's floor; not GE_CanWeakExecute, an execution on a broken guard, which takes health and ends nobody). The host, for X on a boss at a floor of its game's own, lifts that floor for the blow and applies it as any killing blow, the game's own kill and the whole death; if the game still won't let it die (a floor for a scene to come isn't one a finishing move is made at) the floor is put back and nothing is forced, 193 as it was. A plain killing blow there is still not his end: that would be easier than the game. So 393's word is one line for both sides now, told 20 s on with the host up or down: "this boss falls only to a finishing move: a plain blow can't end it."; and 383's restart, which would cut short a guest's tries at the finishing move, waits three minutes at that floor, not 20 s, its line "nobody made this boss's finishing move: the fight starts over." (four lines out, two in: zh in lang.lua, the Arabic rows UNCHECKED and left out; the guides and AUTHORITY.md). The wire: one more value of a field, no new event; both players are on one version as ever. Sim: guestsFinisher (14 checks: the finishing move told as that and his end in both worlds; its ability running with the copy unmarked, and marked with none running, plain blows not taken; one the game won't let die not forced, its floor back; the host down, the guest told what ends him 22 s in and its finishing move his end, no fight starting over). The fake has neither a player's abilities nor effects: what reads them (pbz.lua's finishing and hasEffect) is stood in for there. finisherWithTheHostDown, ninthReview and restartWithOldLines keep 383's first wait for their own timings, with one check of the three minutes; finisherWithTheHostUp has the one line. four 2391, pvp 162, two unchanged, chaos 19. The bench, the fix in the game (the working tree, run215): the report on 1.7.3's own case, "the host died and i was able to kill the boss alone but when it came to combat finish i was glitched". The host down and held, the guest's plain blows taking him to his floor and stopped there; 20 s on the line on the guest's screen (notes/stable/runs/run215-told-guest.png); the guest's game had its copy marked; real F presses on the guest's window, and 48 s in "Finishing move on sp:PengMeng_Seq014; telling the host", "Partner's finishing move", "Host says sp:PengMeng_Seq014 died; its death runs here too" within 0.1 s. Dead in both worlds, the walls open in both, the Dread Axe's card on the guest's screen, the host's respawn going on ("The fight is over for you"), and what a boss's fall with the host down brings as before: 9000 dust of his pay to the guest, the win's steps taken by the host from the guest's world. No error line. With the host up it isn't made in the game: in run212 to run214 the bench's boss stood idle through the guest's presses and offered no finishing move (in run212 the guest, never having struck him, wasn't marked either: the game marks a copy by that player's own blows). The host's side with the host up is run211's. Not looked at yet: Lei Dang's second form, the other boss known to carry such a lock.
396. Five findings of the tenth review on 394 and 395, sent ahead of its report and fixed before the bench runs they bear on. (1) A partner's finishing move the host's game refused made the host's own boss a partner's. `game.applyDamage` forgets a boss as one before the game's kill, and 395 put its bar back with `game.showBossBar`, which marks a bar as the mirror of another's fight: the host's own fight read as over (`net.ownBosses`), `F 0` went out, and the tips and every hold that goes by the host's fight went with it. The mark is put back where it's taken off, in `applyDamage`, for a boss whose kill the game refused, and nothing is shown again. (2) The game's kill isn't refused once Health_Min is 0 (run211: the Axe chief's lock was still on him as it took), so "put back if the game won't let it die" was no guard: a finishing move told during a scene in the host's world, or at a phase's floor, would have been 193 again. The host lifts a floor for X only where a finishing move is the boss's end: a floor of the last point (1), the boss low (a quarter of its health or less), no sequence playing there, and no other phase of it to come (`game.phaseAfter`: a boss of phases has a spawner each, HookManP1_Seq022 before HookManP2_Seq023, ChangHong_Seq106 before ChangHongP2_Seq107, and one with a later phase's spawner in the world isn't ended by a partner's finishing move). Otherwise it's a plain killing blow there, not taken, and the host's log says why: "A finishing move on X isn't its end here: ...". (3) The mark for a finish at low health was looked for by a part of its name, which a Phantom Edge's own mark holds (GE_CanLowHealthExecute_XueDiZi_C, on bosses at full health too): by its whole name now (GE_CanLowHealthExecute_C; `game.hasEffect`'s exact). (4) The mark comes up over the hold's floor (run184: on the copy with the blow from 150 to 70 of 28080), and the finishing move's one blow takes the copy from there to its floor; looked for at the hold's floor alone, it wasn't seen in time and the move went as a plain killing blow. Looked for from a quarter of the copy's health down, and asked once more at the blow itself; the host takes X on a boss that low, not only on one standing at its floor. (5) 394's free kill by another way: `LR` wrote the copy down to what the host had (2) whether a killing blow waited for that answer or not, and one told just before the host's world refreshed gets its LR after it, on the body the refresh made. Only for a blow that waits (the refresh forgets those). Sim: 7 checks, all fail before (guestsFinisher: a Phantom Edge's mark alone, the refused one's fight still the host's own, a scene in the host's world, a phase's floor, a phase to come, the blow from over the floor; restartWithOldLines: the late LR). four 2402, pvp 162, two unchanged, chaos 19. Made in the game on this build (run217, the trial's 7583e54): the host up and in the fight, kept whole; real F presses on the guest's window; three plain killing blows refused ("it has 1 left there"), then the guest's finishing move (GA_Hun_BEx_Common_PengMeng_V2), told as X and taken: dead in both worlds within a tenth of a second, the host alive, its fog walls open, the Dread Axe one each, 3 flow steps and 2 flags told, 9000 dust to the guest, no error line. Lei Dang's second form gave no reading (run221: the bench's staging).
397. A guest's late killing blow ended a boss that had just come back whole (the tenth review, two lenses, in its sim; the base did the same, so it is older than this round, and 383's fight starting over makes it likely: at every restart a guest is striking a copy at 2). A guest tells a killing blow as its copy stands at its floor. One told before the host's word of its world's refresh reached that guest (R goes half a second after the refresh itself, the hook's wait for the respawn step) is read by the host after the refresh, and handlers.D finds the enemy by name: the new, whole body. It made the game's own kill on it: the fresh boss dead in both worlds, his pay out, "beaten" in the host's save and the story on without his fight, by one plain blow. Two guards on the host. From its world's refresh (marked in pbz.lua's Refresh Monster hook itself, `game.refreshedHere`) till half a second after R has gone (`net.killsFrom`, the twin of a guest's half second, 394; set too where a party host's world refreshes for a guest's rest), a killing blow is read as a plain one (`net.lateKill`): a real one is told again 0.8 s on and taken then. And for ten seconds from the refresh (`net.worldNewAt`, NEW_BODY_S) a killing blow on a boss with more than half its health left in the host's world is a plain blow, answered with what the boss has (LR): a guest whose word of the refresh came late tells its blow again, up to four times 0.8 s apart, and no boss is brought from whole to its end in that time. A finishing move (X) goes by the same two, before any floor is lifted. And from the review's read of 396: the word that a boss falls only to a finishing move (FB, and the host's own tip) isn't given at a floor where a partner's finishing move isn't its end either, a phase's floor or one with another phase of it to come: told there, the guest made one, it wasn't taken, and the word came again two minutes on. Limits of `game.phaseAfter`, which goes by spawner names alone: a boss whose phases are one actor isn't told from one at its last point by it. Jing Ye, one spawner, the review's candidate, is covered by his floor instead (the bench, run222, the trial's 7583e54: GE_JingYe_LockHealth holds him at 8817 of 66125, a phase's floor; the guest's game does mark its copy of him for a finish at low health there, GE_CanLowHealthExecute_C, so a finishing move of the guest's would have come as X and been refused as "the floor there is a phase's"; none was offered in 110 s of real F presses), and the forest's YuCong_P3 bodies each count the others as a phase to come, so no partner's finishing move ends him: safe by 193, short of the user's rule if his end is one. Sim: restartWithOldLines, the guest's own last killing blow posted again as the host's world refreshes, as R goes, and seconds later (the boss whole, no K, nothing marked beaten), and a thug's inside the half second (plain) and past it (its death); guestsFinisher, no FB at a phase's floor or with a phase to come. four 2412, pvp 162, two unchanged, chaos 19. In the game (run224, the working tree): 383's fight starting over with the guest striking through the hold, the host respawned, the new bodies whole in both worlds, nothing paid and nothing said dead, no error line; no killing blow happened to be in flight across that restart, so the window itself stands on its sim.
398. Scenes and bells, together. The user, 11-18: "why don't you make the scene for both play at the same time i feel that is what people are asking for i mean if they were both in the same place of the boss. like a synced movie people want to watch together and react together. the same goes for bell if they are near to each other ... for bell you should also make sure if the guest presses it then also register for host if they are near each other after watching the synced movie same goes vise versa". Till now each world's game began a place's scene as its own player stood in the story's box for it: two players a few paces apart had it seconds apart, one standing back had it only as they walked on, the other's fight begun by then; and a bell's first ring was each player's own, the host's world taking no guest's bell at all. First the game was read on the bench (the trial's 7583e54). run219, the first map's Axe chief, Seq014, 39.5 s: the host's scene began 2.1 s after it stood in his box; the guest, set down at that spot, had its own 2 s after that; a sequence's own player takes SetPlayRate (2.0 for 4 s took the guest's from 4.8 s behind to 0.8 s ahead) and gives its place (GetCurrentTime); the scene moves its player 7 m in its first 0.6 s. run220, the bells: the bell's own OnInteract with a player, called by the mod, runs the game's whole ring in that world (InteractAfterMove, RestoreHealth, UpdateQuest, UpdateFlowTag, Restore BackPack Data, the save), the bell woken and lit after it, the player in hand, no menu left up; OnFirstTouchBell called alone wakes nothing; a real first ring of the camp's crane bell is OnInteract, InteractAfterMove 0.9 s on, the ring and its first touch 1 s on, and its scene 5.2 s after that. Now a scene that begins in one world is told with where its player stood just before it (SCENE on <name> <x> <y> <z> <map>; story.lua). A partner within 30 m of that spot, on that map, up and in nothing of their own (no scene, no boss fight of their own, no dialogue, QTE, menu or pause), whose world still has the story's trigger box within 15 m of the spot (`game.storyBox`: TriggerBox_<n>, made as the story runs, gone once its scene has played), is set down in that box and its own game begins the same scene: once a scene, never one its world has played since the game started, and with no box there nobody is moved (a limit: the box taken is the nearest of the story's within 15 m of the spot, which could be another scene's than the partner's). The scene begun later plays up to 1.5 times as fast till the two are a tenth of a second apart (SCENE at <name> <seconds>, a second apart; `game.scenePos`, `game.sceneRate`), and what it gained that way isn't told as a skip at its end. A first ring of a bell is told with where the bell stands and where its ringer stood (BELL ring; the first touch's hook now keeps the bell's place); a partner within 30 m of the bell, free as above, whose world has that bell asleep (`game.bellAt`), is set down a pace behind the ringer and their bell is rung for them by its own OnInteract: the game's first ring there too, with its flags, its save and a crane bell's scene, in the host's world as well (rung, not lit by word: its first ring's steps come). One further off keeps the bell for their own first ring, as before. Skips are as they were (the host's skips the guest's): whose skip should end a scene watched together is asked of the user. `WatchTogether = Off` in PhantomPact.ini keeps scenes and bells each player's own. Older builds ignore the new words (SCENE on / at, BELL): the protocol stands at 5. Sim: watchingTogether and bellsTogether. four 2453 (one older check counted every SCENE word the host sends, and counts its skips now), pvp 162, two unchanged, chaos 19, the events' check at 64 handlers and 59 names. Not in the game yet: sceneshare.ps1 and bellshare.ps1 are written for the bench.
399. The swing sounds' learner read a partner's weapon the moment this PC had loaded it. A report on 1.7.3 (Nexus, 11-19): "once we connected via steam it would crash almost immediately after connecting ... turning off Friend Damage Sound [PartnerHitSounds] in the files would let us make it past 6 seconds of connecting. It now lasts longer but still crashes at random times, ... roughly 20-30 min". Not reproduced, and no cause proven. Read: hitfx.lua is the same in 1.7.3 and here, and the one piece of work that setting adds which starts by itself at a link is the learner of 1.7.3's swing sounds: 25 ms after the partner's W or E, which every sync's start sends again, it reads their weapon's config, its sounds, its abilities, their timelines and every action of each, as soon as StaticFindObject finds the config: 0.7 s after moves.lua had loaded it on the bench (run126), 0.05 s in the sim. For that same weapon the figure is made to wait 6 s (358: its classes can still be on their way, and a call through nothing takes the game with it, no line in the log; a report on 1.7.3, the host's game gone seconds after a guest was back). The learner waits now: a weapon this PC had to load is read 10 s after its load (`moves.loadedAgo`), and none before the partner's figure is settled; a swing's sound keeps to the quiet after a load, as a hit's does. What else that report holds, read from the code and not benched: with the setting off, 1.7.3's crashes after 20 to 30 minutes fit 359, 360 and 381, fixed here; the boss fight one player walked into alone, and the other could never enter, is 326's start spent in the other world (327, 343, 345, 349, 352 and 365 here), or, the first player having won, the boss beaten for both by design; the key the guest never got is the host's story steps reaching the guest's save without the item (351 and 363 give a never-held story thing the host holds, at every sync) and a key door not shared (301, 319, 332). Open: a key the host has already spent is never given, and whether the door its word opens stays open in the guest's own save is not known. Sim: newWeaponWaits, 1 check, failing before (0.05 s after the load). four 2455, pvp 162, two unchanged, chaos 19. Not in the game: the crash was never made on the bench.
400. Scenes and bells together, the eleventh review's findings on 398 (six lenses, read only, on the trial's 7be346d: 21 findings, 6 high, none run-checked yet), taken before 398 saw the bench. Who is set down or rung for (`mayJoin`): not a player held down by Revive (down stays down: a partner's ring healed them, filled the gourd and cleared their down count while they were held), not one in or by a boss fight (their own; a partner's that is on, by its F and not by a bar here, which blinks up for seconds as the host comes to a boss, 344; one being watched), not one fighting anything (the game itself refuses a bell in combat), and only one on their feet. Which scenes are told as begun: not one inside a boss fight (a grab, a phase's) or within 15 s of a boss's fall, which both worlds have by the fight itself and which set a partner down in the boss's own box inside the walls, and not a bell's own. Which box: `game.storyBox` goes by the box's own size and turn, not its middle (the Axe chief's is 43 m by 36, its middle further than the old 15 m from where a player steps in), takes the one box that holds the place told or else the only one within 15 m of it, and takes none when two or more are in reach and not just one holds it (in Zhengdaodaying's underground the story's boxes stand 6 to 12 m apart, the sword puppet's 6 m from the last). The player is set down where the partner stood when the box holds that place, else at the box's nearest point a pace inside, and only onto ground (`game.floorUnder`, a line down: run103's set-down from 34 m off fell through ground that wasn't loaded), their movement stopped, and watched 3 s: one who still drops is put back where they stood. A world whose story is past the teller's isn't set down at all (the telling carries the teller's point: a box can stay after its scene has played, run101, and stood in again it played a second time). The pace: only for a scene begun together (set down for it, or told and begun within 6 s of each other), by the furthest on of the partners in it (a place kept for each), put back to its own speed when the scene ends or changes, and looked at each frame's own way, so a raised rate isn't left through the quiet after a stall. Bells: not rung over a ring of this player's own; a ring is told only while it's fresh (one made with sync off came at the next link with where the ringer stood by then); no set-down behind the ringer without ground there, and then no ring; a ring made for a partner's counts as a bell's own scene in both skip tests. pbz.lua's words on the mod's ring say what run220 showed and what it didn't: the bell woken and its sit run, but no first touch seen, that bell having been sent OnFirstTouchBell alone before. Left as they are, and known: the QTE guard of the pace doesn't hold on a host; WatchTogether is each PC's own; a friend's first ring rests the partner as a ring of their own would (health, the gourd, Revive's count, the save, the respawn's place), against the rule of 11-12 that a partner's sit rests nobody outside a boss fight: asked of the user. Sim: watchingTogether and bellsTogether, 14 checks failing before (main.lua's PBZ now holds revive, for them). four 2481, pvp 162, two unchanged, chaos 19, the events' check clean. Not in the game yet: sceneshare.ps1 and bellshare.ps1 wait for the bench, the second with a verdict line on the first touch of a ring the mod makes.
401. 397's guard on a whole boss went by a clock (REVIEW-10: a high its checkers made three times, and a medium). 397 took a killing blow on a boss with more than half its health as a plain one for ten seconds from the host's refresh: wall-clock time. A guest whose link went quiet both ways for longer than that around the host's respawn kept striking its copy at 2, each strike a killing blow; the relay holds the sure events and sends them all as the link comes back, both windows over by then, and the new, whole boss died (the review's sim: 11.5 s quiet, K 15 times, 0 of 7500, "Beaten"). The same with the host's own game standing still past the ten seconds after its R; with a boss made whole with no refresh at all, by the game's own reset when it lost its target (the bench, run223: 2 back to 27680); and with a sit's reset of a host-run boss (net.sitBossesToFull), where a third player's blow at its floor, told before they heard of the sit, took him straight back to it. The rule goes by the blow now, for all time: a killing blow on a boss with more than half its health left in the host's world, by a blow that took less than half of what it has, is a plain blow, and LR says what it has. The sender's copy stood at its floor and this one doesn't: a body that's gone, however it went. A blow that took it whole is still its end. net.lateKill's half second after a refresh stays, for every enemy. Sim: restartWithOldLines, the old body's killing blows posted a quarter of a minute after the refresh: 1 check, failing before (0 of 7500). four 2485 with 402's, pvp 162, two unchanged, chaos 19, the events' check clean. 402. Then both games began the boss's fight 0.8 ms apart, each told it (B), and each took the other's word: "The other player started a boss fight", the walls shut and "press E at the black wall" on both, no boss of its own in either world. On the host that is the state announceBoss already undoes for a guest who began first: with the fight filed as the guest's, that guest's F 0 takes the host's bar down and opens its walls mid-fight, and a guest's rest refreshes the host's world. Scenes that end together make it the common case. 403. A partner's bell, rung by the mod, had no first ring; a crane's bell so rung loses its scene; and that scene is two (the bench, 11-19, run228 to run231; the eleventh review's #5). 398 rang the partner's bell by the bell's own `OnInteract`, called from the mod. run228, the trial's 5a43949, a guest's real ring of the camp's crane bell with the host 6 m off: in the host's world that call walked the host up, ran the sit's steps (RestoreHealth, UpdateQuest, UpdateFlowTag, the save) and left the bell woken, but no first touch came at all, no PlayRingSound, no OnFirstTouchBell, and no crane's scene: the bell was spent there with no first ring. The ring is asked of the player's own interact component now, as E asks it (`SetCurrentInteractActor`, `DoInteract`: what local co-op does to rest player 1 at a bell for player 2), and it is said when the game takes none (busy with something of their own: the bell stays theirs). run230, that way at the crane bell: the host's own PlayRingSound and OnFirstTouchBell, 2.8 s after the guest's, the sit's steps, and then the bell's menu, as after any sit: the crane's scene never came there, and of the flag and three flow steps a real ring of it tells (run220), one flow step. A crane's first ring by the mod is a bell spent with its scene unseen and the story's steps not made, in the host's world too, which takes none by word. So a bell with a scene of its own (`BP_MelodiousBell_StoneCrane_C`) is never rung for a partner: its first ring stays each player's own, and it is said. When each rings it within 30 s of the other (the ring's word is kept, whoever is near), the parts of its scene are kept together, the later a little faster (keepPace; no "on" is told of a bell's scene, so it's known by the rings). run231, a plain bell (the start's own, asleep in both worlds), the guest's real E with the host 2 m off: the mod's ring in the host's world ran the game's own first ring, step for step (OnInteract, InteractAfterMove, PlayRingSound, OnFirstTouchBell, the sit's steps, the save, the travel map), and left the world as the guest's ring left theirs (no flag, no flow step, the bell). The crane bell rung by each 19.6 s apart, the second ring the user's own hand at that window: the later one's story scene "19.6 s behind your partner's: played a little faster", level 41 s on, the two ends 0.1 s apart, the same two flags left in both worlds. run232, the guest's real ring with the host 1.5 m off: "a bell with a scene of its own ... its first ring stays yours", the host's bell left asleep, the host not moved, no set-down for the guest's second part. run233, the host's own ring watched whole: the second part's own end "ended 205.1 s into its sequence, which plays on: the camera's part was over, no skip", nothing told (on the form before the review's fixes, which looked from where the scene began in its sequence: the same reads of the game). What that scene is, from the same runs: one sequence of 213.2 s in two parts, the crane's own for its first 18 s, the player in hand again, and from 40.6 s in the story's scene at the camp, till 8 s before the sequence's end: a second scene by the same name. Only a scene within 10 s of the first touch was the bell's to the mod, so the second was an ordinary one. It was told as begun (run228: the host, 1 m off, "set down in the story's box for it"). And watched whole by a host it was told as skipped (run231: "skipped after 164.5 of 213.2 s: the guest's too"; a scene's length is asked of its whole sequence): a guest who rang later had the end of their own cut, in 1.7.3 too. A part of a bell's sequence is known by the sequence's name now, for five minutes from its first, and none is told as begun. A scene that ends with its sequence playing on from where it was at the last look (`game.sequencePos`, of that sequence by name and only while its player plays, within 2 s of where plain playing puts it) is over by itself, no skip: a skip stops the sequence or sends it to its end. For every scene. The first part's end is no skip, as since 1.7.3; the second is the story's: a host's real skip of it is told and ends the guest's (the user, 11-19, asked whose skip ends a scene watched together: "Only the host's"). A scene's speed is put back to its own with the camera on the player too (a part that ended while still played faster left its sequence fast through the 22 s to the next). And one who stood by and was neither set down nor rung for is told why in the log, once (`mayJoin` says which: down, a boss fight, a fight, a talk, not on their feet or a menu up): run229's host had died as the bench placed it and stood among the enemies its death brought back, rightly left alone, and nothing said so. A first review of the working tree, read only, was taken in before the commit, its six findings each checked against the code: a raised speed stood when a part ended as the frame's own look saw the end first (the sim: 1 of 6 ends a twentieth of a second apart), its sequence fast through the 22 s to the next part and all of that part; a scene's place and speed are read and set by its sequence's name (the player found can be the last scene's, still known); with three players the pace goes by the partners who rang with this player, two who ring together long after the first no longer sent after the first; the end's look goes by the place last read (`tog.seen`), not by a sum of the seconds gained, which runs short. Sim: the fake bell has its first touch only through the interact component, as the game's; bellsTogether, 26 new checks, 14 failing before. Not done: a crane's scene begun for a partner by the mod (what a real E starts and the component's call doesn't is not known). four 2511, pvp 162, two unchanged, chaos 19, the events' check clean.
404. What the reviews of 398 to 403 found and checked, before 1.7.4 (REVIEW-10's V1, S1 and F1; the review of 401 and 402; the eleventh review's second stage). (1) The play log kept a partner's Steam name that begins with "<" ("<3 Mika", a clan's "<WB>Mika"), or with digits the address rule had rewritten: such a name was taken for one of the mask's own words and written as it is, in the relay's line once a minute and in the panel's, in a file the guides ask players to attach to public reports (they say it "holds no address, name or password"). Only the mask's own words are left now, each matched whole ("<Steam ID>", an address's kind). Checked by four checkers before it came here. (2) 401's rule was beaten by a gap's own blows, and 401's words here that run223's reset is covered were wrong. The rule judges one killing blow as it is read (a boss over half, a blow under half of that). A guest whose link goes quiet keeps striking a copy that is lifted back to the host's last line each second, and the relay keeps every blow, plain and killing, till the link is back. Read in their order, the plain ones took the boss under half and the next killing blow was his end in both worlds, his pay out and "beaten" in both saves: with no refresh at all, the host down and its game healing the boss as the guest's figure went quiet (run223; a reset is under half in its first seconds anyway), and on the new body of a respawn the gap itself caused (the guest taken for gone at 12 s, the host's hold let go). Two rules on the host, nothing on the wire: a killing blow read from a guest not heard for 3 s, or heard again less than 1.5 s ago, is a plain blow (`net.gapKill`), answered with what the boss has (LR) so the guest's game stops telling it; and what a guest told of its blows in a gap in which the host's world refreshed isn't taken at all (`net.gapOld`): the sim's new body had lost 6300 of 7500 to the old one's blows before its fight began. A finishing move (X) is not judged by the gap (the user: a guest's F must count); handlers.D's own checks hold it. A guest's own kill of an enemy it ran alone (GK) counts as it did: the relay gives it to a party's other guests whatever the host does, and refused by the host alone the worlds would disagree. The exact way, each guest answering the refresh (RA), was not taken: a guest on an older build of PROTOCOL 5 never answers, and a 1.7.4 host would drop every blow of theirs after its first rest. A gap's plain blows with no refresh in it still land, the guest's own damage on the body that's there. (3) The host's answer to a killing blow (LR) was written on a copy let go since by another way than a refresh (the host up at a boss its game holds at a floor walks off, its lines of him run out, the copy stands whole again): 2 with no floor, and the next blow was his death in the guest's world alone, told to a party's other guests. Only a copy still held is written (`net.heldAlive`). (4) 399's wait did nothing before a partner's figure exists: `moves.settled`, asked of no figure, says yes, and at a link the weapon's word comes about a second before the figure (run126), so a weapon this PC already had was read at 1.7.3's moment. No figure is not settled now. (The sim makes a figure anew in the same frame, so its check passes before and after: read, not shown.) (5) 402 was the host's alone: with three or more, a guest filed the fight under another guest whose word came after the host's (through the host, and again whenever someone joins), and that guest's drop or F 0 took this player's own bar down and opened its walls mid-fight. A guest keeps a fight it has as the host's, or has begun itself. And a guest whose own game began the fight as the host's did is no longer told to press E at a black wall it stands inside. Left: a fight of guests only is still filed under one of them by the host. (6) From the eleventh review's second stage, each read against the code first: the story box taken as "the only one in reach" is taken only if its middle is in reach too, as before 400 (a talk's scene told 10 m outside an arena's box set the partner down in it, the boss's scene and fight begun in their world alone); a ring isn't asked of the game for a partner who is using something just now, the same bell by their own E among it (`GetInteractingActor`); a scene skipped for the host's skip is put to its own speed first; and how far a partner's scene or ring is comes before mayJoin's searches of all the game's objects, which ran four times a second for every scene a partner began, however far off. Not taken, for after 1.7.4: a guest counted in a fight by copies that only noticed it is still left alone for a scene or a ring (said in the log since 403); the QTE guard that never holds on a host; fight scenes not kept in pace. Sim: thePlayLog 1 new check; restartWithOldLines 8 (the review's reset.lua and its gone path as checks, a hiccup of 2.5 s, the late answer at ten timings); watchingTogether 5, bellsTogether 1, partnerSwingSounds 1; 12 of the 16 fail on 7d04e55, the others guard (the hiccup, the skip's speed, the figure, one that follows). Not in the game: no bench run, the gaps were made in the sim. Suites: four 2546, pvp 162, chaos 19, two 19, events 64 handlers and 59 names, lint 39 files 0 problems, compile 39.
