408. A boss its opening scene had only just made was told as dead, and that scene wasn't shared (the user's own play logs of 11-19, a host alone on test build ally2: "Enemy died here: BP_Monster_HookMan_C_...; telling the other player." a tenth of a second before "a scene begins: Seq022"; the same at his second phase's Seq023, with "up again here after its fall: still a fight" 0.3 s on; the same for the tutorial's boss at Seq003 in the morning's log). shareEnemy takes no health for dead, and an actor whose numbers aren't set yet reads none: it was told that turn, under its actor's own name, which names nothing on a guest. But it counted as a boss's fall (net.bossFellAt), and a scene within FELL_S of a fall isn't told (story.lua's fellLately): the opening scene of such a boss, of all scenes, wasn't told to a partner standing by, who wasn't set down for it (398, the user's ask of 11-18); flowsync's limit was a win's for those seconds; and the second phase was called a fight a whole scene before it began. A death is of one seen alive now: an enemy read as dead that this world hasn't seen alive at that address, under that id, in the last 5 s (net.livedAt, livedAs: written each turn it's alive, forgotten at a rest or a death of this player's and at a map's change) is a body only once it has lain 3 s (net.metDead), and nothing is said of it till then. One seen alive that dies is told that turn, as before. watchingTogether has it (four.lua: on the scripts before, the boss is told dead, the fall counted, the scene not told and the guest not set down: all four checks fail there). In the game, on this build (run248, run249): an enemy seen alive and killed on the host was told 18 ms and 35 ms after the blow, the second a minute after the host's own death, the guest hearing of it 3 and 8 ms on. And on the build before (run247): the host's player killed twice with every monster of the world looked at every frame: the 19 that a death makes anew each came with their health and a controller on their first frame, under their actor's name, none at an address seen before. So no enemy of a rest reads none: it's the scenes' own bosses that do, which the bench can't start; the bomb man told a second after the host was up, in the same log, was a real death. Not covered: a boss whose numbers stay unset for over 3 s is told then (its scene told by then); a guest's side tells a copy read as dead only when this player hit it or their game runs it (syncEnemy), and is left as it was.
409. The stutter about every 20 s was Lua's own collector (the same play log, 64 minutes in the world: 178 ten-second stretches with a single step of the mod's over 25 ms, 79 of them over 40, 27 over 50, the most 56; 172 of the 178 are the stretches where Lua's memory fell by over 5 MB, sawing 46 to 92 MB; a frame of 40 to 80 ms each time, through the hook man's seven tries too. The mod never asks for a collection). On the bench, two linked copies standing at the start bell, every step of every stage timed for 85 s under each setting (gcp.lua, through net.profile; gcrun.ps1, gcrun2.ps1, gcrun3.ps1; run247 to run249). As Lua ships (a round begins when memory has doubled, pause 200): the heap 10 to 45 MB, 4 to 8 steps over 25 ms in the 85 s, the longest 33 to 53 ms. At pause 150: 6 to 9 MB, the steps 17 to 26 ms, about 10 of it the slow look's own, in whose call a round falls since it makes the most. At pause 110: 3.7 to 4.1 MB, which is what the mod really holds (all the rest is garbage, most of it the objects UE4SS makes for every game object looked at, each with something to close: the collector's one step that can't be split pays for those by their number), steps of 12 to 16 ms, and the mod at 110 to 117 ms a second for 76: sooner than by half costs more than it saves. A slower collector changes nothing at pause 200 (step 25 or 50 for 100: the long step is the one that can't be split) and little at 150 (14 to 20 ms), and at step 12 it never caught up (the heap past 200 MB in 85 s, steps of 82 and 110 ms: the manual's own warning); pause 300 made the heap 130 MB and the steps 42 to 68 ms; Lua's generational way was worse (17 steps over 25 ms in 85 s on the host, one of 74 on the guest). (run248's own sums a second were taken with the four-player suite running beside it: only its steps are to go by.) main.lua sets pause 150 as it loads (the incremental collector, its step left at 100; the old setter for a Lua with no such word). joining has it (four.lua: the pause read back; 200 on the scripts before). As built (run249): the pause reads 148 on both copies (the game's Lua keeps it in quarters); and in the same run, the same copies, as built against 200: steps over 25 ms 1 and 1 (26, 25 ms) against 6 and 8 (53, 48, 42, 40; 38, 36, 34, 34); frames over 50 ms none against 5 and 7; the longest frame 43 and 42 ms against 75 and 68; the mod's time 87.3 and 99.9 ms a second against 86.9 and 96.6. What's left is the slow look's own 10 ms with a partner linked (4 to 5 alone, by the play log) and the garbage itself, some 2 MB a second: the frame cost's item.
410. A rider's horse wasn't there for a guest, and its rider's copy slid off it (players on 1.7.4, 11-20: "some bosses like (Jing Ye, the Outlaw) do not appear to the guest and if they do byt clicking (F3) ... the horse boss riding doesn't appear to guest, only host can see it, same goes for some others"). The bench, run250, a late guest at Jing Ye with both worlds' horses and bosses listed every 2 s (bench/rpt.lua): a mount is an enemy of its own (BP_Monster_Horse_JingYe_C, 1800 health; the riders' BP_Monster_Horse_MonsterHorse_C too), its rider attached to it, and its spawner is one the game makes as it runs, with no designer id and an actor name of that session's alone: BP_AISpawner_C_2147456939 on the host, BP_AISpawner_C_2147449150 on the guest. pbz.lua named a spawner with no id by its own name, the level's, which both PCs share, and this one's the same way: "sp:" and a name no other PC has. To coop.lua that is an id: the guest's own horse, unpaired for 3 s by the host (by class it pairs only within 20 m of the host's), was asked after (HAS), the host had none of that name (NOT), and the horse was put out of the way, hidden and parked up high, for as long as it stood by the host, its rider's copy riding nothing; the host's horse meanwhile was asked of this world's spawners every 10 s (bring.missing), which none answers to. Second, the rider's copy was steered to the host's line of it by itself: off its saddle by 0.3 to 2 m at any look of that run, and back onto it with a jump of 3 to 6 m as each move began, ten times in 100 s, none of them the host's. Now: a name the game gave as it ran is no id (`game.runtimeName`: a number of 2e9 or more at its end and no "_UAID_"; the engine counts such names down from 2147483647), so an enemy from such a spawner keeps its actor's name, this PC's alone (`net.ownName`): never asked after, never hidden for a "no", never asked of the spawners; a host says nothing to an older guest's HAS for such a name, so that guest's horse stays too. What a paired copy rides (`game.mountOf`, GetAttachParentActor) is paired with the host's enemy of that class that has no id, however far off (asked only while such a line has no copy, twice a second at most). A rider whose mount is a copy is left on it, unsteered and unturned (puppets.lua's `p.rides`, looked at every 0.5 s; for its first second left on a mount not paired yet): its mount's own line moves the two. And what a rider this game runs rides (handed over, 263, or asleep on a party's host, 277: `handover.rides`) is this game's own too, never paired: paired, it was held and steered to where the host's own stood asleep, the rider on it. Sim: `aRiderAndItsMount`, 19 checks, 11 failing on 0b1bc7d, where it shows the whole chain ("sp:BP_AISpawner_C_2147456939", HAS, NOT, hidden, never paired, 30 m off). The build in the game (run252): each horse by its actor's own name (the host's BP_Monster_Horse_JingYe_C_2147452327, the guest's ..._2147455171). The guest's own horse, its rider on it, was set 40 m off and held in the air, and the guest came in late by F3: paired with the host's within the second, 18 cm from it, never hidden, never asked after; from then on the guest's horse 0.1 to 3 m from the host's as they gallop, and the guest's rider within 1 cm of its horse second by second, as the host's is (53 and 71 cm in the two seconds of a move that lifts him off the saddle: 49 and 69 on the host in the same two). Its pictures have no boss in view (the guest stood turned away): the proof is the two worlds' lists. Left: a mount is never handed over with its rider (a handover goes by a name both share: the old names made it flap, "their game runs it now" and "this game's to run again" nine times each in the bench's old logs), so a host that only watches a guest's fight with a mounted boss sees the rider's copy off the host's own idle horse.
411. A boss's teleport reached a guest half a second late (the same players: "boss teleport doesn't sync up for guest very well. Makes it near impossible for guest to fight on certain fights where boss is technically spamming teleport"). The bench, run251, A-Hua with the guest in late, her place every frame on both copies (rpt.lua's RPF lines, rpf.py): her teleport moves are dashes of 5 to 6 m at 75 to 90 m a second, 150 to 390 cm between two lines of a thirtieth of a second, where her fastest plain attack goes 90 and a charging horse 140; two more blink 14 and 17 m at once. puppets.lua set a copy to its line only past SNAP_CM, 10 m, which covered the blinks (there in 0.03 s); nearer, the copy was walked, at 14 m a second at most, and nudged 8% a frame: 3.9 to 6.9 m behind the host's boss after each dash and 0.52 to 0.66 s in coming, her blow struck meanwhile from where the host had her. (A copy that plays the move itself, its own game having started it, made the same dash in step: 1.6 m off at most, there in 0.23 s.) Now readEnemies marks a jump on a line's record: a step of over 150 cm from the line before, and either faster than 45 m a second or from standing still to standing still (a blink after a quiet quarter second); steer sets the copy to the line at once and for 0.35 s after whenever it is over 120 cm from it, the line itself, not carried forward (at a dash's speed a late line would carry it metres past the end). One under 4 m ahead of the line on the jump's own way is left: its own dash, a link's delay before the host's line of it, and set back each frame it would stutter. A rider is its mount's to move (410). No new word on the wire: an older partner's lines are read the same. Sim: `anEnemysJump`, 11 checks, 6 failing on 0b1bc7d (a 6.5 m dash: 0.70 s there, 0.07 s here). The build in the game (run252, the same fight, 50 s with the guest's copy a figure that only follows the host's lines): two dashes, nine such steps, the copy 2.2 m off at most and within 60 cm 0.14 s after a step at the latest; all through those 50 s it stood 3 cm off in the middle and 96 at the 99th percentile (run251: 31 and 564). Over the run's 117 s, 28 steps, 0.26 s at the latest. (A guest set beside her starts its own scene of her; its copy is hidden then and its own scene moves it: nothing of that is seen, and nothing here changes it.)
412. Two riders' horses could be paired crosswise (found reading 410 back; no player's report). A mount goes by no id both PCs share, so 410 still paired it by kind and nearness first, within 20 m, and by its rider only if that left it unpaired. With two riders of one camp (the spear riders of Pangzhen, both horses BP_Monster_Horse_MonsterHorse_C) the nearer of this world's two can be the other rider's, for a guest come late most of all, its own two wherever its game had them; and each rider's copy is left on its mount since 410, so each rider stood where the other's is. Now a mount is paired by its rider before any pairing by kind: what a copy of the host's rides here (`puppets.ridden`, kept as each copy is asked what it rides, no new call) is the host's enemy of that kind, with no id, whose line is within 5 m of the host's line of that rider: a rider is attached to its mount, so their two lines say one place. Whatever that line or this mount was paired with before is let go, so a crosswise pairing made in the round before the rider was known rights itself the round after. 410's own pass after the pairing by kind is gone. Sim: `twoRidersAndTheirMounts`, 3 checks, all failing on 410's code (each horse 6 m off, where the other's stands on the host); `aRiderAndItsMount` unchanged and passing. Not in the game: run252 was 410's pass, with one rider.
413. PartnerHitSounds and the game gone: "when PartnerHitSounds is enabled, every time a player swings/attacks, the other player crashes", "one player stays still, the other swings ... the player who is stationary will crash immediately. Running/moving is fine" (Nexus, 19 and 22 Nov, on 1.7.4, two players; the same from two more since 1.7.3, the first build with swing sounds: with only hit sounds, 1.7.1 and 1.7.2, nobody said it). Never on the bench, on the same game and UE4SS builds: eight weapons learned, hundreds of swings and hits played, both by the call on the actor and by the one at its place (run253). UE4SS's own source at this build's commit shows the empty table handed for the sound call's callback is a delegate bound to nothing, so not that. Its cause is not known. What a mod can do about a call the game doesn't come back from, hitfx.lua's guard: what is handed to the game is checked for its class first (a sound event, a Niagara system, a scene component, an actor: by `IsA`, a class the game hasn't got checking nothing, as before); and each kind of call (a partner's swing sound on their figure, a hit's sound on an enemy, its blood, the hit-effect chooser's answer, the reading of a partner's weapon) leaves a note in hitfx_guard.txt beside the mod's files before its first six of a session and takes it out 2 s on. A session that finds a note left was ended inside that call, or by what it set off: a sound is posted at the actor's place from then on (`PostEventAtLocation`: no component of the sound engine's made on the body, no callback), and after a session ended in that too not at all; blood, the chooser and the reading have one step, off; a weapon whose reading ended a session is passed over, two of them and none is read. The log of the session after names the call and the sound, the setting's own line says what is held back, and deleting the file starts over (so does a later build that makes these calls another way: `GUARD_EPOCH`). So a PC it happens on has it once for a kind, twice at most, and the rest of co-op stays. Nothing new goes over the wire, no new text on the screen. In the game, run253: the guest set to the place's call played 15 hits and 58 swing sounds by it (this game's takes the event, a place, a turn and the world's object: found by trying the two shapes it has had, the other refused before any call), the host 10 and 44 on actors; the notes were in each file while a kind's first six ran and the file was gone after; no class missing; no error. four.lua: partnerHitSounds +14 and partnerSwingSounds +10 checks, 19 of them failing on ef7e8b4.
414. Local co-op, the endless fall (Nexus bug "splitscreen glitch", 20 Nov, on 1.7.4, with its play log): both players in Mu Xiaokui's fight, player 1 killed. The game itself took player 1 3164 m off within 1.5 s and had them up there, "the white place": no floor under them, nor 190 m further on. They fell 29 m, were down from the fall a second later, were brought back to the same place by the mod's own respawns ("Both players are down: back to the bell", the game's Respawn by hand, and player 1's revival beside player 2), fell again, and so on for the eight minutes the log runs, player 2 taken along each time. The bench, runs 255 to 265, both players down far from their bell: Respawn called by hand sets player 1 down at once where its world isn't in, as a scene of the game's own (its cutscene word is on from 0.3 s after), and 1.4 s on moves them 21 m to where they rise; they fell 98 m through it and died of the fall, and the game's own respawn after that death stood them at the bell, whole, 6 s on. What a line down finds in a world's first seconds is no ground: the land's far look comes in first (set on it 3 s after a 4 km move, player 1 fell through), and under that bell something lies 47 m down that never holds. So after any far move at once (over 30 m between two looks, ten a second), for 12 s: a player 1 who falls 1.5 m from where the move left them is put back there and held still (MOVE_None), player 2 with them (their own far move's hold no longer ends by the clock meanwhile). Held, they stay the game's to move: moved a little they are held where it put them, set falling again they are held again, stood up by it that ground is tried. Four times a second a floor is looked for within 15 m under them and they are set on it to try it: standing on it a second, it is the ground; fallen a metre through it, they are held again and it is tried again a second on. A far move of the game's lets them go; 20 s with no ground takes them back, once, to where player 1 last stood on the ground, held and tried there the same way; nobody is let fall by the clock. Not in a scene: the game's own respawn is one and has its own way through (above); held through it instead (run265) player 1 stood at the bell 4.4 s on with no second death, but with the 76 health that respawn gives before its rest and player 2 still down, so it is left alone; the report's loop has no scene on in its log. A fall in play, with no far move before it, is the game's own. In the game, run256 and 266: put 4 km off where there is nothing, both held unhurt for 20 s, taken back, the first floor found there given up as it didn't hold and the ground stood on some seconds later; put 870 m off over ground not in yet, held till it was and set down standing, player 2 beside them. Not proven in the game: the report's own place and boss (what the white place is, and whether the game goes on from it once player 1 isn't killed there, isn't known). four.lua: localVoid, 17 checks.
415. Local co-op: a player 2 off alone woke what the story brings (the same report: Mu Xiaokui "had all the phases at once ... the boss names filled the screen", and "a random poison butcher knife enemy in iron fists boss fight arena"). The lone-player-2 waking claims every spawner within 60 m of them that may spawn and has nothing out: a boss's later phases too (MuXiaoKuiP2_Seq046new stands by her meadow), and a scene's enemies. Those named for a sequence ("_Seq") or a later phase (`game.isLaterPhase`) are left for the game to bring for player 1. four.lua: localStorySpawners, 2 checks, both failing on ef7e8b4. Sim only.
416. Local co-op: player 2's stick let go and the game not told (Nexus, 22 Nov: "when player two moves or their camera moves the game doesn't register when the joystick has returned to neutral resulting in the camera spinning or the character running endlessly"). A player hears a stick only as it moves: the one event that says "back in the middle" comes once, and whatever takes it on its way (something on the screen that answers the pads for a moment and is no menu to the mod) leaves player 2's controller with its last push for good. The mod let go of held input at a menu's open and close only (1.7.3's report, player 1 running on). Now main.dll says in pads.txt whether a pad's sticks are at rest (a second word on each line, read by the first digit as before by older scripts), and with player 2's at rest for 0.3 s while the game still has one of theirs pushed past 0.35 (`GetInputAnalogKeyState`), what their controller holds is let go, once a rest (looked at again a second in). Not with an older main.dll, nor a player 2 on the keyboard. Not seen on a pad here (the bench has none): by the sim alone, localLetGo, 7 checks; main.dll builds.
417. A guest's copy had no death to see, and a guest's finishing move (E) was cut short (the players on 1.7.4: "enemies when they die they are like glitched for guest and finish combat with e also glitched for some weapons effects"). The bench, run253, frame by frame on both copies: the host's own enemy dies in `MaleThug_Throw_Dead_Normal_F_Montage`, 1.7 s; its copy on the guest was ended at the host's word by the game's kill effect, which has no blow behind it and plays no fall: its flinch stopped, no montage, down from one frame to the next. A copy the guest was executing (`AM_MaskAssassin_BEd_Tang_M_01` under the player's `AM_Hun_BEx_Tang_M_01`) was ended as the execution's blow took its last health, half a second into a paired move of four. Now, before the kill (coop.lua's handlers.K, `net.dieLater`): a copy in an execution of this player's own is left in it till the game's own state for it ends (left for its montage's length it got up again, at 3.4 s of 6.1: run261); any other plays its kind's death montage for a second (the fall; the rest of it is the body lying), found by the flinch it is in or was last told in: `<kind>_Hit_<how>_Montage` has `<kind>_Dead_Normal_<F|B>_Montage`, and `_Dead_Launch_` for one in the air, beside it or one folder up (the bench's lists, run254 and 261), only one this game has loaded, and none for a flinch of a body already down. Through the show the copy keeps its floor and its place in the round's held (left out, releaseHeld lifted the floor and the player's next blow ended it there and then, with its own pay: run259), is not weighed, asked after or put aside, and every 0.03 s a flinch or move that came on it is taken off and its fall goes on from where it was (run260: it stood up out of its fall at the player's next blow). Then the kill as before; told dead again meanwhile, nothing more; one gone meanwhile is ended when it is next met. One with nothing to show (never flinched, a kind with no such montage) is ended at once, as before. In the game, run262: the host's kill, the guest's kill and the guest's execution, each shown (1.0 s, 1.0 s, 2.5 s) and then ended, a flinch and a move taken off the fall within 34 ms, no error line. Not done: the host's own view of a partner's kill (its enemy still drops with no fall), and bosses, whose whole death runs as before. four.lua: aCopysDeath, 22 checks.
418. (Pull request 19, by YCCCode.) A full-screen pickup card with no confirm prompt, play stopped behind it (their report: the Broken Illusion Pipa, `I_02001`). Their log: the host got the pipa as the guest's share, then the host's own scene gave it again; the story-item rule took the second one back, but by then both grants had driven the pickup screen. Now the shared pipa goes into the bag with no card of its own (its flag raised as before; the scene's card is the game's own); a partner's find's card is not put up over a card that is on screen, whatever the bag says, nor in a scene or a link's first quiet seconds, and a try that found a card up isn't counted (`game.pickupCard.onScreen`, `game.showPickupTips`); and a card the bag still holds that has shown no focus button for 3 s, the player still, gets the bag's own `ReadyToPlayConfirmUI` once more, once a card (`game.watchPickupCard`: a look every 2 s, every second while a card is up). Nothing closes a card but the player: 90's closer goes (`game.closeStuckCards`: a boss's card with no focus button and the bag not showing it, closed 3 and 6 s after a boss was ended here at the host's word), the pull request's own first try at a wider one having closed cards before their player confirmed. Why that is right, in the game, run267: a card that closes at Space had no focus button at any look (on a window that isn't in front), and a card put up the game's way for a partner's find, or by the bench's own two calls, had the bag's flag down while it was up on the host: the closer's two signs are a usable card's too. A card with no way out, reported again, needs another sign. Also run267, two copies: the look costs 0.5 ms; an elite's own drop card got the one extra call and closed at the real Space; a partner's key came up as a card 2 s after its share and closed at Space; the guest's pipa reached the host with no card, the host's own find of it after showed one card and was kept to one; no error line. four.lua: pickupCardRecovery, 12 checks, 5 of them failing on 1.7.5.
419. (Pull request 19, by YCCCode.) The Gold-Masked One at the Dragon Pillar (`sp:MoTianOld_Seq018-Mask`) ignored whoever came second: they couldn't hurt him, and he never turned to them. Their log, a guest-first fight: the host crossed the fog wall and took the boss over (joined, or a blow landed: `handover.hostIn`) about 1.5 s before the host's own encounter began, on a copy its own sequence hadn't started; in the sim an idle copy stayed off the enemy's side, and the boss the guest ran never had the host on its hatred list. For this fight alone, and two players: joining or striking no longer hands it to the host, only the host's own bar does; while the guest's game runs it, that game puts the host's ghost on the boss's hatred list each second and steers its target between the two as a host does (`aggroParty` from `handover.share`, the host's blows counted for it by `game.partnerHit`), and lets its picker go at the handover; and the late player's idle copy, the host's mirror or the guest's, goes on the enemy's side and team while it follows the fight (`game.enemySide`'s second argument: an idle one too, side and team each set only where it differs; `game.ownSide` puts it back, and a copy this game then starts for itself keeps what it has). four.lua: dragonPillarBoss, 14 checks, 6 of them failing on 1.7.5. By the sims alone: the bench has no way to that story fight (its spawner is made by the sequence), and the author's own check with two game clients was pending at the merge.
420. (Pull request 20, by XxaymanXx. It was built on 1.7.4 and uploaded over 1.7.5's scripts, so taken as it came it undid 413 to 419; merged instead file by file against v1.7.4, 420 to 433, with what is said at each and at the end.) A door opened at a partner's word stayed shut and solid ("when he opens a door it stays closed for me", their note of 11-23): the game's `SwitchWithoutPlaySequence` flips `DoorOpen` without moving the door, and read after the call a shut door read as open. Its state is read before the call now, and one that was still shut has its solid parts made unseen and passable (blockers.lua's `syncedDoors`), put back when a look finds the door shut again; a new map starts over.
421. (Pull request 20.) A host's enemy's projectiles were not in the guest's world unless its copy there ran the ability itself: an archer or a boss loosed nothing on the guest's screen. The host tells each enemy's bullet (`BSHOT`, the quick lane: the enemy, the bullet's data, where and how it flies; `game.enemyShotOwner`, mob and player bullet folders), and the guest fires it from its own copy of that enemy, so it flies and hits there as the game's own (from the boss within 60 m if the enemy isn't paired, else from the host's figure); not if that copy started an ability itself in the last 2 s (`puppets.abilityRecent`: its own bullets are coming). Refused and capped as a player's `SHOT` is. four.lua, shots: an enemy's comes up on the guest from its copy, a guest's copy's own goes nowhere.
422. (Pull request 20.) A partner's Phantom Edge on their figure. The game's `SetWeaponForceHide` takes four arguments and moves.lua passed three, known since 1.7.0 and left (hiding a figure's Phantom Edge for a partner with none never worked): four now, the slot's current weapon too. The weapon is drawn for the length of a swing of its own (`moves.showEdgeForMove`), shown at once after a swap, and its montage is read whether the game calls it armed or not. Put right on top: with no swing going the wanted state was nil, not false, the game's `SetArmed` was handed nothing each time the figure was dressed, and a weapon put away stayed drawn (four.lua, "put away"). The sim's fake takes four arguments as the game does.
423. (Pull request 20.) A figure's finishing move left its effects on this player's own screen: its particle and post-process parts are turned off 0.05, 0.25 and 0.6 s into a finishing move it plays (moves.lua, co-op only).
424. (Pull request 20.) The partner's figure: steered and turned by the length of the frame, the same at any frame rate; not set gliding for one frame of falling at a step or a landing (0.3 s of it first: "walking glitchy" on the host's screen); and animated every frame however far off (`bEnableUpdateRateOptimizations` off on its meshes: it walked in jerks seen from a distance).
425. (Pull request 20.) Enemy copies: pulled and turned by the length of the frame; a copy turns through a move as the host's enemy does (it kept the way it faced as the move began and lunged off the wrong way); a boss that holds this player in a grab is not pulled out of it; a boss attached to something is left where that puts it; a boss's copy snaps at 12 m, not 10. Merged with 410 to 412: a rider is still its mount's to place, a jump is still set where the host's line has it. A copy the game calls dead is let go of at once.
426. (Pull request 20.) A finishing move (E) with two players. The one whose player starts it tells the other (`EX`: the enemy, where it stands, its half of the move and how far in); the other game takes its own E off that enemy (not a boss: its execute and break effects removed, its posture lifted, the game's Executing state for 3 s, pbz.lua's `game.execLock`), sets it there and plays the enemy's half, and drops its own player's hits on it meanwhile; both pressing within the ping, the host's stands. Told by the player's montage (`net.isExecName`: the paired moves, BEx, GEx, BDEx, Execution) or the enemy's Executing state; a simple execution ("SEx") is neither, and needs none: 417's fall shows. Not taken: the kill put off till the move's end (`net.deferredK`, 1.8 s or the move's last word). With the host's line of the copy gone at the kill's word, the copy lost its floor through that wait, the move's next blow ended it by the game's own death, and both players were paid for it twice (the bench, run268: one elite, 327 shared to the guest and 321 back to the host). 417's show waits a move out instead, the copy held and its floor kept, and ends it at no pay (`puppets.deathShow`'s third argument: with a move flagged and no hold of the game's left on the copy, no fall of its kind is shown after). In the game, the fixed build (run272 to 274): a simple execution by each player twice, a paired one by the guest on two elites and by the host on one with the guest watching: the other screen's copy played the enemy's half (`AM_MaskAssassin_BEd_Tang_M_01`, Executing, its health following) and died at the kill, "its execution is shown here first", the dust shared once each way (334, 339, 325; 6 back once), no error line.
427. (Pull request 20.) What breaks on an enemy in one world breaks in the other: a boss parried or its posture broken (`PARRY`, its hit state for 1.2 s; never its guarding, and none of its effects stripped: `game.PARRY_STRIPS_EFFECTS` is off), an enemy's shield (`SHLD`, told by its montage's name; its shield parts hidden, the Break state), and a shield or protection named in `game.MIRROR_EFFECTS` that leaves the enemy in the game that runs it is taken off the other's copy (`GEOFF`, looked at twice a second, twelve enemies a look).
428. (Pull request 20.) A boss's doubles and summons made as its fight runs (classes named StuntDouble or For<Boss>) were in the host's world only: told with their class (`DYN`), made on the guest 1 to 2 s on if its own boss hasn't made them by then, counted by class against the host's, and those over the host's count or 55 m off taken away again (`game.spawnByClass`: BP_Monster classes only, the path checked). Only a "tw<n>:" name is a ghost twin now (`game.isBoss`, `game.bossTwin`): a copy made this way goes by the host's name.
429. (Pull request 20.) A player's summoned allies (the Ctrl powers) were taken for enemies and shared as such, or pruned a second after they came. They are no enemy now (`game.isPlayerSummon`), and are shown on the partner's PC as a picture only, where they stand and what they play (`PSUM`, `PSUMP` sixteen times a second, `PSUMEND`; no collision, no attack, on the player's side, no lock-on). Added: twelve of a partner's at most, four made a second.
430. (Pull request 20.) An enemy dead at a partner's word that stood there. After the kill, a body with no death or finishing montage on it is let fall (`game.collapseStandingDead`: 0.2 s on, a boss 0.45 s and not in a scene), the host's own view of a partner's kill too, open since 417; a copy whose fall 417 shows is left to it. The quiet kill's dissolve hides the body at 0.6 s, not 1.6. Not taken: a guest's copies of enemies left with no floor, to die by the guest's own blow. Every kill and its pay would have gone by the way a far-off kill goes (GK), which 1.7.5 does not do and the bench has not seen; a copy still dies at the host's word (four.lua's 20 checks of that way stand as they were).
431. (Pull request 20.) The host down in a fight. Its body is passed through by others and off the enemies' lists once its partner's figure is on them, so a boss no longer strikes at the body (`game.setDownBody`); a guest's own enemies drop a figure that is hidden (`net.guestOwnFight`); and an enemy handed to the guest's game with its AI stopped is started at once, not 7 s on. In, and off: `handover.HOST_DOWN_GIVES`, a boss given to the guest's game while the host is down. With it on, a boss that falls then reaches the host as the guest's own kill, and what the host is owed for such a fall (334, 383, 388) is proven only for a fall its own game tells: four.lua's "a boss, then a death that rolls nothing 0.3 s on, the host down" paid nobody.
432. (Pull request 20.) Boss bars. A dead boss's bar, a second bar on one boss and a bar with nobody behind it are taken down (`game.cleanBossBars`); a bar the HUD hands back is taken for the boss it is shown for (`game.shownBarWidget`); one registered again within 0.8 s of coming down is not told as a new fight; the game's own bar on a boss is never torn down by the mirror's keeper; and a boss whose body is swapped mid-fight is not scaled a second time (`game.bossMaxCap`). Kept from 1.6.9: the keeper still puts the bar on a copy a pairing swapped in (four.lua, reusedBars).
433. (Pull request 20, the rest.) Missing enemies are brought back sooner (0.6 s unpaired, six a round); a pooled body at the world's origin is no enemy; an ability's class is loaded if this PC hasn't it, and a running one cancelled to start it again; 120 new montages may be loaded a minute; places are taken to 1e8 cm; a hit's sound and blood go only with health taken; a copy this player struck in the last 4 s, the host 35 m off, is not lifted back to the host's health; an enemy the host held for a guest's game that no longer runs it is let go; a partner's flyover of a level (`LD_...`) plays here too, as a picture; no partner's scene is joined over a pickup card or within 8 s of a memory flower; F3 beside the fight (60 m) goes in without the trip; F7's mark names the nearest enemy's class, montage and effects. Not taken from it, 1.7.5's kept: its horse names (410's pairing by the rider stays: with a name of its own a 1.7.5 partner would put the horse away again, and merged blindly the name's test threw on every enemy with no spawner id); its closer of cards with no button (418 took the closer out for cause); a partner's bell shared only within 4.5 m and with no set-down (the 30 m and the set-down are the rule of 11-18); the shop and upgrade unlocks left out of a story catch-up, and the two commands made to look into them; a hit's enemy looked for past the round's list for every hit, not the killing blow alone (in a party a boss asleep on the host took both guests' blows there and died: sitBosses); and F3 refused within 60 m (a joiner held watching stayed held, and 419's second player could not join: now in, without the trip, a held watcher by the trip as before). The sims on the merged build: four 2729 (shots, the Phantom Edge and boss armour parts changed as said; boss armour's blow now waits for its command to be read, which fell a frame late one run in two), pvp 162, chaos 19; a 1.7.5 player with a 1.7.6 player, each way round, links and shares hits, kills and a boss fight with no error. `two` differs by design: the fourth argument, the copies' steering, a figure's hatred, a bar's flicker not logged. In the game besides 426's runs (run268 to 276): the host's kill and the guest's on both screens with 417's fall; Jing Ye started by the host, joined by F3, struck by both (20 of the guest's blows applied in 35 s), one boss in each world, a horse of that map 0.9 m from the host's on the guest's; no error line in any run. The mod's own time at rest, two copies on one PC, 1.7.5 against the merge: the host 98 against 108 ms a second (the new looks: summons 5%, finishing moves 3%), the guest 140 against 139, 60 frames a second in both. Not seen in the game: 420 to 425's looks, 427 to 429, 432's bars beyond one fight.
434. Other mods, the first piece (players on 1.7.6: "Can it show other players the mods it has installed? My friend and I have tried it, and from his perspective, my mod is not displaying", and three more asking for a "mod merge"). A mod changes one PC's files, and a partner's game draws this player from its own files: what a mod changes shows there only if that PC has the mod too. Nothing of a mod is sent, and nothing will be: the files are their authors', and a game shouldn't load what a partner hands it. New is that each PC says how it stands (`mods.lua`). It lists its own mods once a session: the pak mods under `Content/Paks` (a `.pak` with its `.utoc` and `.ucas` is one mod; not the game's own files, told by their platform ending, since real mods are named `ProjectPBZ-..._997_P` too) the UE4SS mods switched on (`mods.txt`, `mods.json`, an `enabled.txt`; not UE4SS's own, not this one), and the JavaScript mods of the game's two script loaders (PBZ Mods Menu's, under `Content/JavaScript/PBZModsMenu/Mods`, when its `hid.dll` lies beside the game's exe; PBZModLoader's, under `ProjectPBZ/PBZModLoader/mods`, but those its `mods.ini` turns off; found on the bench, whose game had the Mods Menu and Phantom Master Ninja running and a first list had neither), through main.dll's new `pp_files` (Lua can't list a folder; names and sizes only; a path that isn't UTF-8 is read as the PC's own code page, for a game folder with such letters). It sends a mark for each (`MODS`: two hashes, of the name and of the name with the size, forty an event; a version before leaves the word) and compares what comes back. The player is told once per difference, by their own mods' names alone: which of them the partner doesn't have, how many the partner has more, which are another version there (by size). A partner's names are never shown or logged, only counted. More than two players: one line for all, each comparison in the log by the player's number. What was told is kept in `mods_told.txt`, so the same difference isn't told at every link; `mods` says how it stands, `ModsCheck = Off` turns all of it off. Chinese rows are in; the Arabic rows are in the tsv unchecked, so those four tips stay English in Arabic till the author has checked them (`make_lang_ar.py` now leaves out an unchecked whole-line pattern as it does an unchecked whole line). Six new lists a minute are taken from one player, no more (each is a comparison, a log line, a tip). Checked: `otherMods` (50 checks: no mods, differing ones, a drop and back, the next session, the setting off, an older main.dll, 45 mods in two events, a flood of lists, parties of four and of two, JavaScript mods with and without their loaders), the full suites on the 1.7.7 build (four 2,779, pvp 162, chaos 19), and a 1.7.6 player with one on this build each way round (the old side leaves the word, the new side says nothing); main.dll's folder list in its own tests (`relay_cli test`, nine checks; `relay_cli files <folder>` prints one), and `mods.lua`'s list read from the test copy's real folders through it, by a full path and by one from where the game runs, two made-up mods found and named. In the running game (the bench, run277, 11-24, `notes/stable/bench/modscheck.ps1`; a first try that evening stopped at the game's own "make sure Steam is not in Offline Mode"): both copies read their folders through `pp_files` and listed the run's two made-up mods (a lone `.ucas` under `Paks\~mods`, a UE4SS mod that does nothing), each compared the other's marks as the same and said nothing; a third made up on the guest alone was compared on both at once and told 8 s on, each tip on its own screen ("your partner doesn't have these mods of yours: BenchOnly, BenchTwo. ..." on the guest, "your partner has mods you don't have (2). ..." on the host), `mods_told.txt` written by both, no error line, the tick's cost 0.2 ms over the session. (The bench's pictures were the window's top-left two thirds till `Shot` made its own process DPI-aware: the tip line is at the bottom.) And with real mods (run278, `modsreal.ps1`): a real pak mod kept on the bench (Phantom Boss Rematch 1.1.1, its three files copied into `Paks\~mods` for the run) beside the bench's own Mods Menu and Phantom Master Ninja. Both copies listed the three by name ("3 (1 pak, 0 UE4SS, 2 JavaScript): PhantomBossRematch, PBZModsMenu, PhantomMasterNinja") and compared as the same; with two of them taken off the guest's own list by hand (the bench's copies share one game folder), the host was told on its screen "your partner doesn't have these mods of yours: PhantomBossRematch, PhantomMasterNinja. ..." and the guest "your partner has mods you don't have (2). ..."; no error line, 59 and 60 frames a second. Released as 1.7.7. Not done: a mod's version is told by its files' sizes, not their contents; a link's size, where a mod manager puts mods in by links, couldn't be made on the test account; and nothing more is shown of a mod both players have than the game itself draws (the next piece).

The story from start to end on two real copies (the bench, the night of 11-16, the combined build). The host's world was jumped through the table boss by boss (`storyjump`, 26 stages to its end), and the guest's story point and done-set read 30 s and 60 s after each: the host's own, all 26 times, at 30 s already. A survey of the 40 boss spawners of the nine maps in that end-of-story world, both copies flown to each: 14 bosses out, the same health on both copies wherever both had one; the four spots where one copy had none at the first look agreed within seconds. Real fights with the bosses that fight when approached (Lei Dang's second form, Xiong Zhang, Bi Yuan, Jing Ye, Gu He): the same health on both copies throughout, both players' blows counted, the boss's blows landing on the guest (Bi Yuan: 11 on a guest fighting alone), a guest's killing blow ending it on both with the dust to both (Lei Dang 20000 each, Bi Yuan 15000 each). Where the game holds its boss up for a finisher or a second wind of its own (Peng Meng, Jing Ye) the guest's blow isn't taken as the kill, as designed. The bosses of a story scene stand unstarted till that scene (AI None; no QA command starts one): one was started the game's way (326). A census of the game's switches on two maps and a half (the second review's G4): 220 shared ones both copies had loaded, none open in one world and shut in the other. The kinds the mod shares: doors (`BP_SingleDoor_Poverty`, `BP_DoubleDoor_XiaoKang`, `BP_DoubleDoor_G`, `BP_Block_Gate`, `BP_Block_Door`, `BP_SlenderDoor`) and `BP_Wharf`, `BP_StingerTrigger`, `BP_SwitchDevice`, `BP_NarrativeCage`, `BP_Lift` and its kinds, `BP_BrokenBell`, `BP_MoveableStuff_Blocker`. It ended with 325's crash. The morning of 11-17, on 8a57314 and then the combined build (`claude/stable-coop-trial` 71cc45f, 5615e0a). The switches of the seven other maps, on both copies: 425 shared ones both had loaded, one of them different between the worlds: a door the probe itself had opened in the host's read shut there a minute on and open in the guest's (open in both after the next load; not seen again in three tries at it: far off and back, both players' deaths, a reload). One opening of each door kind in the host's world, by the call the word makes: open on the guest within 6 s each time (block gates, plain and snow; block doors; slender, single and double doors; a dry trunk). The kinds that call doesn't change in either world need a real use: lifts, cages and their devices, wharves, moveable blockers, the triggers, and Xigu's key device, which wants a key the bench save hasn't got. A door opened by word stayed open through its player's death and respawn, the partner's, 2 km away and back, and a map's reload. Story bosses with the guest arriving late, by F3 after the host's scene and fight had begun, the hits counted both ways and the health the same on both copies each time: Peng Meng and Zhui Ming (327), ChiLian (7 of the guest's blows applied, 13 taken), Wu Lengshuang (13, 17), and with 343 A-Hua (20, 9; killed by the guest's blow, 9000 dust to each), Chang Hong (16, 12) and the sword puppet of the main camp (27, 4). Yan Qiansui took no health from plain swings, the host's own neither, and struck the late guest 5 times. Not reached by the bench's tools: the small Lion Dance, Wan Jun and Lei Dang's first form, whose fights begin further on in their levels after a scene. And in the game: 340 (the stuck row left alone through two loads, nothing said of it), 342 (1.7.3's main.dll under the new scripts: no link on either copy, the reason on screen), 344 (Wan Jun's bar blinked as the host flew up: the guest's bar, walls and the figure's side back as they were 5 s on). Late morning, on 94aa911: 345 and 349 as told in their entries. Three copies: the watcher 42 m from the elite, a stray mirror bar on its copy, never had the fight through six rounds of the fighter stepping in, striking and falling (the fight begun again at full health each time), and the fighter's kill paid 6600 dust to all three; the watcher 25 m off is in the fight by the game's own doing (its own bar), the fighter's respawn held while that lasts, and the kill paid all three. G3, G4 and G8 as in 348. Yan Qiansui as before; Wu Lengshuang's fight didn't start this time (the story put there in one jump). The bench was given back four times with its saves and mod as they were (51 and 688 files, none different). The figure sweep of 343's entry, on 0d83f4e: an hour on two copies through all 26 stages and eight maps, trips and loads between them, no error line; the bench given back as it was. Eight more bosses with the guest joining a fight the host had begun without it, by F3 (11561b0, the afternoon of 11-17): the ones that start as a player comes up. Jing Ye, Xiao Xue (86 of the host's items held for the guest's own scene, which came), the Emei swordswoman, Bi Yuan, Feng Xue Dao Ke (the guest 1.5 km off at the bell; 5 held, its own scene), the Elder Brother with his soldiers, Lei Dang's second form and the bone-blade puppet: at each the host applied the guest's blows (10 to 46 in half a minute), the boss's health was the same on both copies after them, one figure of it stood in the guest's world, and no error line came. Fifteen bosses have been fought so, and Yan Qiansui, who takes no health from plain swings of either player; the story's others can't be started by the bench (the game's own test commands have nothing for a quest step or a boss), and wait for real play. And 279 in the game, a guest down as a story boss falls (A-Hua, the same afternoon): in her fight with 7 blows landed, the guest died and its respawn was held; the host killed her with real blows; 2.7 s after her fall the guest was on its feet at its bell, with her 9000 dust once, as the host had, the win's flags and flow steps taken, the fog walls open, the same story point on both, no error line. Wan Jun, the small Lion Dance and Lei Dang's first form stay unreached: at Wan Jun's stage no quest step of the jumped story points at him, the box by him plays a scene after which he is gone, and the level goes on to the sword puppet, whose health lock neither real blows nor the game's own kill-nearby command undo on the bench. Two shipped features this round hadn't touched in the game, on the same build: a PvP match where the two stood (lobby, start, the count, both at the match's 1360 health, the guest down twice, the finish, the result, both home in co-op at 850, linked); and a chest: three thugs by it fought by the guest with real blows (each one's health the same on both copies after every exchange, their deaths and dust on both), then the chest opened with the real E key: told, the host's copy of it open 85 m off, the same loot in both bags, shown on the host as the partner's finds. No error line. Xiao Xue's numbers (the same run, and run100 on the build before): with co-op off, a blow stuck till a rise (16402 back to 16482 twice in 20 s, the host alone): once the difficulty set back at co-op's end (17:23:26; a difficulty set writes every enemy's health to its base), once her heal at her grab (17:23:29). With co-op's 150% her game cut her to 16482 (378's rule) and the scaling raised her again, "1 enemy at 150% health" at each: 369 and 378. The drops to 16482 with her most kept at 24723, seen only while the guest swung, were the mod's: the guest's own game wrote its copy back to base, and the drop went to the host as the guest's blow, 8241 at once, so she stood under the game's own health (15922, 15762). Read here at first as the game's alone; the sixth review's V3, fixed in 364. The eight bosses that start as a player comes up, with a late guest again, on the final build (the night of 11-17, run150, the combined build with 370): Jing Ye, Xiao Xue, the Emei swordswoman, Bi Yuan, Feng Xue Dao Ke, the Elder Brother, Lei Dang's second form and the bone-blade puppet: at each the host applied the guest's blows (5 to 35 in half a minute), the health the same on both copies after them, no error line in the half hour, no read caught; and no boss but Xiao Xue had its numbers written back by the game. And a PvP match on that build with 371 in (run158): the lobby, the start, both at the match's 1360 health, the guest down twice (1-0, 2-0), the finish, the result, both home in co-op at 850, linked; no error line.
